## XVI Bradshaw / 十六　布拉德肖

===Original===
A month later Gilbert Standerton came back from the Foreign Office to his little house in St. John's Wood.

"There is a man to see you, Gilbert," said his wife.

"I think I know, it is my bank manager," he said.

He greeted the tall man who rose to meet him with a cheery smile.

===Chinese===
一个月后，吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿（Gilbert Standerton）从外交部回到他在圣约翰林的小住宅。

「吉尔伯特，有位先生要见你。」他的妻子说。

「我想我猜得到，是我的银行经理。」他说。

他笑吟吟地招呼那位起身相迎的高个子男人。

===Original===
"Now, Mr. Brown," he said, "I have to explain to you exactly what I want done. There is a man in America, he has been there some week or two, to whom I owe a large sum of money—eighty thousand pounds, to be exact—and I want you to see that I have sufficient fluent capital to pay it."

"You have quite sufficient, Mr. Standerton," said the manager, "even now, without selling any of your securities."

"That is good. You will have all the particulars here," said Gilbert, and took a folded sheet of paper from his pocket. "It is really a trust, in the sense that it is to be transferred to two men, Thomas Black and George Smith. They may subdivide it again, because I believe," he smiled, "they have other business associates who happen to be entitled to share."

===Chinese===
「好了，布朗先生，」他说，「我得跟你把要办的事情交代清楚。有个人在美国——他过去一两个星期了——我欠着他一大笔钱，确切地说是八万英镑，我要你保证我有足够的流动资金来支付这笔款子。」

「您的资金绰绰有余，斯坦德顿先生，」经理说，「就是眼下这样，一股证券不卖也够了。」

「那就好。所有的细节都在这儿。」吉尔伯特说着，从口袋里掏出一张折起的纸。「严格说来这是一笔信托，因为这钱要转交给两个人——托马斯·布莱克（Thomas Black）和乔治·史密斯（George Smith）。他们没准还会再分下去，因为据我所知，」他莞尔一笑，「他们另有一些生意伙伴，恰好也有资格分一份。」

===Original===
"I did not congratulate you, Mr. Standerton," said the bank manager, "upon the marvellous service you rendered the city. They say that through you every penny which was stolen by the famous Wallis gang has been recovered."

"I think that pretty well described the position," said Gilbert quietly.

"I was reading an account of it in a paper the other day," the bank manager went on. "It was very providential that there was an alarm of fire next door to their headquarters."

"It was providential that it was found before the fire reached the Safe Company's premises," said Gilbert. "Fortunately the firemen saw me through the skylight. That made things rather easy, but it was some time before they got me out, as you probably know."

===Chinese===
「我还没向您道贺呢，斯坦德顿先生，」银行经理说，「恭喜您为这座城市立下的那桩奇功。他们说，大名鼎鼎的沃利斯（Wallis）匪帮偷走的每一个便士，都是靠您才追了回来。」

「我想这话说得八九不离十。」吉尔伯特淡淡地说。

「前几天我还在报纸上读到一段始末记，」银行经理接着说，「他们总部隔壁恰好响起了火警，真是天意。」

「那份东西赶在火势烧到保险柜公司的房产之前被找到，才是天意。」吉尔伯特说，「幸亏消防队员从天窗里瞧见了我。这下事情好办多了，不过他们费了好一阵子才把我弄出来，这您大概知道。」

===Original===
"Did you ever see this man Wallis?" asked the bank manager curiously.

"Didn't the papers tell you that?" bantered Gilbert with a dry smile.

"They say you learnt in some way that there was to be a burglary at your uncle's, and that you went up to his place, and there you saw Mr. Wallis under the very window of the library, on the parapet or something."

"On the terrace it was," said Gilbert quietly.

"And that he flew at the sight of you?"

"That is hardly true," said Gilbert, "rather put it that I persuaded him to go. I was not sure that he had not already secured the necklace, and I went through the window into the room without realising there was anybody there. You see, there were heavy curtains which hid the light. Whilst I was there he escaped, that is all."

===Chinese===
「你见过沃利斯这个人吗？」银行经理好奇地问。

「报上没告诉你吗？」吉尔伯特带着干巴巴的笑打趣道。

「他们说，你不知怎么打听到你叔叔家要遭贼，就赶去了他那儿，就在书房窗户底下、在女儿墙还是什么的地方，撞见了沃利斯先生。」

「其实是在露台上。」吉尔伯特平静地说。

「他一见到你就飞也似的逃了？」

「那可不太确实，」吉尔伯特说，「不如说，是我劝他走的。我当时拿不准他是不是已经把项链弄到了手，就径直翻窗进了屋，没察觉里面还有人。你是知道的，厚厚的窗帘把灯光全遮住了。我在屋里那会儿，他逃掉了，就是这么回事。」

===Original===
He made one or two suggestions regarding the transfer of the money and showed the bank manager out, then he joined Edith in the drawing-room.

She came to him with a little smile.

"Does the Foreign Office seem very strange to you?" she asked.

"It did seem rather strange after my other exploits."

He laughed.

"I never thought Sir John had sufficient influence to get you back."

"I think he has greater influence than you imagine," he said; "but then there were other considerations. You see, I was able to render the Foreign Office one or two little acts of service in the course of my nefarious career, and they have been very good."

===Chinese===
他就款项的转账又提了一两条建议，送走银行经理，随后便到客厅去陪伊迪丝（Edith）。

她带着浅浅的笑容迎上前来。

「外交部在你看来是不是很陌生？」她问。

「在我那些别的冒险经历之后，起初确实觉得怪别扭的。」

他笑了。

「我原先可没想到约翰爵士（Sir John）有那样的影响力，能把你弄回去。」

「我看他的影响力比你想象的还要大，」他说，「不过话说回来，也有别的考量。你要知道，在我那趟『无法无天的营生』里，我顺带也替外交部办过一两件小事，他们待我不薄。」

===Original===
She looked at him wistfully.

"And do we go back now to where we started?" she asked.

"Where did we start?" he countered.

"I do not know that we started anywhere," she said thoughtfully.

She had been looking at a time table when he came into the room, and now she picked it up and turned the pages idly.

"Are you interested in that Bradshaw?"

"Very," she said. "I am just deciding."

"Deciding what?" he asked.

"Where—where we shall spend our honeymoon," she faltered.

===Chinese===
她怅然若失地望着他。

「那我们现在要回到最初开始的地方去吗？」她问。

「我们是从哪儿开始的？」他反问道。

「我说不上我们是从什么地方开始的。」她若有所思地说。

他进屋时她一直在翻一本时刻表，这时她又把它拿起来，懒懒地翻动着书页。

「你对那本布拉德肖（Bradshaw）有兴趣？」

「很有兴趣，」她说，「我正在做决定。」

「决定什么？」他问。

「决定——决定我们去哪儿度蜜月。」她嗫嚅道。

## IX Edith Meets the Player / 九　伊迪丝邂逅琴师

===Original===
Edith turned to her waiting maid.

"Go out and bring the girl in at once," she said quietly.

"Which girl, madam?" asked the startled servant.

"The girl who is playing," said Edith. "Hurry please, before she goes."

===Chinese===
伊迪丝转向候在一旁的女仆。

「出去，马上把那姑娘请进来。」她轻声说。

「哪位姑娘，夫人？」女仆吃了一惊，问道。

「正在演奏的那位姑娘。」伊迪丝说，「劳驾快些，趁她还没走。」

===Original===
She was filled with sudden determination to unravel this mystery. She might be acting disloyally to her husband, but she adjusted any fear she may have had on the score with the thought that she might also be helping him. The maid returned in a few minutes and ushered in a girl.

Yes, it was the girl she had seen on her wedding night. She stood now, framed in the doorway, watching her hostess with frank curiosity.

"Won't you come in?" said Edith. "Have you had any dinner?"

"Thank you very much," said the girl, "we do not take dinner, but I had a very good tea."

===Chinese===
她心头陡然生出一股决心，非要解开这个谜不可。这样做或许是对丈夫不忠，可她又暗自宽解：这说不定也是在帮他的忙，如此便把这一层顾虑压了下去。几分钟后，女仆回来，领进来一位姑娘。

不错，正是她新婚之夜见过的那个姑娘。此刻她立在门框中间，坦坦然然地打量着女主人，眼神里满是好奇。

「快请进吧。」伊迪丝说，「用过晚饭了吗？」

「多谢您，」姑娘说，「我们是不吃晚饭的，不过下午茶吃得很好。」

===Original===
"Will you sit down for a little while?"

With a graceful inclination of her head the girl accepted the invitation.

Her voice was free from the foreign accent which Edith had expected. She was indubitably English, and there was a refinement in her tone which Edith had not expected to meet.

===Chinese===
「请坐下稍待片刻，好吗？」

姑娘优雅地颔首，应了这番邀请。

她的口音不带一丝伊迪丝预想中的外国腔。她无疑是英国人，而且谈吐之间自有一种雅致，倒是伊迪丝始料未及的。

===Original===
"I suppose you wonder why I have sent for you?" asked Edith Standerton.

The girl showed two rows of white, even teeth in a smile.

"When people send for me," she said demurely, "it is either to pay me for my music, or to bribe me to desist!"

There was frank merriment in her eyes, her smile lit up the face and changed its whole aspect.

===Chinese===
「我想你一定纳闷，我为什么叫你来吧？」伊迪丝·斯坦德顿问道。

姑娘嫣然一笑，露出一口整整齐齐的白牙。

「人家把我叫去，」她端庄地说，「不是付我琴钱，就是塞钱求我别再拉了！」

她眼里透着毫不掩饰的顽皮笑意，一笑之下，整张脸都亮了，神情也随之一变。

===Original===
"I am doing both," said Edith, "and I also want to ask you something. Do you know my husband?"

"Mr. Standerton," said the girl, and nodded. "Yes, I have seen him, and I have played to him."

"Do you remember a night in June," asked Edith, her heart beating faster at the memory, "when you came under this window and played"—she hesitated—"a certain tune?"

The girl nodded.

"Why, yes," she said in surprise, "of course I remember that night of all nights."

"Why of all nights?" asked Edith quickly.

===Chinese===
「这两样我都要做，」伊迪丝说，「另外我还想问你点事。你认识我丈夫吗？」

「斯坦德顿先生。」姑娘说着点了点头。「是的，我见过他，还给他演奏过。」

「你还记得六月里的一个晚上吗？」伊迪丝问，想起那晚，她的心跳得快了起来，「那天你来到这扇窗下，演奏了」——她迟疑了一下——「一支曲子？」

姑娘点点头。

「咦，记得，」她惊讶地说，「别的晚上兴许会忘，那一晚当然忘不了。」

「为什么别的晚上会忘，偏偏那一晚忘不了？」伊迪丝急忙问。

===Original===
"Well, you see as a rule my grandfather plays for Mr. Standerton, and that night he was ill. He caught a bad chill on Derby Day—we were wet through by the storm, for we were playing at Epsom—and I had to come here and deputise for him. I did not want to go out a bit that night," she confessed with a bitter laugh, "and I hate the tune; but it was all so mysterious and so romantic."

"Just tell me what was 'mysterious' and what was 'romantic,' " said Edith.

The coffee came in at that moment, and she poured a cup for her visitor.

"What is your name?" she asked.

"May Wing," said the girl.

===Chinese===
「唉，是这样的，平时给斯坦德顿先生演奏的是我祖父，那晚他病了。他在德比大赛日着了凉，病得不轻——我们叫暴雨浇了个透心凉，因为当时正在埃普索姆演奏——我只好上这儿来替他跑一趟。那天晚上我一点儿也不想出门，」她苦笑着坦白道，「何况我讨厌那支曲子；可这一切又是那么神秘、那么罗曼蒂克。」

「那就跟我说说，什么叫做『神秘』，什么叫做『罗曼蒂克』。」伊迪丝说。

这时咖啡端了进来，她给客人斟了一杯。

「你叫什么名字？」她问。

「梅·温（May Wing）。」姑娘说。

===Original===
"Now tell me, May, all you know," said Edith, as she passed the coffee, "and please believe it is not out of curiosity that I ask you."

"I will tell you everything," said the girl, nodding "I remember that day particularly because I had been to the Academy of Music to take my lesson—you would not think we could afford that, but granny absolutely insists upon it. I got back home rather tired. Grandfather was lying down on the couch. We live at Hoxton. He seemed a little troubled. 'May,' he said, 'I want you to do something for me tonight.' Of course, I was quite willing and happy to do it."

===Chinese===
「梅，现在把你所知道的都告诉我吧。」伊迪丝一面把咖啡递过去，一面说，「也请你相信，我这么问并非出于好奇。」

「我什么都告诉您。」姑娘点点头说。「那天的事我记得特别清楚，因为我刚去音乐学院上完课——您准以为我们上不起，可奶奶非要我上不可。回到家我累得够呛。祖父正躺在长椅上歇着。我们住在霍克斯顿。他看上去有点心事重重的样子。『梅，』他说，『今晚我要你替我做件事。』不用说，我是千肯万肯，乐意得很。」

===Original===
The girl stopped suddenly.

"Why, how extraordinary," she said, "I believe I have got proof in my pocket of all that I say."

She had hanging from her waist a little bag of the same material as her dress, and this she opened and searched inside.

She brought out an envelope.

===Chinese===
姑娘忽然停住了口。

「咦，真稀奇，」她说，「我想我说的一切，口袋里就揣着凭据呢。」

她腰间挂着一只小提包，料子同她的连衣裙一样，她打开包，在里面翻找。

她掏出一只信封。

===Original===
"I will not show you this yet," she said, "but I will tell you what happened. Grandfather, as I was saying, was very troubled, and he asked me if I would do something for him, knowing of course that I would.

" 'I have had a letter which I cannot make head or tail of,' he said, and he showed me this letter."

The girl held out the envelope.

Edith took it and removed the card inside.

"Why, this is my husband's writing!" she cried.

"Yes," nodded the girl.

===Chinese===
「这东西我暂时还不能给您看，」她说，「不过我可以把后来的事讲给您听。我方才说了，祖父心里烦恼得很，他问我肯不肯替他办件事——自然料定我必肯的。」

「『我收到一封信，怎么也看不明白了，』他说，接着便把这封信拿给我看。」

姑娘把信封递了过来。

伊迪丝接过来，抽出里面那张卡片。

「哎呀，这是我丈夫的笔迹！」她失声叫道。

「是的。」姑娘点头道。

===Original===
It bore the postmark of Doncaster, and the letter was brief. It was addressed to the old musician, and ran:—

> "Enclosed you will find a postal order for one pound. On receipt of this go to the house of Mr. Standerton between the hours of half-past seven and eight o'clock and play Rubenstein's 'Melody in F.' Ascertain if he is at home, and if he is not return the next night and play the same tune at the same hour."

That was all.

===Chinese===
信封上盖着唐卡斯特的邮戳，信写得很短，是写给老乐师的，内容如下：——

> 「随函附上一英镑邮政汇票一张。接信后，请于七点半至八点之间前往斯坦德顿先生宅邸，演奏鲁宾斯坦的《F大调旋律》。查明他是否在家；若不在，请于次日晚间同一时刻再来，演奏同一支曲子。」

仅此而已。

===Original===
"I cannot understand it," said Edith, puzzled. "What does it mean?"

The girl musician smiled.

"I should like to know what it meant too. You see, I am as curious as you, and think it is a failing which all women share."

"And you do not know why this was sent?"

"No."

===Chinese===
「我弄不明白了。」伊迪丝困惑地说，「这是什么意思呢？」

姑娘乐师微微一笑。

「我也很想知道它是什么意思。您瞧，我跟您一样好奇，我想这是天下女人共有的毛病。」

「你也不知道为什么寄这封信？」

「不知道。」

===Original===
"Or what is its meaning?"

Again the girl shook her head.

Edith looked at the envelope and examined the postmark.

It was dated May the twenty-fourth.

"May the twenty-fourth," she repeated to herself. "Just wait one moment," she said, and ran upstairs to her bedroom.

===Chinese===
「也不知道它究竟是什么意思？」

姑娘又摇了摇头。

伊迪丝端详着信封，细看那邮戳。

邮戳日期是五月二十四日。

「五月二十四日。」她喃喃自语。「请稍等一下。」说着，她跑上楼进了自己的卧室。

===Original===
Feverishly she unlocked her bureau and took out the red-covered diary in which she had inscribed the little events of her life in Portland Square. She turned to May the twenty-fourth. There were only two entries. The first had to do with the arrival of a new dress but the second was very emphatic:—

> "G. S. came at seven o'clock and stayed to dinner. Was very absentminded and worried apparently. He left at ten. Had a depressing evening."

She looked at the envelope again.

===Chinese===
她急切地打开写字台的小柜，取出那本红皮日记——她一直把在波特兰广场生活的点滴琐事记在里面。她翻到五月二十四日那一页。上面只有两条记录。第一条记的是新到了一件衣裳，第二条却写得斩钉截铁：——

> 「吉·斯七点来，留下吃了晚饭。一副心不在焉的样子，显然有心事。十点走的。这个晚上过得闷得很。」

她又看了看那只信封。

===Original===
"Doncaster, 7:30," it said.

So the letter had been posted a hundred and eighty miles away half an hour after he had arrived in Portland Square.

She went back to the dining-room bewildered, but she controlled her agitation in the presence of the girl.

"I must really patronise one of the arts," she smiled.

She took a half-sovereign from her purse and handed it to May.

"Oh, really," protested the little musician.

===Chinese===
上面是「唐卡斯特，七点半」。

也就是说，这封信是在他到达波特兰广场半小时之后，从一百八十英里外寄出的。

她满腹惶惑地回到饭厅，但在姑娘面前强自按捺住了激动。

「看来我真该赞助赞助一门艺术才是。」她微笑道。

她从钱包里取出一枚半镑金币，递给梅。

「哎，这怎么行。」小乐师推辞道。

===Original===
"No, take it, please. You have given me a great deal to think about. Has Mr. Standerton ever referred to this incident since?"

"Never," said the girl. "I have never seen him since except once when I was on the top of an omnibus."

A few minutes later the girl left.

Here was food for imagination, sufficient to occupy her mind, thought Edith.

"What did it mean?" she asked, "what mystery was behind all this?"

===Chinese===
「不，请收下。你让我多了一肚子要琢磨的事。这以后斯坦德顿先生提起过这件事吗？」

「从没有过，」姑娘说，「后来我再没见过他，只有一回，我坐在公共马车顶层的时候瞧见过他。」

几分钟后，姑娘告辞走了。

这可够人浮想联翩的了，够她好好琢磨一阵子的，伊迪丝想。

「这是什么意思呢？」她自问，「这背后藏着什么谜？」

===Original===
Now that she recalled the circumstances, she remembered that Gilbert had been terribly distrait that night; he was nervous, she had noticed his hand shaking, and had remarked to her mother upon his extraordinary absentmindedness.

And if he had expected the musician to call, and if he himself had specified what tune should be played, why had its playing produced so terrible an effect upon him? He was no poseur.

There was nothing theatrical in his temperament.

He was a musician, and loved music as he loved nothing else in the world save her!

She thought of that reservation with some tenderness.

He had loved her then, whatever might be his feelings now, and the love of a strong man does not easily evaporate, nor is it destroyed at a word.

===Chinese===
如今回想起来，她记起那晚吉尔伯特失魂落魄得厉害；他很紧张，她注意到他的手直发抖，还向母亲说起过他那异乎寻常的心不在焉。

再说，倘若他早料到乐师要来，倘若曲子是他自己指定的，那么这曲子奏响时，怎会在他身上引起那么可怕的反应？他可不是个装腔作势的人。

他的性子里没有半点戏子的做作。

他是个音乐家，爱音乐之深，普天之下唯有对她的爱能与之相比！

想到这个「唯她除外」，她心头泛起几分柔情。

那时候他是爱她的，不管如今心绪如何；而一个铁铮铮男子的爱，不会轻易消散，更不会一句话就烟消云散。

===Original===
Since their marriage his piano had not been opened. He had been a subscriber to almost every musical event in London, yet he had not attended a single concert, not once visited the opera.

With the playing of the "Melody in F" it seemed to her there had ended one precious period of his life.

She had suggested once that they should go to a concert which all musical London was attending.

"Perhaps you would like to go," he had suggested briefly. "I am afraid I shall be rather busy that night." This, after he had told her not once, but a score of times that music expressed to him every message and every emotion in language clearer than the printed word.

What did it mean? She was seized with a sudden energy, a sudden desire for knowledge—she wanted to share a greater portion of his life. What connection had this melody with the sudden change that had come to him? What association had it with the adoption of this strenuous life of his lately? What had it to do with his resignation from the Foreign Office and from his clubs?

She was certain there must be some connection, and she was determined to discover what.

===Chinese===
自打结婚以来，他的钢琴就不曾开过箱。伦敦大大小小的音乐盛事，他几乎每样都订了票，可一场音乐会也没去听过，歌剧院更是连一次都没踏进过。

在她看来，随着那曲《F大调旋律》的奏响，他生命中一段宝贵的岁月便告终结了。

有一回她提议，两人去听一场音乐会——全伦敦爱乐的人倾巢而去的那一场。

「也许你愿意去，」他当时淡淡地说，「恐怕那天晚上我会相当忙。」——这可是从他嘴里说出来的，尽管他曾不止一次、而是几十次对她说过：音乐能向他传达一切讯息、一切情感，那语言比白纸黑字还要明白。

这到底是什么意思？她陡然生出一股劲头，一股求知的热望——她要更多地分担他的生活。这支旋律同他身上骤然发生的变化有什么牵连？同他近来过的这种劳苦操心的日子又有什么关联？同他辞去外交部职务、退出各家俱乐部的事又有什么干系？

她笃定这里面必有牵连，而且打定主意非查个水落石出不可。

===Original===
As she was in the dark she could not help him. She knew instinctively that to ask him would be of little use. He was of the type who preferred to play a lone hand.

She was his wife, she owed him something. She had brought unhappiness into his life, and she could do no less than strive to help him. She would want money.

She sat down and wrote a little note to her mother. She would take the three hundred pounds which were due from the broker; she even went so far as to hint that if this matter were not promptly settled by her parent she herself would see Mr. Warrell and conclude negotiations.

She had read in the morning paper the advertisement of a private detective agency, and for a while she was inclined to engage a man. But what special qualifications did private detectives have that she herself did not possess? It required no special training to use one's brains and to exercise one's logical faculties.

She had found a mission in life—the solution of this mystery which surrounded her husband like a cloud. She found herself feeling cheerful at the prospect of the work to which she had set her hand.

===Chinese===
她既然蒙在鼓里，就帮不上他的忙。她本能地知道，开口去问他不会有什么用处。他是那种宁可单枪匹马行事的人。

她是他的妻子，欠着他一份情。她已给他的生活带来了不幸，除了尽力帮他，她别无选择。她将会需要用钱。

她坐下来，给母亲写了张便条。她要取回经纪人名下应付她的那三百英镑；她甚至暗示，倘若母亲不赶紧把这件事了结，她就亲自去见沃雷尔先生，把这笔交易谈妥。

她在晨报上看到过一家私家侦探社的广告，有一阵子颇想雇个人。可是私家侦探又有什么她本人不具备的特殊本事呢？动脑筋、使逻辑，这都用不着什么特别的训练。

她给自己找到了一项使命——解开那团像乌云般笼罩着丈夫的谜。想到手头这份事业，她发觉自己的心情竟轻快起来。

===Original===
"You should find yourself an occupation," Gilbert had said in his hesitating fashion.

She smiled, and wondered exactly what he would think if he knew the occupation she had found.

---

===Chinese===
「你该给自己找点事做。」吉尔伯特当时吞吞吐吐地说。

她莞尔一笑，暗想倘若他知道她找的是这么一份「事做」，不知会作何感想。

---

===Original===
The little house in Hoxton which sheltered May and her grandfather was in a respectable little street in the main inhabited by the members of the artisan class. Small and humble as the dwelling was it was furnished in perfect taste. The furniture was old in the more valuable and more attractive sense of the word.

Old man Wing propped up in his armchair sat by a small fire in the room which served as kitchen and dining-room. May was busy with her sewing.

"My dear," said the old man in his gentle voice. "I do not think you had better go out again tonight."

===Chinese===
梅和祖父栖身的那座霍克斯顿小屋，坐落在一条体面的小街上，街上住的多半是工匠人家。屋子虽小虽简朴，陈设却雅致极了。家具是那种年头越久越金贵、越耐看的旧式家具。

温老先生倚在扶手椅里，坐在小炉火旁——这间屋子厨房饭厅两用。梅正忙着针线活。

「亲爱的，」老人柔声说，「我看你今晚最好别再出门了。」

===Original===
"Why not, grandpa?" asked the girl without looking up from her work.

"Well, it is probably selfishness on my part," he said, "but somehow I do not want to be left alone. I am expecting a visitor."

"A visitor!"

Visitors were unusual at No. 9 Pexton Street, Hoxton. The only visitor they knew was the rent man who called with monotonous regularity every Monday morning.

"Yes," said her grandfather hesitatingly, "I think you remember the gentleman; you saw him some time ago."

"Not Mr. Standerton?"

===Chinese===
「怎么啦，爷爷？」姑娘头也不抬地问道。

「唉，也许是我自私，」他说，「可我总不想独自一个闷在家里。今晚有位客人要来。」

「客人！」

在霍克斯顿佩克斯顿街九号，客人可是稀罕物。他们认得的唯一客人，就是每礼拜一上午风雨无阻、准时上门的收租人。

「是啊，」祖父迟疑着说，「我想你还记得那位先生吧，前些日子你见过他。」

「不是斯坦德顿先生吧？」

===Original===
The old man shook his head.

"No, not Mr. Standerton," he said, "but you will recall how at Epsom a rather nice man helped you out of a crowd after a race?"

"I remember," she said.

"His name is Wallis," said the old man, "and I met him by accident today when I was shopping."

"Wallis," she repeated.

===Chinese===
老人摇了摇头。

「不是，不是斯坦德顿先生，」他说，「不过你总记得吧，在埃普索姆，赛马散场之后，有位挺好的先生帮你从人群里挤出来？」

「我记得。」她说。

「他名叫沃利斯，」老人说，「今天我出门买东西，恰好碰上了他。」

「沃利斯。」她重复了一遍。

===Original===
Old Wing was silent for a while, then he asked—

"Do you think, my dear, we could take a lodger?"

"Oh, no," protested the girl. "Please not!"

"I find the rent rather heavy," said her grandfather, shaking his head, "and this Mr. Wallis is a quiet sort of person and not likely to give us any trouble."

Still the girl was not satisfied.

"I would rather we didn't," she said. "I am quite sure we can earn enough to keep the house going without that kind of assistance. Lodgers are nuisances. I do not suppose Mrs. Gamage would like it."

===Chinese===
温老头沉默了片刻，然后问道——

「亲爱的，你看咱们能收个房客吗？」

「哎呀，不要，」姑娘反对道，「千万别收！」

「我觉得房租的担子够沉的了，」祖父摇着头说，「再说这位沃利斯先生是个安安静静的人，多半不会给咱们添什么麻烦。」

姑娘还是不乐意。

「我看还是不收的好，」她说，「我敢肯定，不靠这种帮衬，我们挣的也够维持这个家了。房客最招人烦。何况加米奇太太怕也不会乐意。」

===Original===
Mrs. Gamage was the faded neighbour who came in every morning to help straighten the house.

The girl saw the old man's face fall and went round to him, putting her arm around his shoulder.

"Do not bother, grandpa dear," she said, "if you want a lodger you shall have one. I think it would be rather nice to have somebody in the house who could talk to you when I am out."

There was a knock at the door.

"That must be our visitor," she said, and went to open it. She recognised the man who stood in the doorway.

===Chinese===
加米奇太太是隔壁那位颜色黯淡的老邻居，每天上午过来帮着收拾屋子。

姑娘见老人的脸沉了下来，便绕到他跟前，搂住他的肩膀。

「别往心里去，亲爱的爷爷，」她说，「您想要房客，那就收一个。我想家里有个人，我出门的时候能陪您说说话，倒也不坏。」

门外响起敲门声。

「准是咱们那位客人来了。」她说着去开门。她认出了立在门口的那个男人。

===Original===
"May I come in?" he asked. "I wanted to see your grandfather on a matter of business. I suppose you are Miss Wing."

She nodded.

"Come in," she said, and led the way to the kitchen.

===Chinese===
「我可以进来吗？」他问，「我有桩生意上的事要见你祖父。想必你就是温小姐吧。」

她点了点头。

「请进。」她说着，在前头引路进了厨房。

===Original===
"I will not keep you very long," said Mr. Wallis. "No, thank you, I will stand while I am here. I want to find a quiet lodging for a friend of mine. At least," he went on, "he is a man in whom I am rather interested, a very quiet sobersides individual who will be out most of the day, and possibly out most of the night too." He smiled. "He is a—" He hesitated. "He is a taxicab driver, to be exact," he said, "though he does not want this fact to be well known because he has seen—er—better days."

"We have only a very small room we can give your friend," said May, "perhaps you would like to see it."

===Chinese===
「不会耽搁你们太久。」沃利斯先生说，「不用，谢谢，我站着说就行。我想替我一位朋友找处清静的寓所。至少，」他接着说，「他是我相当看重的一个人，一个非常安静、古板严肃的家伙，白天多半在外头，夜里说不定也多半不回家。」他笑了笑。「他是个——」他迟疑了一下。「确切说，他是个开出租汽车的，」他说，「不过他不想让这事广为人知，因为他从前——呃——也见过好日子。」

「我们只有一间很小的屋子可以给您那位朋友。」梅说，「您要不要看看？」

===Original===
She took him up to the spare bedroom which they had used on very rare occasions for the accommodation of the few visitors who had been their guests. The room was neat and clean, and George Wallis nodded approvingly.

"I should like nothing better than this for myself," he said.

He himself suggested a higher price than she asked, and insisted upon paying a month in advance.

"I have told the man to call, he ought to be here by now; if you do not mind, I will wait for him."

It was not a long wait, for in a few minutes there arrived the new lodger. He was a burly man with a heavy black beard, clipped short, and the fact that he was somewhat taciturn and short of speech rather enhanced his value as a lodger than otherwise.

===Chinese===
她领他上楼去看那间备用的卧室——难得有客留宿时，他们才用它招待客人。屋子整洁干净，乔治·沃利斯（George Wallis）赞许地点了点头。

「要是我自己住，再好不过就是这么一间了。」他说。

他自己开的价比她要的还高，并且非要预付一个月不可。

「我已经叫那人过来了，这会儿该到了；不介意的话，我等他一等。」

这一等并不久，几分钟之后新房客就到了。那是个膀阔腰圆的汉子，蓄着一部浓黑的胡须，剪得短短的；他为人沉默寡言、少言少语，这倒让他这个房客的价值不减反增。

===Original===
Wallis took farewell of the old man and his granddaughter, and accompanied by the man, whose name was given somewhat unpromisingly as Smith, he walked to the end of the street.

He had something to say, and that something was important.

"I have got you this place, Smithy," he said, as they walked slowly towards Hoxton High Street, "because it is quiet and fairly safe. The people are respected, and nobody will bother you."

"They are not likely to worry me in any way, are they?" said the man addressed as Smith.

"Not at present," replied the other, "but I do not know exactly how things are going to develop. I am worried."

===Chinese===
沃利斯向老人和他的孙女儿道了别，由那人陪着——那人的名字报得颇不起眼，叫史密斯——朝街尾走去。

他有话要说，而这话事关紧要。

「我替你弄到这地方，史密西，」两人朝霍克斯顿大街慢慢走去，他说道，「因为这儿清静，也相当保险。这家人受人尊敬，没人会来搅扰你。」

「他们总不至于来找我的麻烦吧？」那位被称作史密斯的人说。

「眼下还不会，」对方答道，「可我不清楚事情往后会怎么发展。我心里正犯愁呢。」

===Original===
"What are you worried about?"

George Wallis laughed a little helplessly.

"Why do you ask such stupid questions?" he said with good-natured irritation. "Don't you realise what has happened? Somebody knows our game."

"Well, why not drop it?" asked the other quietly.

"How can we drop it? My dear good chap, though in twelve months we have accumulated a store of movable property sufficiently valuable to enable us all to retire upon, there is not one of us who is willing at this moment to cut out—it would take us twelve months to get rid of the loot," he said thoughtfully.

"I do not exactly know where it is," said Smith with a little smile.

===Chinese===
「你愁什么？」

乔治·沃利斯有点无可奈何地笑了。

「你怎么净问些蠢话？」他说，气恼里带着几分好脾气。「你还不明白出了什么事吗？有人识破了咱们的把戏。」

「那干吗不收手呢？」对方平静地问。

「怎么收手？我亲爱的大好人，咱们十二个月攒下的这批动产，足够值钱，够大伙儿全都金盆洗手、坐吃一辈子了，可眼下没有一个人甘心就此罢手——要把这批赃物出清，还得花上十二个月呢。」他若有所思地说。

「那批东西究竟藏在哪儿，我也不太清楚。」史密斯微微笑道。

===Original===
"Nobody knows that but me," replied Wallis with a little frown, "that is the worrying part of it. I feel the whole responsibility upon me. Smithy, we are being really watched."

The other smiled.

"That isn't unusual," he said. But Wallis was very serious.

"Whom do you suspect?" he asked.

The other did not answer for a moment.

===Chinese===
「除了我，谁也不知道。」沃利斯微微皱眉答道，「这正是叫人犯愁的地方。全部担子都压在我一个人身上。史密西，咱们真的被人盯上了。」

对方笑了。

「这可不新鲜。」他说。可沃利斯一脸正色。

「你疑心是谁？」他问。

对方半晌没作声。

===Original===
"I do not suspect, I know," he said. "A few months ago, when Calli and I were doing a job in Hatton Garden we were interrupted by the arrival of a mysterious gentleman, who watched me open the safe and disappeared immediately afterwards. At that time he did not seem to be particularly hostile or have any ulterior motive in view. Now, for some reason which is best known to himself, he is working against us. That is the man we have got to find."

"But how?"

"Put an advertisement in the paper," said the other sarcastically: "Will the gentleman who dogs Mr. Wallis kindly reveal his identity, and no further action will be taken."

"But seriously!"

"We have got to discover who he is, there must be some way of trapping him; but the only thing to do, and I must do it for my own protection, is to get you all together and share out. We had better meet."

===Chinese===
「我不是疑心，我是知道，」他说，「几个月前，卡利（Calli）跟我在哈顿花园干一票活儿，来了位神秘的先生，把活儿搅了。他瞧着我把保险柜打开，随后立刻就不见了踪影。那时候他瞧着并没有特别的敌意，也看不出另有什么图谋。如今，不知出于什么只有他自己才明白的缘由，他在跟咱们作对。要找的正是这个人。」

「可是怎么找？」

「在报上登条启事呗。」对方讥讽道：「『烦请尾随沃利斯先生的那位绅士亮明身份，概不追究。』」

「别打岔，说正经的！」

「咱们非得查明他是谁不可，总归有法子诱他上钩；不过眼下唯一要办的——为了保全我自己，我非办不可——就是把大伙儿聚齐，把赃分了。咱们最好碰个头。」

===Original===
Smith nodded.

"When?"

"Tonight," said Wallis. "Meet me at the. …

He mentioned the name of a restaurant near Regent Street.

It was, curiously enough, the very restaurant where Gilbert Standerton invariably dined alone.

===Chinese===
史密斯点了点头。

「什么时候？」

「今晚。」沃利斯说，「到……跟我碰头。」

他说出了摄政街附近一家餐馆的名字。

说来也巧，那恰恰是吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿向来独自用晚餐的那家餐馆。

## III Gilbert Leaves Hurriedly / 三　吉尔伯特匆匆离去

===Original===
Gilbert was dressing for dinner when the storm came up over London. It had lost none of its intensity or strength. For an hour the street had glared fitfully in the blue lightning of the electrical discharges, and the house rocked with crash after crash of thunder.

He himself was in tune with the element, for there raged in his heart such a storm as shook the very foundations of his life. Outwardly there was no sign of distress. The face he saw in the shaving-glass was a mask, immobile and expressionless.

He sent his man to call a taxicab. The storm had passed over London, and only the low grumble of thunder could be heard when he came out on to the rain-washed streets. A few wind-torn wisps of cloud were hurrying at a great rate across the sky, stragglers endeavouring in frantic haste to catch up the main body.

===Chinese===
吉尔伯特正在换装准备赴宴，暴风雨忽地袭至伦敦上空。风暴的威势丝毫未减。一个钟头里，街道在青白色的电光中时明时灭，雷声一声接一声，震得房屋直晃。

他自己的心绪恰与这场风雨同调，因为他心里也正翻腾着一场风暴，撼动着他人生的根基。外表却看不出一丝愁苦。剃须镜里那张脸像一副面具，纹丝不动，毫无表情。

他打发仆人去叫出租汽车。暴风雨已经掠过伦敦，他走上被雨水冲洗过的街道时，只听得远处低低的雷声。几绺被风撕碎的云正疾速掠过天空，掉队的残云拼命追赶着大部队。

===Original===
He descended from his cab at the door of No. 274 Portland Square slowly and reluctantly. He had an unpleasant task to perform, as unpleasant to him, more unpleasant, indeed, than it could be to his future mother-in-law.

He did not doubt that the suspicion implanted in his mind by Leslie was unfair and unworthy.

He was ushered into the drawing-room, and found himself the solitary occupant. He looked at his watch.

===Chinese===
他在波特兰广场274号门前下了车，动作迟缓，满心不情愿。他有一桩不快的差事要办——这差事落在他身上已经够难受了，说实话，比落在他未来的岳母身上还要难受。

他心里明白，莱斯利（Leslie）种在他脑子里的那个疑团，既不公平，又不光彩——这一点他毫不怀疑。

他被引进客厅，发现只有自己一个人。他看了看表。

===Original===
"Am I very early, Cole?" he asked the butler.

"You are rather, sir," said the man, "but I will tell Miss Cathcart you are here."

Gilbert nodded. He strolled across to the window, and stood, his hands clasped behind him, looking out upon the wet street. He stood thus for five minutes, his head sunk forward on his breast, absorbed in thought. The opening of the door aroused him, and he turned to meet the girl who had entered.

===Chinese===
「我是不是来得太早了，科尔（Cole）？」他问管家。

「是有点儿早，先生，」管家说，「不过我会去禀告卡思卡特小姐，说您到了。」

吉尔伯特点了点头。他踱到窗前，双手交叠在背后，望着湿漉漉的街道出神。他就这么站了五分钟，头垂在胸前，全然陷入沉思。门一响，他惊醒过来，转身迎向进屋的姑娘。

===Original===
Edith Cathcart was one of the most beautiful women in London, though "woman" might be too serious a word to apply to this slender girl who had barely emerged from her schooldays.

In some grey eyes of a peculiar softness a furtive apprehension always seems to wait—a fear and an appeal at one and the same time. So it was with Edith Cathcart. Those eyes of hers were forever on guard, and even as they attracted they held the overeager seeker of friendship at arm's length. The nose was just a little retroussé; the sensitive lips played supporter to the apprehensive eyes. She wore her hair low over her forehead; it was dark almost to a point of blackness. She was dressed in a plain gown of sea-green satin, with scarcely any jewel or ornamentation.

===Chinese===
伊迪丝·卡思卡特（Edith Cathcart）是伦敦最美的女子之一，尽管用「女子」这个词来形容这位刚刚走出校门的苗条姑娘，未免太老成了些。

有些人的灰眼睛出奇地温柔，那里面仿佛总潜藏着一丝躲闪的忧惧——既是害怕，又是恳求。伊迪丝·卡思卡特的眼睛正是如此。她那双眼睛永远在戒备着，一面吸引人，一面又把过分热切的求友者拒于一臂之外。鼻子微微上翘；敏感的双唇则处处护着那双怯生生的眼睛。她的头发低低地覆在额前，深得几乎发黑。她穿一袭素净的海绿色缎面长裙，几乎不戴什么珠宝首饰。

===Original===
He walked to meet her with quick steps and took both her hands in his; his hungry eyes searched her face eagerly.

"You look lovely tonight, Edith," he said, in a voice scarcely above a whisper.

She released her hands gently with the ghost of a smile that subtly atoned for her action.

"Did you enjoy your Derby Day?" she asked.

"It was enormously interesting," he said; "it is extraordinary that I have never been before."

"You could not have chosen a worse day. Did you get caught in the storm? We have had a terrible one here."

===Chinese===
他快步迎上去，握住她的双手；他那双如饥似渴的眼睛急切地搜索着她的脸。

「你今晚真美，伊迪丝。」他说，声音轻得几乎像耳语。

她轻轻抽回双手，唇边掠过一丝若有若无的笑意，微妙地弥补了这一举动。

「德比大赛日过得快活吗？」她问。

「有意思极了，」他说，「说来奇怪，我以前竟从没去过。」

「再没有比那天更糟的日子了。你没叫暴雨淋着吧？我们这儿可挨了一场可怕的风暴。」

===Original===
She spoke quickly, with a little note of query at the end of each sentence. She gave you the impression that she desired to stand well with her lover, that she was in some awe of him. She was like a child, anxious to acquit herself well of a lesson; and now and then she conveyed a sense of relief, as one who had surmounted yet another obstacle.

Gilbert was always conscious of the strain which marked their relationship. A dozen times a day he told himself that it was incredible that such a strain should exist. But he found a ready excuse for her diffidence and the furtive fear which came and went in her eyes like shadows over the sea. She was young, much younger than her years. This beautiful bud had not opened yet, and his engagement had been cursed by overmuch formality.

===Chinese===
她说话很快，每句话末尾都带着一点探询的口气。她给人的印象是：她想讨情人的欢心，又多少有点怕他。她像个急着背好功课的孩子；时不时还流露出一种如释重负之感，仿佛又闯过了一道难关。

吉尔伯特始终觉得出两人关系中的那道紧张。一天总有十几回，他对自己说，这样的紧张简直叫人难以置信。但对她那份羞怯、对她眼里来来去去如海上掠影般的隐隐畏惧，他总能找到现成的借口：她还年轻，比她的年纪还要年轻得多。这朵美丽的花苞尚未开放，何况这桩婚约又叫过多的虚礼拘束坏了。

===Original===
He had met her conventionally at a ball. He had been introduced by her mother, again conventionally, he had danced with her and sat out with her, punted her on the river, motored her and her mother to Ascot. It was all very ordinary and commonplace. It lacked something. Gilbert never had any doubt as to that.

He took the blame upon himself for all deficiencies, though he was something of a romancist, despite the chilly formalism of the engagement. She had kept him in his place with the rest of the world, one arm's length, with those beseeching eyes of hers. He was at arm's length when he proposed, in a speech the fluency of which was eloquent of the absence of anything which touched emotionalism. And she had accepted in a murmured word, and turned a cold cheek for his kiss, and then had fluttered out of his arms like an imprisoned bird seeking its liberty, and had escaped from that conventional conservatory with its horrible palms and its spurious Tanagra statuettes.

===Chinese===
他是在一场舞会上按部就班地认识她的。介绍人是她母亲——同样按部就班；他同她跳过舞，陪她坐过舞歇，在河上为她撑过篙，开着车载她和她的母亲去过阿斯科特。一切都平平常常、寡淡无味。总缺了点什么。这一点吉尔伯特从不怀疑。

种种缺欠他都揽在自己身上，虽说婚约冷冰冰地拘于俗礼，他骨子里却是个爱幻想的浪漫人。她用那双恳求的眼睛，把他同世上其余的人一样拒在一臂之外。他求婚时也隔着这一臂的距离，一席话说得流利之至，恰恰证明其中毫无动情的成分。她低声细语地应允了，却把一副冷冰冰的脸颊凑过来受他一吻，随即像一只囚鸟寻着了自由似的扑棱出他的怀抱，逃出那间俗气的温室——里面立着可怕的棕榈和冒牌的塔纳格拉陶俑。

===Original===
Gilbert in love was something of a boy; an idealist, a dreamer. Other grown men have shared his weakness, there are unsuspected wells of romance in the most practical of men. So he was content with his dreams, weaving this and that story of sweet surrender in his inmost heart. He loved her, completely, absorbingly. To him she was a divine and a fragrant thing.

He had taken her hand again in his, and realised with pain, which was tinctured with amusement that made it bearable, that she was seeking to disengage herself, when Mrs. Cathcart came into the room.

===Chinese===
恋爱中的吉尔伯特颇像个孩子；一个理想主义者，一个耽于梦幻的人。别的成年男子也有他这毛病：即便最讲实际的男人，心底也藏着无人知晓的浪漫之泉。所以他甘愿守着自己的梦，在内心深处编织着一段又一段甜蜜相许的故事。他爱她，完完全全、如痴如醉地爱着。在他眼里，她是神圣而芬芳的。

他重又把她的手握在手里，却痛苦地察觉她正想抽回去——好在痛苦里掺着几分自嘲的谐趣，倒还受得住。正在这时，卡思卡特夫人（Mrs. Cathcart）走进了客厅。

===Original===
She was a tall woman, still beautiful, though age had given her a certain angularity. The ravages of time had made it necessary for her to seek artificial aid for the strengthening of her attractions. Her mouth was thin and straight and uncompromising, her chin too bony to be beautiful. She smiled as she rustled across the room and offered her gloved hand to the young man.

"You're early, Gilbert," she said.

"Yes," he replied awkwardly. Here was the opportunity which he sought, yet he experienced some reluctance in availing himself of the chance.

===Chinese===
她是个高个子女人，风韵犹存，只是上了年纪，身子骨显出几分棱角。岁月的侵蚀逼得她不得不借助人工手段来加固自己的魅力。她的嘴又薄又直，寸步不让；下巴骨太突出，算不上美。她窸窸窣窣地穿过客厅，微笑着把戴着手套的手伸给年轻人。

「你来得早，吉尔伯特。」她说。

「是啊。」他局促地应道。他寻找的正是个机会，可真到了跟前，又有些踌躇，不想轻易用它。

===Original===
He had released the girl as the door opened, and she had instinctively taken a step backward, and stood with her hands behind her, regarding him gravely and intently.

"Really," he said, "I wanted to see you."

"To see me?" asked Mrs. Cathcart archly. "No, surely not me!"

Her smile comprehended the girl and the young man. For some reason which he could not appreciate at the moment Gilbert felt uncomfortable.

===Chinese===
门开时他已放开了姑娘，她本能地退后一步，双手背在身后，神情严肃而专注地望着他。

「其实，」他说，「我是想见见您。」

「见我？」卡思卡特夫人狡黠地问，「不对吧，总不会是我吧！」

她的笑容把姑娘和年轻人都笼罩在内。不知怎的——吉尔伯特一时也说不上来——他只觉得浑身不自在。

===Original===
"Yes, it was to see you," he said, "but it isn't remarkable at this particular period of time."

He smiled again.

She held up a warning finger.

"You must not bother about any of the arrangements. I want you to leave that entirely to me. You will find you have no cause to complain."

"Oh, it wasn't that," he said hastily, "it was something more—more—"

===Chinese===
「没错，就是想见您，」他说，「不过在眼下这个节骨眼上，也不算什么稀奇事。」

他又笑了笑。

她竖起一根手指警示他。

「各项筹备你一律不用操心。我要你把这事全交给我。保管你挑不出半点错来。」

「哦，不是为那个，」他急忙说，「是另外一件——一件更——」

===Original===
He hesitated. He wanted to convey to her the gravity of the business he had in hand. And even as he approached the question of an interview, a dim realisation came to him of the difficulty of his position. How could he suggest to this woman, who had been all kindness and all sweetness to him, that he suspected her of motives which did credit neither to her head nor her heart? How could he broach the subject of his poverty to one who had not once but a hundred times confided to him that his expectations and the question of his future wealth were the only drawbacks to what she had described as an ideal love marriage?

"I almost wish you were poor, Gilbert," she had said. "I think riches are an awful handicap to young people circumstanced as you and Edith will be."

===Chinese===
他迟疑了。他想让她明白自己手头这桩事情的分量。可刚要提单独谈谈的请求，他就隐约意识到自己处境有多为难。对这个待他百般和气、千般温存的女人，他怎么开得了口，说自己疑心她的动机既不高明也不厚道？对她这样一个人——她可不止一次、而是上百次向他吐露：他的遗产预期和他将来发家的问题，是她口中那桩理想爱情婚姻唯一的缺憾——他又怎么提得起自己贫穷的话题？

「我几乎巴不得你是个穷人呢，吉尔伯特，」她曾这样说，「依我看，像你和伊迪丝这样境况的年轻人，有钱实在是天大的拖累。」

===Original===
She had conveyed this suspicion of his wealth more than once. And yet, at a chance word from Leslie, he had doubted the purity of her motives! He remembered with a growing irritation that it had been Mrs. Cathcart who had made the marriage possible; the vulgar-minded might even have gone further, and suggested that she had thrown Edith at his head. There was plenty of groundwork for Leslie's suspicion, he thought, as he looked at the tall, stylish woman before him. Only he felt ashamed that he had listened to the insidious suggestion.

"Could you give me a quarter of an hour—" He stopped. He was going to say "before dinner," but thought that possibly an interview after the meal would be less liable to interruption.

"—after dinner?"

"With pleasure," she smiled. "What are you going to do? Confess some of the irregularities of your youth?"

===Chinese===
她对他家产的这番「疑心」表露过不止一次。可他竟凭莱斯利随口的一句话，就怀疑起她动机的纯贞！他带着一股越来越盛的恼意想起，促成这桩婚事的正是卡思卡特夫人；心思鄙俗的人甚至可以更进一步，说她是有意把伊迪丝往他怀里推。瞧着眼前这位高挑时髦的女人，他想，莱斯利的疑心不是没有来由。只是他羞愧自己竟听了那句阴损的进言。

「您能不能给我一刻钟——」他顿住了。他本想说「晚饭之前」，转念一想，饭后谈或许不容易被人打断。

「——晚饭之后，行吗？」

「非常乐意。」她微笑道，「你要做什么？坦白几句年轻时荒唐事？」

===Original===
He shook his head with a little grimace.

"You may be sure I shall never tell you those," he said.

"Then I will see you after dinner," she assented. "There are a lot of people coming tonight, and I am simply up to my eyes in work. You bridegrooms," she patted his shoulder with her fan reproachfully, "have no idea what chaos you bring into the domestic life of your unfortunate relatives of the future."

===Chinese===
他微微做了个苦脸，摇了摇头。

「你放心，那些事我永远不会告诉你。」他说。

「那就晚饭后见。」她应允道，「今晚要来一大帮人，我忙得脚不沾地。你们这些新郎倌哪，」她用扇子嗔怪地拍了拍他的肩，「根本想不到会给自家倒霉的未来姻亲的家庭生活搅出多大的乱子。」

===Original===
Edith stood aloof, in the attitude she had adopted when he had released her, watchful, curious, in the scene, but not of it. It was an effect which the presence of Mrs. Cathcart invariably produced upon her daughter. It was not an obliteration, not exactly an eclipse, as the puzzled Gilbert had often observed. It was as though the entrance of one character of a drama were followed by the immediate exit of her who had previously occupied the scene. He pictured Edith waiting at the wings for a cue which would bring her into active existence again, and that cue was invariably the retirement of her mother.

===Chinese===
伊迪丝冷立一旁，还是他放开她时的那副姿态，警觉而好奇，身在戏中，心却在戏外。卡思卡特夫人一出场，总会在女儿身上产生这种效果。那不是抹杀，也不全然是遮蔽，困惑的吉尔伯特常这么琢磨。倒像是一出戏里，一个角色上场，先前占着舞台的那位便立即下场。他想象伊迪丝候在侧幕边，等着一句能让她重新活过来的台词——而那句台词，从来都是她母亲的退场。

===Original===
"There are quite a number of nice people coming tonight, Gilbert," said Mrs. Cathcart, glancing at a slip of paper in her hand. "There are some you don't know, and some I want you very much to meet. I am sure you will like dear Dr. Cassylis—"

A smothered exclamation caught her ear, and she looked up sharply. Gilbert's face was set: it was void of all expression. The girl saw the mask and wondered.

"What is it?" asked Mrs. Cathcart.

"Nothing," said Gilbert steadily, "you were talking about your guests."

===Chinese===
「今晚要来不少体面人呢，吉尔伯特。」卡思卡特夫人说，眼睛扫了扫手里的一张纸条，「有几个你不认识，还有几个我很想让你见见。我敢说你准会喜欢亲爱的卡西利斯医生（Dr. Cassylis）——」

一声压住的惊呼传进她耳朵，她霍地抬头。吉尔伯特的脸绷得紧紧的：毫无表情。姑娘瞧见了这副面具，心里纳闷。

「怎么了？」卡思卡特夫人问。

「没什么，」吉尔伯特镇定地说，「您刚才在说您的客人们。」

===Original===
"I was saying that you must meet Dr. Barclay-Seymour—he is a most charming man. I don't think you know him?"

Gilbert shook his head.

"Well, you ought to," she said. "He's a dear friend of mine, and why on earth he practises in Leeds instead of maintaining an establishment in Harley Street I haven't the slightest idea. The ways of men are beyond finding out. Then there is. …"

She reeled off a list of names, some of which Gilbert knew.

===Chinese===
「我是说你一定要见见巴克利-西摩医生（Dr. Barclay-Seymour）——他是个极有魅力的人。你应该不认识他吧？」

吉尔伯特摇了摇头。

「那你真该认识认识，」她说，「他是我的挚友，他到底为什么偏在利兹行医，而不在哈利街开间诊所，我实在想不明白。男人的心思没个准儿。还有一位是……」

她一口气报出一串名字，有几个吉尔伯特认得。

===Original===
"What is the time?" she asked suddenly. Gilbert looked at his watch.

"A quarter to eight? I must go," she said. "I will see you immediately after dinner."

She turned back as she reached the door irresolutely.

"I suppose you aren't going to change that absurd plan of yours," she asked hopefully.

===Chinese===
「几点了？」她突然问。吉尔伯特看了看表。

「八点差一刻？我得走了，」她说，「晚饭后我立刻见你。」

她走到门口，又迟疑地回过身来。

「你总不至于还要坚持你那个荒唐的计划吧。」她满怀希冀地问。

===Original===
Gilbert had recovered his equanimity.

"I do not know to which absurd plan you are referring," he said.

"Spending your honeymoon in town," she replied.

"I don't think Gilbert should be bothered about that."

It was the girl who spoke, her first intrusion into the conversation. Her mother glanced at her sharply.

"In this case, my dear," she said freezingly, "it is a matter in which I am more concerned than yourself."

===Chinese===
吉尔伯特已经恢复了平静。

「我不知道您指的是哪一条荒唐计划。」他说。

「在城里度蜜月呀。」她答道。

「我看不该拿这种事烦吉尔伯特。」

说话的是那姑娘，这是她头一回插进谈话。她母亲锐利地扫了她一眼。

「在这个问题上，亲爱的，」她冷冰冰地说，「我可比你操心得多。」

===Original===
Gilbert hastened to relieve the girl of the brunt of the storm. Mrs. Cathcart was not slow to anger, and although Gilbert himself had never felt the lash of her bitter tongue, he had a shrewd suspicion that his future wife had been a victim more than once.

"It is absolutely necessary that I should be in town on the days I referred to," he said. "I have asked you—"

"To postpone the wedding?" said Mrs. Cathcart. "My dear boy, I couldn't do that. It wasn't a reasonable request, now was it?"

She smiled at him as sweetly as her inward annoyance allowed her.

"I suppose it wasn't," he said dubiously.

===Chinese===
吉尔伯特赶紧抢上前去，替姑娘挡下这场风暴的锋头。卡思卡特夫人动气来得快，虽然吉尔伯特自己从未领教过她那张利嘴的厉害，他却精明地疑心，他未来的妻子早就不止一次当过它的牺牲品。

「我说过的那几天，我实在非留在城里不可，」他说，「我请求过您——」

「把婚期往后推？」卡思卡特夫人说，「我亲爱的孩子，那可不行。这要求不合情理，你说是不是？」

她朝他微笑，笑得她心里的恼火所能容许的那般甜。

「大概是不合情理。」他将信将疑地说。

===Original===
He said no more, but waited until the door had closed behind her, then he turned quickly to the girl.

"Edith," he said, speaking rapidly, "I want you to do something for me."

"You want me to do something?" she asked in surprise.

"Yes, dearest. I must go away now. I want you to find some excuse to make to your mother. I've remembered a most important matter which I have not seen to—"

He spoke hesitatingly, for he was no ready liar.

===Chinese===
他没再说什么，只等门在她身后关上，便急忙转向姑娘。

「伊迪丝，」他飞快地说，「我要你替我做件事。」

「你要我替你做事？」她惊讶地问。

「是的，最亲爱的。我现在必须走。我想请你给你母亲找个托词。我想起一件顶要紧的事，一直没顾上办——」

他说得吞吞吐吐，因为他不善撒谎。

===Original===
"Going away!"

It was surprise rather than disappointment, he noticed, and was pardonably irritated.

"You can't go now," she said, and that look of fear came into her eyes. "Mother would be so angry. The people are arriving."

From where he stood he had seen three motor broughams draw up almost simultaneously in front of the house.

"I must go," he said desperately. "Can't you get me out in any way? I don't want to meet these people, I've very good reasons."

===Chinese===
「要走！」

他注意到，那语气里是惊讶多过失望，于是没好气起来——这也在情理之中。

「你现在不能走，」她说，那丝畏惧又爬进她眼里，「母亲会气坏的。客人们就要到了。」

从他站的地方，他瞧见三辆轿车几乎同时停在了宅门前。

「我非走不可，」他孤注一掷地说，「你能不能想个法子让我出去？我不想见这些人，我有充分的理由。」

===Original===
She hesitated a moment.

"Where are your hat and coat?" she asked.

"In the hall—you will just have time," he said.

She was in the hall and back again with his coat, led him to the farther end of the drawing-room, through a door which communicated with the small library beyond. There was a way here to the garage and to the mews at the back of the house.

===Chinese===
她迟疑了片刻。

「你的帽子和外套在哪儿？」她问。

「在门厅里——你这就去，还来得及。」他说。

她去门厅取了他的外套回来，领他走到客厅尽头，穿过一扇门，通向后面的小书房。从这儿有条路，可通到车库和屋后的马厩巷。

===Original===
She watched the tall, striding figure with a troubled gaze, then as he disappeared from view she fastened the library door and came back to the drawing-room in time to meet her mother.

"Where is Gilbert?" asked Mrs. Cathcart.

"Gone," said the girl.

"Gone!"

Edith nodded slowly.

"He remembered something very important and had to go back to his house."

===Chinese===
她忧心忡忡地望着那高大阔步的身影，等他消失在视线之外，便闩上书房门，回到客厅，正赶上母亲进来。

「吉尔伯特呢？」卡思卡特夫人问。

「走了。」姑娘说。

「走了！」

伊迪丝缓缓点头。

「他想起一件顶要紧的事，不得不回自己家一趟。」

===Original===
"But of course he is returning?"

"I don't think so, mother," she said quietly. "I fancy that the 'something' is immensely pressing."

"But this is nonsense!" Mrs. Cathcart stamped her foot. "Here are all the people whom I have specially invited to meet him. It's disgraceful!"

"But, mother—"

"Don't 'but mother' me, for God's sake!" said Mrs. Cathcart.

===Chinese===
「可他当然还会回来的吧？」

「我看不会了，母亲，」她轻声说，「我想那件『什么事』十万火急。」

「这简直是胡闹！」卡思卡特夫人跺了跺脚，「我专门请来见他的客人全到了，他却走了！真丢人！」

「可是，母亲——」

「看在上帝份上，别拿『可是母亲』来堵我！」卡思卡特夫人说。

===Original===
They were alone, the guests were assembling in the larger drawing-room, and there was no need for the elder woman to disguise her feelings.

"You sent him away, I suppose?" she said. "I don't blame him. How can you expect to keep a man at your side if you treat him as though he were a grocer calling for orders?"

The girl listened wearily, and did not raise her eyes from the carpet.

"I do my best," she said in a low voice.

===Chinese===
她们单独待着，客人们正在大客厅里聚集，年长的这位妇人用不着再掩饰自己的情绪了。

「是你把他打发走的吧？」她说，「我不怪他。你把男人当成上门取订单的杂货铺伙计似的，还指望他留在你身边吗？」

姑娘疲惫地听着，眼睛没离开地毯。

「我是尽了力的。」她低声说。

===Original===
"Your worst must be pretty bad if that is your best. After I've strained my every effort to bring to you one of the richest young men in London, you might at least pretend that his presence is welcome; but if he were the devil himself you couldn't show greater reluctance at meeting him or greater relief at his departure."

"Mother!" said the girl, and her eyes were filled with tears.

"Don't 'mother' me, please!" said Mrs. Cathcart deliberately. "I am sick to death of your faddiness and your prejudices. What on earth do you want? What am I to get you?"

She threw out her arms in exasperated despair.

===Chinese===
「要是这就叫尽力，那你不用心的时候得糟成什么样。我拼了全力给你弄来伦敦数得着的阔少爷之一，你至少也该装出副欢迎他的样子；可你的表现，仿佛他哪怕是魔鬼本人，你见他时的勉强、送他走时的庆幸，也不过如此了。」

「母亲！」姑娘说，眼里噙满了泪水。

「请别一口一个『母亲』！」卡思卡特夫人一字一板地说，「你的怪脾气和偏见真让我烦透了。你到底想要什么？还要我给你弄来什么？」

她气恼绝望地摊开双臂。

===Original===
"I don't want to marry at all," said the girl in a low voice. "My father would never have forced me to marry."

It was a daring thing to say, an exhibition of greater boldness than she had ever shown before in her encounters with her mother. But lately there had come to her a new courage. That despair which had made her dumb glowed now to rage, the fires of rebellion smouldered in her heart; and, albeit the demonstrations of her growing resentment were few and far between, her courage grew upon her venturing.

===Chinese===
「我根本不想嫁人。」姑娘低声说，「若是父亲在，他绝不会逼我结婚。」

这话出口真是大胆，比她以往同母亲交锋时所表现的都大胆。可近来她生出了一种新的勇气。那曾使她哑口无言的绝望，此刻燃成了怒火，反叛的火种在她心里闷烧；虽说她日渐增长的怨愤表露得既少且稀，她的胆量却随着一次次冒险壮大起来。

===Original===
"Your father!" breathed Mrs. Cathcart, white with rage, "am I to have your father thrown at my head? Your father was a fool! A fool!" She almost hissed the word. "He ruined me as he ruined you because he hadn't sufficient sense to keep the money he had inherited. I thought he was a clever man. I looked up to him for twenty years as the embodiment of all that was wise and kind and genial, and all those twenty years he was frittering away his competence on every hair-brained scheme which the needy adventurers of finance brought to him. He would not have forced you! I swear he wouldn't!" She laughed bitterly. "He would have married you to the chauffeur if your heart was that way inclined. He was all amiability and incompetence, all good-nature and inefficiency. I hate your father!"

===Chinese===
「你父亲！」卡思卡特夫人气得脸色发白，「难道还要把你父亲也摔到我头上来？你父亲是个傻子！傻子！」这个词几乎是从她牙缝里嘶出来的。「他毁了我，也毁了你，因为他没有足够的头脑守住继承来的钱财。我还当他是个聪明人。我敬重了他二十年，把他当作智慧、仁厚、和蔼的化身；而那整整二十年，他都在把家底挥霍给各色轻率的投机计划——全是不名一文的金融冒险家们送到他跟前去的。他才不会逼你！我发誓他不会！」她苦笑了一声。「你的心要是向着那个汽车司机，他都能把你嫁给他。他浑身是和气和无能，满是好脾气和不顶事。我恨你父亲！」

===Original===
Her blue eyes were opened to their widest extent and the cold glare of hate was indeed apparent to the shrinking girl. "I hate him every time I have to entertain a shady stockbroker for the advantage I may receive from his knowledge of the market; I hate him for every economy I have to practise; I hate him every time I see my meagre dividends come in and as I watch them swallowed up by the results of his folly. Don't make me hate you," she said, pointing a warning finger at the girl.

===Chinese===
她的蓝眼睛睁到了最大，那仇恨的冷光，瑟缩着的姑娘看得真真切切。「每当我不得不陪某个来路不清的股票经纪人应酬，好换取一点他在行情上的门路，我就恨他；我每省一分钱，就恨他一分；每回眼看着我那点可怜的股息进账，又被他蠢行的窟窿吞个精光，我就恨他。别逼得我也恨你。」她说着，朝姑娘竖起警告的手指。

===Original===
Edith had cowered before the torrent of words, but this slander of her dead father roused something within her, put aside all fear of consequence, even though that consequence might be a further demonstration of that anger which she so dreaded.

Now she stood erect, facing the woman she called mother, her face pale, but her chin tilted a little defiantly.

"You may say what you like about me, mother," she said quietly, "but I will not have you defame my father. I have done all you requested: I am going to marry a man who, though I know he is a kindly and charming man, is no more to me than the first individual I might meet in the street tonight. I am making this sacrifice for your sake: do not ask me to forego my faith in the man who is the one lovable memory in my life."

Her voice broke a little, her eyes were bright with tears.

===Chinese===
在这番话的洪流面前，伊迪丝本来一直在畏缩，可对亡父的这番诋毁在她心里激起了别的东西，让她把对后果的恐惧统统抛开——哪怕那后果，可能是她如此惧怕的那场怒火再度发作。

此刻她站得笔直，面对着她称作母亲的女人，脸色苍白，下巴却微微扬起，带着一点挑衅。

「关于我，您爱说什么都行，母亲，」她平静地说，「但我不许您诋毁我的父亲。您要求的我都做了：我就要嫁给一个男人，我知道他心地好、人也迷人，可他对我而言，同今晚街上碰见的头一个路人没有什么两样。我为您做出这个牺牲：别再要求我放弃对这个人的信念——他是我此生唯一可爱的回忆。」

她的声音有些哽咽，眼里闪着泪光。

===Original===
Whatever Mrs. Cathcart might have said, and there were many things she could have said, was checked by the entry of a servant.

For a moment or two they stood facing one another, mother and daughter, in silence. Then without another word Mrs. Cathcart turned on her heel and walked out of the room.

The girl waited for a moment, then went back to the library through which Gilbert had passed. She closed the door behind her and turned on one of the lights, for it was growing dark. She was shaking from head to foot with the play of these pent emotions of hers. She could have wept, but with anger and shame. For the first time in her life her mother had shown her heart. The concentrated bitterness of years had poured forth, unchecked by pity or policy. She had revealed the hate which for all these years had been gnawing at her soul; revealed in a flash the relationship between her father and her mother which the girl had never suspected.

===Chinese===
卡思卡特夫人本来也许还要说些什么——她可说的多着呢——却被仆人进来打断了。

母女俩面对面默默站了片刻。然后卡思卡特夫人一言不发，转身走出房间。

姑娘等了片刻，然后回到吉尔伯特方才穿过的那间书房。她关上身后的门，拧亮一盏灯——天色渐暗了。她因为这一番郁积情感的翻腾，从头到脚都在发抖。她本可以哭一场，却是因为愤怒和羞耻。平生第一次，母亲向她袒露了心底。经年累月积下的怨毒一泻而出，全无怜悯，也不讲策略。她亮出了这些年一直啃噬她灵魂的恨；一闪之间，也亮出了姑娘从不曾疑心过的、她父亲与母亲之间的关系。

===Original===
That they had not been on the most affectionate terms Edith knew, but her short association with the world in which they moved had reconciled her mind to the coolness which characterised the attitudes of husband and wife. She had seen a score of such houses where man and wife were on little more than friendly terms, and had accepted such conditions as normal. It aroused in her a wild irritation that such relationships should exist: child as she was, she had felt that something was missing. But it had also reconciled her to her marriage with Gilbert Standerton. Her life with him would be no worse, and probably might be a little better, than the married lives of those people with whom she was brought into daily contact.

===Chinese===
父母俩感情并不怎么亲厚，这一点伊迪丝早知道；但同他们往来那个圈子短短的接触，已让她对这对夫妻之间惯有的冷淡安之若素。她见过一二十家这样的人家，夫妻之间顶多算是朋友，她也把这种光景当作常态接受了。这样的关系竟然存在，让她心头泛起一阵无名的烦躁：虽说还是个孩子，她早就觉得缺了点什么。可这也让她对自己与吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿的婚事认了命。同他过活，不会比她日日打交道的那些人家的婚后日子更坏，说不定还好上一点。

===Original===
But in her mother's vehemence she caught a glimpse of the missing quality of marriage. She knew now why her gentle father had changed suddenly from a genial, kindly man, with his quick laugh and his too willing ear for the plausible, into a silent shadow of a man, the sad, broken figure she so vividly retained in her memory.

Here was a quick turn in the road of life for her an unexpected vista flashing into view suddenly before her eyes. It calmed her, steadied her. In those few minutes of reflection, standing there in the commonplace, gloomy little library, watching through the latticed panes the dismal mews which offered itself for inspection through a parallelogram of bricked courtyard, she experienced one of those great and subtle changes which come to humanity.

===Chinese===
可在母亲的激愤里，她瞥见了婚姻里缺失的那样东西。她如今明白了，她那温和的父亲为什么会突然从一个爽朗爱笑、耳根软、什么像样话都肯听的和善之人，变成一道沉默的影子——那副愁苦颓唐的身影，她还清清楚楚记在心里。

对她来说，这是人生路上的一个急转弯，一片料想不到的景象蓦地闪现在眼前。这让她平静下来，也让她站稳了。那几分钟里，她站在那间平平常常、光线幽暗的小书房中，透过格状窗棂，望着砖砌庭院框出的一方平行四边形里那副凄凉的马厩巷景象，经历了一场人生中偶有的那种重大而微妙的变化。

===Original===
There was a new outlook, a new standard by which to measure her fellows, a new philosophy evolved in the space of a second. It was a tremendous upheaval of settled conviction which this tiny apartment witnessed.

She was surprised herself at the calmness with which she returned to the drawing-room and joined the party now beginning to assemble. It came as a shock to discover that she was examining her mother with the calm, impartial scrutiny of one who was not in any way associated with her. Mrs. Cathcart observed the girl's self-possession and felt a twinge of uneasiness.

===Chinese===
眼前有了新的视界，有了衡量世人的新标准，有了一瞬间孕育出的新哲学。这间小小的屋子，见证了一场既定信念的大翻腾。

回到客厅、加入渐聚的宾客之中时，自己何以这般镇定，连她也觉得诧异。她发觉自己正用一种冷静、超然的眼光打量母亲，像个同母亲毫无干系的人——这一发现叫她自己也吃了一惊。卡思卡特夫人察觉了姑娘的沉着，心里掠过一丝不安。

===Original===
She addressed her unexpectedly, hoping to surprise her to embarrassment, and was a little staggered by the readiness with which the girl met her gaze and the coolness with which she disagreed to some proposition which the elder woman had made.

It was a new experience to the masterful Mrs. Cathcart. The girl might be sulking, but this was a new variety of sulks, foreign to Mrs. Cathcart's experience.

She might be angry, yet there was no sign of anger; hurt—she should have been in tears. Mrs. Cathcart's experienced eye could detect no sign of weeping. She was puzzled, a little alarmed. She had gone too far, she thought, and must conciliate, rather than carry on the feud until the other sued for forgiveness.

===Chinese===
她出其不意地找话同她说，想让她窘迫失态，不料姑娘坦然迎住她的目光，又那般冷静地驳回了她提的某个主张，倒叫她有点儿乱了阵脚。

对惯于发号施令的卡思卡特夫人来说，这可是桩新鲜事。姑娘或许是在赌气，可这是一种新型的赌气，卡思卡特夫人闻所未闻。

她也许在生气，可看不出一点气恼的样子；若是伤了心——那就该以泪洗面。卡思卡特夫人的老到眼光瞧不出半点哭过的痕迹。她纳闷了，也有点慌了。她想，自己做得过了头，得去缓和缓和，不能把这场僵局拖到对方讨饶为止。

===Original===
It irritated her to find herself in this position, but she was a tactician first and foremost, and it would be bad tactics on her part to pursue a disadvantage. Rather she sought the status quo ante bellum, and was annoyed to discover that it had gone forever.

She hoped the talk that evening would confuse the girl to the point of seeking her protection; but to her astonishment Edith spoke of her marriage as she had never spoken of it before, without embarrassment, without hesitation, coolly, reasonably, intelligently.

The end of the evening found Edith commanding her field and her mother in the position of a suitor.

===Chinese===
落到这般境地让她恼火，但她首先是个战术家，明知不利还穷追猛打，在她看来是下策。她宁可去寻回「开战之前」的原状，却恼恨地发现，那原状已经一去不返了。

她指望当晚的谈话能把姑娘搅得晕头转向，来寻求她的庇护；可叫她吃惊的是，伊迪丝谈起自己的婚事，竟像从未有过的那般——不窘迫，不迟疑，冷静、在理、明白。

到晚上收场时，伊迪丝已稳稳占了上风，母亲反倒落到了求和的地步。

===Original===
Mrs. Cathcart waited till the last guest had gone, then she came into the smaller drawing-room to find Edith standing in the fireplace, looking thoughtfully at a paper which lay upon the mantelshelf.

"What is it interests you so much, dear?"

The girl looked round, picked up the paper and folded it slowly.

"Nothing particularly," she said. "Your Dr. Cassylis is an amusing man."

===Chinese===
卡思卡特夫人等到最后一位客人走了，才走进小客厅，见伊迪丝立在壁炉前，若有所思地望着壁炉台上摊着的一张纸。

「什么东西这么入你的眼，亲爱的？」

姑娘回过头，拿起那张纸，慢慢地折起来。

「没什么特别的，」她说，「您那位卡西利斯医生是个有趣的人。」

===Original===
"He is a very clever man," said her mother tartly.

She had infinite faith in doctors, and offered them the tribute which is usually reserved for the supernatural.

"Is he?" said the girl coolly. "I suppose he is. Why does he live in Leeds?"

"Really, Edith, you are coming out of your shell," said her mother with a forced smile of admiration. "I have never known you take so much interest in the people of the world before."

===Chinese===
「他是个非常聪明的人。」母亲尖刻地说。

她对医生们有着无限的信仰，献给他们的敬意，通常是留给神明的。

「是吗？」姑娘淡淡地说，「大概吧。他为什么住在利兹？」

「说真的，伊迪丝，你总算肯从壳里钻出来了。」母亲强笑着赞许道，「我从没见你对世上的人这么上心过。」

===Original===
"I am going to take a great deal of interest in people," said the girl steadily. "I have been missing so much all my life."

"I think you are being a little horrid," said her mother, repressing her anger with an effort; "you're certainly being very unkind. I suppose all this nonsense has arisen out of my mistaken confidence."

The girl made no reply.

===Chinese===
「往后我要大大地关心人了。」姑娘坚定地说，「这辈子我已经错过太多东西了。」

「我看你这样子有点可恶，」母亲强压着怒气说，「你这样实在很不厚道。我猜，这些胡闹全是我信错了人惹出来的。」

姑娘没有答话。

===Original===
"I think I'll go to bed, mother," she said.

"And whilst you're engaged in settling your estimate of people," said Mrs. Cathcart with ominous calm, "perhaps you will interpret your fiancé's behaviour to me. Dr. Cassylis particularly wanted to meet him."

"I am not going to interpret anything," said the girl.

"Don't employ that tone with me," replied her mother sharply.

===Chinese===
「我想去睡了，母亲。」她说。

「趁着你忙着给世人定估价，」卡思卡特夫人带着不祥的平静说，「也许你肯给我解释解释你未婚夫的举止。卡西利斯医生可是特意要见他的。」

「我什么也不打算解释。」姑娘说。

「别用那种腔调跟我说话。」母亲厉声答道。

===Original===
The girl stopped, she was halfway to the door. She hardly turned, but spoke to her mother over her shoulder.

"Mother," she said, quietly but decidedly, "I want you to understand this: if there is any more bother, or if I am again made the victim of your crossness, I shall write to Gilbert and break off my engagement."

"Are you mad?" gasped the woman.

Edith shook her head.

"No, I am tired," she said, "tired of many things."

There was much that Mrs. Cathcart could have said, but with a belated wisdom she held her tongue till the door had closed behind her daughter. Then, late as the hour was, she sent for the cook and settled herself grimly for a pleasing half hour, for the vol-au-vent had been atrocious.

===Chinese===
姑娘停住了，她已走到半路上，快到门口。她几乎没有回身，只越过肩头对母亲说话。

「母亲，」她平静却决然地说，「我要您明白：要是再有什么纠葛，或者我再成了您坏脾气的牺牲品，我就写信给吉尔伯特，解除婚约。」

「你疯了吗？」妇人倒抽一口气。

伊迪丝摇了摇头。

「没有，我只是累了，」她说，「对许多事情都累了。」

卡思卡特夫人本有许多话可说，但她总算来了点迟到的明智，忍着直到门在女儿身后关上。随后，尽管夜深，她还是叫来厨子，铁青着脸预备享受半个钟头的快意——那道法式酥盒实在做得糟透了。

## II Sunstar's Derby / 二　太阳星的德比大赛

===Original===
There it was again!

Above the babel of sound, the low roar of voices, soft and sorrowful, now heard, now lost, a vagrant thread of gold caught in the drab woof of shoddy life gleaming and vanishing. … Gilbert Standerton sat tensely straining to locate the sound.

It was the "Melody in F" that the unseen musician played.

===Chinese===
又来了！

在那片鼎沸人声之上，一阵低回的乐音，轻柔而哀婉，忽而听得见，忽而听不见，像一缕流徙的金线，缠在粗鄙生活的灰暗织物里，忽明忽灭。……吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿绷紧了身子，竭力追寻那声音的来处。

那位看不见的乐师奏的，正是《F大调旋律》。

===Original===
"There's going to be a storm."

Gilbert did not hear the voice. He sat on the box-seat of the coach, clasping his knees, the perspiration streaming from his face.

There was something tragic, something a little terrifying in his pose. The profile turned to his exasperated friend was a perfect one—forehead high and well-shaped, the nose a little long, perhaps, the chin strong and resolute.

Leslie Frankfort, looking up at the unconscious dreamer, was reminded of the Dante of convention, though Dante never wore a top-hat or found a Derby Day crowd so entirely absorbing.

===Chinese===
「要来暴风雨了。」

吉尔伯特没有听见这话。他坐在马车的车夫座旁，双臂抱着膝头，汗珠顺着脸淌个不停。

他这副姿态里有一种悲剧的、甚至有几分骇人的东西。转向他那恼火的朋友的侧影堪称完美——额头高而端正，鼻子也许略长了些，下巴坚强果决。

莱斯利·弗兰克福特（Leslie Frankfort）仰头望着这位浑然忘我的梦中人，想起世人惯见的那幅但丁像——只是但丁从不戴大礼帽，也从不觉得德比大赛日的人群有这么好看。

===Original===
"There's going to be a storm."

Leslie climbed up the short stepladder, and swung himself into the seat by Gilbert's side.

The other awoke from his reverie with a start.

"Is there?" he asked, and wiped his forehead.

Yet as he looked around it was not the murky clouds banking up over Banstead that held his eye; it was this packed mass of men and women, these gay placards extolling loudly the honesty and the establishment of "the old firm," the booths on the hill, the long succession of canvas screens which had been erected to advertise somebody's whisky, the flimsy-looking stands on the far side of the course, the bustle, the pandemonium and the vitality of that vast, uncountable throng made such things as June thunderstorms of little importance.

===Chinese===
「要来暴风雨了。」

莱斯利蹬上短短的活动梯，一转身坐到吉尔伯特身边。

对方这才一惊，从遐想中醒过来。

「是吗？」他问着，抹了抹额头。

可他环顾四周时，抓住他眼睛的并不是班斯特德上空越积越厚的阴云，而是这挤挤挨挨的一大群男男女女，是那些高声颂扬「老字号」信誉与招牌的鲜艳广告牌，是山坡上的一排排棚摊，是为某家威士忌做广告而竖起的一长列帆布围屏，是赛道对面那些看似单薄薄弱的看台——这浩浩荡荡、数不清的人潮里那股喧嚣、鼎沸与生气，让六月里的雷雨这类小事显得无足轻重。

===Original===
"If you only knew how the low brows are pitying you," said Leslie Frankfort, with good-natured annoyance, "you would not be posing for a picture of 'The Ruined Gambler.' My dear chap, you look for all the world, sitting up here with your long, ugly mug adroop, like a model for the coloured plate to be issued with the Christmas Number of the *Anti-Gambling Gazette*. I suppose they have a gazette."

Gilbert laughed a little.

"These people interest me," he said, rousing himself to speak. "Don't you realise what they all mean? Every one of them with a separate and distinct individuality, everyone with a hope or a fear hugged tight in his bosom, everyone with the capacity for love, or hate, or sorrow. Look at that man!" he said, and pointed with his long, nervous finger.

===Chinese===
「你要是知道那些粗人多么可怜你，」莱斯利·弗兰克福特又好气又好笑地说，「就不会再摆出这副《赌毁的赌徒》画中人造型了。我亲爱的伙计，你坐在这儿，长脸耷拉着，活脱脱是《反赌博公报》圣诞特刊附赠彩印画的模特儿。想来他们真有这么一份公报吧。」

吉尔伯特轻轻笑了。

「这些人让我着迷。」他打起精神说，「你难道不明白他们都意味着什么吗？每一个都是各自分明、独一无二的个体，每一个胸中都紧搂着一个希望或一个恐惧，每一个都有爱、恨、悲伤的能力。瞧那个人！」他说着，用细长的、神经质的手指指过去。

===Original===
The man he indicated stood in a little oasis of green. Hereabouts the people on the course had so directed their movements as to leave an open space, and in the centre stood a man of medium height, a black bowler on the back of his head, a long, thin cigar between his white, even teeth. He was too far away for Leslie to distinguish these particulars, but Gilbert Standerton's imagination filled in the deficiencies of vision, for he had seen this man before.

As if conscious of the scrutiny, the man turned and came slowly towards the rails where the coach stood. He took the cigar from his mouth and smiled as he recognised the occupant of the box-seat.

"How do you do, sir?"

His voice sounded shrill and faint, as if an immeasurable distance separated them, but he was evidently shouting to raise his voice above the growling voices of the crowd. Gilbert waved his hand with a smile, and the man turned with a raise of his hat, and was swallowed up in a detachment of the crowd which came eddying about him.

===Chinese===
他指的那个人站在一小片绿洲般的空当里。赛马场上的人流在这儿恰好让开，围出一块空地，当中立着一个中等身材的男人，黑色圆顶礼帽扣在后脑勺上，一口整齐的白牙间咬着一支细细的长雪茄。离得太远，这些细节莱斯利辨不出来，可吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿的想象力补足了目力的不足——这个人他从前见过。

像是觉察到了这道注视，那人转过身，朝马车所停的围栏慢慢走来。他认出了车夫座旁的那位乘客，取下嘴里的雪茄，笑了笑。

「您好啊，先生。」

他的声音听着又尖又细，仿佛两人隔着千山万水，其实显然是在扯着嗓子喊，好压过人群的低吼。吉尔伯特含笑挥了挥手，那人举帽致意，转过身去，随即被一阵打着旋涌来的人群吞没了。

===Original===
"A thief," said Gilbert, "on a fairly large scale—his name is Wallis; there are many Wallises here. A crowd is a terrible spectacle to the man who thinks," he said half to himself.

The other glanced at him keenly.

"They're terrible things to get through in a thunderstorm," he said, practically. "I vote we go along and claim the car."

Gilbert nodded.

===Chinese===
「一个贼，」吉尔伯特说，「相当有规模的贼——他名叫沃利斯；这儿有的是沃利斯。对肯思考的人来说，人群是一幅可怕的景象。」他半自语道。

对方锐利地瞥了他一眼。

「顶着雷雨从人堆里挤出去才叫可怕呢。」他实打实地说，「我提议，咱们这就去把车领出来。」

吉尔伯特点了点头。

===Original===
He rose stiffly, like a man with cramp, and stepped slowly down the little ladder to the ground. They passed through the barrier and crossed the course, penetrated the little unsaddling enclosure, through the long passages where pressmen, jockeys and stewards jostled one another every moment of race days, to the roadway without.

In the roped garage they found their car, and, more remarkable, their chauffeur.

The first flicker of blue lightning had stabbed twice to the Downs, and the heralding crash of thunder had reverberated through the charged air, when the car began to thread the traffic toward London. The storm, which had been brewing all the afternoon, broke with terrific fury over Epsom. The lightning was incessant, the rain streamed down in an almost solid wall of water, crash after crash of thunder deafened them.

===Chinese===
他僵硬地站起身，像个抽了筋的人，慢慢踩着小梯下到地上。他们穿过栅栏，越过赛道，钻进小小的卸鞍围场，再穿过那些长长的过道——每逢赛马日，记者、骑师和赛会干事便在那里挤来挤去——来到外面的路上。

在围起绳栏的停车场里，他们找到了自己的汽车，而且——更难得的是——还找到了他们的司机。

第一道青白的闪电已两度劈上岗地，报信的雷声在饱含电荷的空气里滚动回荡，这时汽车开始穿行于车流之中，朝伦敦驶去。酝酿了一下午的暴风雨，终于在埃普索姆上空以骇人的狂怒倾泻而下。闪电连绵不绝，雨水像几乎连成实体的一堵水墙倾盆而下，一声接一声的雷震得他们耳朵发聋。

===Original===
The great throng upon the hill was dissolving as though it was something soluble; its edges frayed into long black streamers of hurrying people moving toward the three railway stations. It required more than ordinary agility to extricate the car from the chaos of charabancs and motor-cabs in which it found itself.

Standerton had taken his seat by the driver's side, though the car was a closed one. He was a man quick to observe, and on the second flash he had seen the chauffeur's face grow white and his lips twitching. A darkness almost as of night covered the heavens. The horizon about was rimmed with a dull, angry orange haze; so terrifying a storm had not been witnessed in England for many years.

===Chinese===
山坡上那一大群人正在消解，仿佛是件可溶的东西；边缘散成一绺绺长长的黑色人流，急匆匆涌向三个火车站。汽车陷在大巴和出租汽车乱成一团的夹缝里，没有超乎寻常的身手还真开不出来。

虽然这辆车是封闭式的，斯坦德顿还是坐到了司机身旁。他是个眼尖的人，第二道闪电一亮，他就看见司机的脸白了，嘴唇直抽动。一种几乎如黑夜般的黑暗笼罩了天空。四周地平线上镶着一圈暗沉沉、怒冲冲的橙色霾光；这样骇人的风暴，英格兰已多年未见。

===Original===
The rain was coming down in sheets, but the young man by the chauffeur's side paid no heed. He was watching the nervous hands of the man twist this way and that as the car made detour after detour to avoid the congested road.

Suddenly a jagged streak of light flicked before the car, and Standerton was deafened by an explosion more terrifying than any of the previous peals.

The chauffeur instinctively shrank back, his face white and drawn; his trembling hands left the wheel, and his foot released the pedal. The car would have come to a standstill, but for the fact that they were at the top of a declivity.

"My God!" he whimpered, "it's awful. I can't go on, sir."

===Chinese===
大雨倾盆如注，可司机身旁的年轻人毫不在意。他只顾盯着那人紧张不安的双手——汽车为避开堵塞的道路一次次绕行，那双手一会儿这样拧，一会儿那样转。

突然，一道锯齿状的闪光在车前掠过，一声爆炸般的巨响震得斯坦德顿耳朵发聋——比先前哪一声雷都可怕。

司机本能地向后一缩，脸色惨白，五官都走了形；他颤抖的双手离开方向盘，脚也松开了踏板。若不是车正好处在一个斜坡的坡顶，汽车就得停摆了。

「天哪！」他带着哭腔说，「太可怕了。我开不下去了，先生。」

===Original===
Gilbert Standerton's hand was on the wheel, his neatly-booted foot had closed on the brake pedal.

"Get out of it!" he muttered. "Get over here, quick!"

The man obeyed. He moved shivering to his master's place, his hands before his face, and Standerton slipped into the driver's seat and threw in the clutch.

It was fortunate that he was a driver of extraordinary ability, but he needed every scrap of knowledge as he put the car to the slope which led to the lumpy Downs. As they jolted forward the downpour increased, the ground was running with water as though it had been recently flooded. The wheels of the car slipped and skidded over the greasy surface, but the man at the steering-wheel kept his head, and by and by he brought the big car slithering down a little slope on to the main way again. The road was sprinkled with hurrying, tramping people. He moved forward slowly, his horn sounding all the time, and then of a sudden the car stopped with a jerk.

===Chinese===
吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿的手已搭上方向盘，穿着漂亮靴子的脚踩住了刹车踏板。

「让开！」他低声道，「过来这边，快！」

那人依从了。他抖抖索索地挪到主人的座位上，双手遮着脸，斯坦德顿滑进驾驶座，踩下离合器。

幸而他的车技出类拔萃，可把车驶上通往坑洼不平的岗地的坡道时，他还是用上了浑身的本事。车子一路颠簸向前，雨越下越大，地面水流纵横，仿佛刚遭过水淹。车轮在滑腻的路面上打滑侧溜，但握着方向盘的人沉着不乱，不一会儿便让这部大车溜下一段小坡，重新回到干道上。路上散布着匆匆赶路、深一脚浅一脚的行人。他缓缓前行，喇叭一路鸣着，忽然，车猛地一顿，停住了。

===Original===
"What is it?"

Leslie Frankfort had opened the window which separated the driver's seat from the occupants of the car.

"There's an old chap there," said Gilbert, speaking over his shoulder, "would you mind taking him into the car? I'll tell you why after."

He pointed to two woebegone figures that stood on the side of the road. They were of an old man and a girl; Leslie could not see their faces distinctly. They stood with their backs to the storm, one thin coat spread about them both.

===Chinese===
「怎么了？」

莱斯利·弗兰克福特打开了分隔驾驶座与车厢的小窗。

「那边有位老人家，」吉尔伯特回头说，「请他上车好吗？缘故我回头再告诉你。」

他指着路边两个愁苦的身影。那是一位老人和一个姑娘；莱斯利看不清他们的脸。两人背对着风暴站着，一件单薄的外套展开来罩住两个人。

===Original===
Gilbert shouted something, and at his voice the old man turned. He had a beautiful face, thin, refined, intellectual; it was the face of an artist. His grey hair straggled over his collar, and under the cloak he clutched something, the care of which seemed to concern him more than his protection from the merciless downpour.

The girl at his side might have been seventeen, a solemn child, with great fearless eyes that surveyed the occupants of the car gravely. The old man hesitated at Gilbert's invitation, but as he beckoned impatiently he brought the girl down to the road and Leslie opened the door.

"Jump in quickly," he said. "My word, you're wet!"

He slammed the door behind them, and they seated themselves facing him.

They were in a pitiable condition; the girl's dress was soaked, her face was wet as though she had come straight from a bath.

===Chinese===
吉尔伯特喊了句什么，听到他的声音，老人转过身来。他有一张俊美的脸，清瘦、文雅、有书卷气；那是一张艺术家的脸。灰白的头发散乱地披在领子上，斗篷底下他紧攥着一件东西，看护那件东西，仿佛比躲避无情的暴雨还让他上心。

他身边的姑娘约莫十七岁，是个神情庄重的孩子，一双大眼睛毫无惧色，肃然打量着车里的人。老人对吉尔伯特的邀请还有些迟疑，见他又不耐烦地招手，便领着姑娘下了路基，莱斯利推开车门。

「快上来，」他说，「哎呀，你们湿成这样！」

他随手砰地关上车门，两人在他对面坐下了。

他们那副样子着实可怜；姑娘的衣裙湿透了，脸上水淋淋的，像刚从浴缸里出来似的。

===Original===
"Take that cloak off," said Leslie brusquely. "I've a couple of dry handkerchiefs, though I'm afraid you'll want a bath towel."

She smiled.

"It's very kind of you," she said. "We shall ruin your car."

"Oh, that's all right," said Leslie cheerfully. "It's not my car. Anyway," he added, "when Mr. Standerton comes in he will make it much worse."

He was wondering in his mind by what freakish inclination Standerton had called these two people to the refuge of his Limousine.

===Chinese===
「把那斗篷脱了吧。」莱斯利直截了当地说，「我有两条干手帕，只怕你们用得上的是浴巾。」

她笑了。

「您真好心，」她说，「我们要把您的车弄坏了。」

「哦，没关系，」莱斯利乐呵呵地说，「这不是我的车。再说，」他补了一句，「等斯坦德顿先生一进来，车还要更糟呢。」

他心里纳闷，斯坦德顿到底出于什么古怪的念头，竟把这两个人叫进他那辆豪华轿车里避难。

===Original===
The old man smiled as he spoke, and his first words were an explanation.

"Mr. Standerton has always been very good to me," he said gently, almost humbly.

He had a soft, well-modulated voice. Leslie Frankfort recognised that it was the voice of an educated man. He smiled. He was too used to meeting Standerton's friends to be surprised at this storm-soddened street musician, for such he judged him to be by the neck of the violin which protruded from the soaked coat.

"You know him, do you?"

===Chinese===
老人说话时面带微笑，开口头一句便是解释。

「斯坦德顿先生一向待我很好。」他轻声说，近乎谦卑。

他的嗓音柔和、抑扬有度。莱斯利·弗兰克福特听得出，这是一个受过教育的人的声音。他笑了。斯坦德顿的各路朋友他见得多了，对这位浇得透湿的街头乐师并不惊讶——从那件湿透的外衣里露出的琴颈，他便断定了对方的身份。

「您认识他？」

===Original===
The old man nodded.

"I know him very well," he said.

He took from under his coat the thing he had been carrying, and Leslie Frankfort saw that it was an old violin. The old man examined it anxiously, then with a sigh of relief he laid it across his knees.

"It's not damaged, I hope?" asked Leslie.

"No, sir," said the other; "I was greatly afraid that it was going to be an unfortunate ending to what has been a prosperous day."

===Chinese===
老人点了点头。

「我同他很熟。」他说。

他从外衣底下取出一路护着的那样东西，莱斯利·弗兰克福特看清了，是一把旧小提琴。老人急切地查验了一阵，然后如释重负地叹了口气，把它横放在膝上。

「没磕坏吧，我希望？」莱斯利问。

「没有，先生，」对方说，「我还直担心，这么顺遂的一天，到头来要落个不幸的收场呢。」

===Original===
They had been playing on the Downs, and had reaped a profitable harvest.

"My granddaughter also plays," said the old man. "We do not as a rule care for these great crowds, but it invariably means money"—he smiled—"and we are not in a position to reject any opportunity which offers."

They were now drawing clear of the storm. They had passed through Sutton, and had reached a place where the roads were as yet dry, when Gilbert stopped the car and handed the wheel to the shamefaced chauffeur.

"I'm very sorry, sir," the man began.

===Chinese===
他们一直在岗地上卖艺，收获颇丰。

「我的孙女儿也演奏，」老人说，「我们平日不爱凑这种大热闹，可大热闹总意味着钱」——他笑了笑——「而眼下我们还没到能把送上门的机会往外推的地步。」

这时他们已驶出风暴的范围。过了萨顿，到了路面还干燥的地方，吉尔伯特停下车，把方向盘交给那位满面羞惭的司机。

「实在对不起，先生。」那人开口道。

===Original===
"Oh, don't bother," smiled his employer, "one is never to be blamed for funking a storm. I used to be as bad until I got over it … there are worse things," he added, half to himself.

The man thanked him with a muttered word, and Gilbert opened the door of the car and entered. He nodded to the old man and gave a quick smile to the girl.

"I thought I recognised you," he said. "This is Mr. Springs," he said, turning to Leslie. "He's quite an old friend of mine. I'm sure when you have dined at St. John's Wood you must have heard Springs' violin under the dining-room window. It used to be a standing order, didn't it, Mr. Springs?" he said. "By the way," he asked suddenly, "were you playing—"

He stopped, and the old man, misunderstanding the purport of the question, nodded.

===Chinese===
「哦，不必在意，」雇主微笑道，「怕暴风雨不算什么丢人的事。我从前也一样怕，后来才熬过去……天底下还有更可怕的呢。」他半自语地补了一句。

那人含含糊糊道了声谢，吉尔伯特拉开车门坐进去。他朝老人点了点头，又朝姑娘很快地一笑。

「我就说我认得你们。」他说。他又转向莱斯利：「这位是斯普林斯先生（Mr. Springs），我的老朋友了。你在圣约翰林吃过几回饭，准在饭厅窗下听过斯普林斯的小提琴吧。往常那可是雷打不动的老规矩，是不是，斯普林斯先生？」他说着，忽然问道，「对了，你们方才演奏的是——」

他顿住了，老人会错了这问题的意思，点了点头。

===Original===
"After all," said Gilbert, with a sudden change of manner, "it wouldn't be humane to leave my private band to drown on Epsom Downs, to say nothing of the chance of his being struck by lightning."

"Was there any danger?" asked Leslie in surprise.

Gilbert nodded.

"I saw one poor chap struck as I cleared the Downs," he said; "there were a lot of people near him, so I didn't trouble to stop. It was a terrifying experience."

===Chinese===
「毕竟，」吉尔伯特忽然换了副口气说，「眼看我的私家乐队淹死在埃普索姆岗地，未免太不人道了——更别说他还有遭雷劈的危险呢。」

「真有危险吗？」莱斯利惊讶地问。

吉尔伯特点了点头。

「我驶离岗地时，亲眼看见一个可怜的家伙遭了雷击，」他说，「他身边人多，我就没停车费事。那场面真吓人。」

===Original===
He looked back out of the little oval window behind.

"We shall have it again in London tonight," he said, "but storms do not feel so dangerous in town as they do in the country. They're not so alarming. Housetops are very merciful to the nervous."

They took farewell of the old man and his granddaughter at Balham, and then, as the car continued, Leslie turned with a puzzled look to his companion.

"You're a wonderful man, Gilbert," he said; "I can't understand you. You described yourself only this morning as being a nervous wreck—"

===Chinese===
他回头望着身后那扇小小的椭圆形车窗。

「今晚伦敦还得再来一场，」他说，「不过在城里，暴风雨不比乡下那么吓人。没那么叫人心惊。屋顶对胆小的人是很仁慈的。」

他们在巴勒姆同老人和他的孙女儿道了别。车继续前行，莱斯利带着困惑的神色转向同伴。

「你真是个奇人，吉尔伯特，」他说，「我摸不透你。就在今天早上，你还说自己是个神经衰弱的废人——」

===Original===
"Did I say that?" asked the other dryly.

"Well, you didn't admit it," said Leslie, with an aggrieved air, "but it was a description which most obviously fitted you. And yet in the face of this storm, which I confess curled me up pretty considerably, you take the seat of your chauffeur and you push the car through it. Moreover, you are sufficiently collected to pick up an old man, when you had every excuse to leave him to his dismal fate."

For a moment Gilbert made no reply; then he laughed a little bitterly.

"There are a dozen ways of being nervous," he said, "and that doesn't happen to be one of mine. The old man is an important factor in my life, though he does not know it—the very instrument of fate."

===Chinese===
「我这么说过吗？」对方干巴巴地问。

「好吧，你是没认账，」莱斯利一脸委屈地说，「可那形容明明白白就是你的写照。可面对这场风暴——不瞒你说，我都吓得够呛——你倒坐上司机的位子，硬是把车开了出来。更有甚者，你还有闲心捎上一位老人家，虽然你满有理由任他去听天由命。」

吉尔伯特一时没答话；随后苦笑了一下。

「神经质的毛病有十来种，」他说，「那种偏偏不在其列。这位老人是我生命里一个要紧的角色，虽然他自己不知道——他简直就是命运手里的工具。」

===Original===
He dropped his voice almost solemnly. Then he seemed to remember that the curious gaze of the other was upon him.

"I don't know where you got the impression that I was a nervous wreck," he said briefly. "It's hardly the ideal condition for a man who is to be married this week."

"That may be the cause, my dear chap," said the other reflectively. "I know a lot of people who are monstrously upset at the prospect. There was Tuppy Jones who absolutely ran away—lost his memory, or some such newspaper trick."

Gilbert smiled.

===Chinese===
他的声音低了下来，近乎郑重。随即他像是想起对方正用好奇的目光盯着自己。

「不知道你从哪儿得来那么个印象，说我是什么神经衰弱的废人。」他简短地说，「对一个本礼拜就要结婚的人来说，那可不是什么理想状态。」

「那兴许正是缘故呢，亲爱的伙计，」对方若有所思地说，「我认识好多人，一想到娶亲就慌得不成样子。就有那么个塔皮·琼斯（Tuppy Jones），干脆逃之夭夭——说是失了记忆，反正尽是报上那套花活。」

吉尔伯特笑了。

===Original===
"I did the next worst thing to running away," he said a little moodily. "I wanted the wedding postponed."

"But why?" demanded the other. "I was going to ask you that this morning coming down, only it slipped my memory. Mrs. Cathcart told me she wouldn't hear of it."

Gilbert gave him no encouragement to continue the subject, but the voluble young man went on—

"Take what the gods give you, my son," he said. "Here you are with a Foreign Office appointment, an Under-Secretaryship looming in the near future, a most charming and beautiful bride in prospect, rich—"

===Chinese===
「我干了仅次于逃跑的坏事，」他有点闷闷地说，「我想把婚期往后推。」

「那是为什么？」对方追问，「今早来的路上我就想问你，只是忘了。卡思卡特夫人告诉我，她连听都不要听。」

吉尔伯特没有接他的话头，可这位健谈的年轻人自顾自说了下去——

「神明赏你什么，你就接着吧，我的孩子，」他说，「瞧瞧你：外交部的好差事，次官的位子已在不远处招手，一位又迷人又美丽的新娘在望，还富有——」

===Original===
"I wish you wouldn't say that," said Gilbert sharply. "The idea is abroad all over London. Beyond my pay I have no money whatever. This car," he said, as he saw the other's questioning face, "is certainly mine—at least, it was a present from my uncle, and I don't suppose he'll want it returned before I sell it. Thank God it makes no difference to you," he went on with that note of hardness still in his voice, "but I am half inclined to think that two-thirds of the friendships I have, and all the kindness which is from time to time shown to me, is based upon that delusion of riches. People think that I am my uncle's heir."
