"But aren't you?" gasped the other.

Gilbert shook his head.

"My uncle has recently expressed his intention of leaving the whole of his fortune to that admirable institution which is rendering such excellent service to the canine world—the Battersea Dogs' Home."

===Chinese===
「这话你别说了。」吉尔伯特厉声道，「这个念头整个伦敦到处都是。除了薪水，我一无所有。这辆车，」他看见对方疑问的脸色，接着说，「确实是我的——至少，是叔父送的礼物，我想在我把它卖掉之前，他不至于要回去。谢天谢地，这对你们几位毫无影响，」他继续说道，声音里仍带着那股硬邦邦的调子，「可我几乎要相信，我交下的朋友，三分之二是冲着那个财富的错觉来的，人家时不时对我表示的好意，也全建立在那错觉上。人们都当我是叔父的继承人。」

「可你不是吗？」对方倒抽一口气。

吉尔伯特摇了摇头。

「我叔父最近已表了态，要把全部财产捐给那个为犬类世界提供出色服务的可敬机构——巴特西狗之家（Battersea Dogs' Home）。」

===Original===
Leslie Frankfort's jovial face bore an expression of tragic bewilderment.

"Have you told Mrs. Cathcart this?" he asked.

"Mrs. Cathcart!" replied the other in surprise. "No, I haven't told her. I don't think it's necessary. After all," he said with a smile, "Edith isn't marrying me for money, she is pretty rich herself, isn't she? Not that it matters," he said hastily, "whether she's rich or whether she's poor."

===Chinese===
莱斯利·弗兰克福特那张乐呵呵的脸上，露出近乎悲怆的困惑神情。

「这事你告诉卡思卡特夫人了吗？」他问。

「卡思卡特夫人！」对方惊讶地答道，「没有，我没告诉她。我看没必要。毕竟，」他微笑道，「伊迪丝嫁我又不是图钱，她自己就相当有钱，不是吗？呃，她有钱也罢、没钱也罢，」他急忙补了一句，「都不要紧。」

===Original===
Neither of the two men spoke again for the rest of the journey, and at the corner of St. James's Street Gilbert put his friend down.

He continued his way to the little house which he had taken furnished a year before, when marriage had only seemed the remotest of possibilities, when his worldly prospects had seemed much brighter than they were at present.

Gilbert Standerton was a member of one of those peculiar families which seem to be made up entirely of nephews. His uncle, the eccentric old Anglo-Indian, had charged himself with the boy's future, and he had been mainly responsible for securing the post which Gilbert now held. More than this, he had made him his heir, and since he was a man who did nothing in secret, and was rather inclined to garrulity, the news of Gilbert's good fortune was spread from one end of England to the other.

===Chinese===
剩下的路程，两人谁也没再说话。到了圣詹姆斯街口，吉尔伯特让朋友下了车。

他继续赶路，回到那幢小宅——一年前他连家具一道租下的，那时结不结婚还像是最遥远的事，他的人生前景也比眼下明亮得多。

吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿出身于一种奇特的人家——那家人仿佛全由侄子组成。他那位久居印度、性情古怪的叔父，把孩子的前程揽在了自己身上；吉尔伯特如今担任的职位，主要就是他张罗来的。还不止如此，叔父立了他做继承人；这位老先生做事从不背着人，又生性健谈，于是吉尔伯特要走好运的消息，从英格兰这头传到了那头。

===Original===
Then, a month before this story opens, had come like a bombshell a curt notification from his relative that he had deemed it advisable to alter the terms of his will, and that Gilbert might look for no more than the thousand pounds to which, in common with innumerable other nephews, he was entitled.

It was not a shock to Gilbert except that he was a little grieved with the fear that in some manner he had offended his fiery uncle. He had a too lively appreciation of the old man's goodness to him to resent the eccentricity which would make him a comparatively poor man.

It would have considerably altered the course of his life if he had notified at least one person of the change in his prospects.

===Chinese===
谁知就在故事开篇前一个月，亲戚一封简短的通知突如其来，如同炸雷：他认为有必要更改遗嘱条款，吉尔伯特所能指望的，顶多是那一千英镑——同无数别的侄子一样，那是他名分下应得的一千镑。

这于吉尔伯特算不上打击，只是他有点难过，生怕自己不知怎么得罪了脾气火爆的叔父。老人待他的好处，他感念至深，因此对这桩要让他变得相当清贫的怪念头，并无怨怼。

倘若他把境遇的这一变故，至少告诉过一个人，他一生的轨迹都会大不相同。

## I The Amateur Safe Smasher / 一　业余破柜贼

===Original===
On the night of May 27th, 1925, the office of Gilderheim, Pascoe and Company, diamond merchants, of Little Hatton Garden, presented no unusual appearance to the patrolling constable who examined the lock and tried the door in the ordinary course of his duty. Until nine o'clock in the evening the office had been occupied by Mr. Gilderheim and his head clerk, and a plainclothes officer, whose duty it was to inquire into unusual happenings had deemed that the light in the window on the first floor fell within his scope, and had gone up to discover the reason for its appearance. The 27th was a Saturday, and it is usual for the offices in Hatton Garden to be clear of clerks and their principals by three at the latest.

===Chinese===
1925年5月27日夜里，位于小哈顿花园街的钻石商行吉尔德海姆-帕斯科公司（Gilderheim, Pascoe and Company）在巡警眼里一切如常——这位巡警照例查看了门锁、推了推店门。晚上九点之前，商行里一直有吉尔德海姆先生和他的主任文书；一位负责调查异常动静的便衣警察认定二楼窗口的那盏灯光归他过问，便上楼去查明缘由。27日是星期六，哈顿花园一带的写字间，最迟三点钟便人去楼空，这是惯例。

===Original===
Mr. Gilderheim, a pleasant gentleman, had been relieved to discover that the knock which brought him to the door, gripping a revolver in his pocket in case of accidents, produced no more startling adventure than a chat with a police officer who was known to him. He explained that he had today received a parcel of diamonds from an Amsterdam house, and was classifying the stones before leaving for the night, and with a few jocular remarks on the temptation which sixty thousand pounds' worth of diamonds offered to the unscrupulous "night of darkness," the officer left.

At nine-forty Mr. Gilderheim locked up the jewels in his big safe, before which an electric light burnt day and night, and accompanied by his clerk, left No. 93 Little Hatton Garden and walked in the direction of Holborn.

===Chinese===
吉尔德海姆先生是位和气的绅士。听到敲门声，他口袋里握着左轮手枪以备万一，走过去开门，却发现虚惊一场——门外不过是一位他熟识的警官，来聊了几句。他解释说，今天刚收到阿姆斯特丹一家商行发来的一包钻石，正赶在打烊前给石头分等级；警官打趣了几句，说这价值六万英镑的钻石，对不择手段的「黑夜行客」是多大的诱惑，随后便离开了。

九点四十分，吉尔德海姆先生把珠宝锁进那只大保险柜——柜前一盏电灯昼夜长明——随后带着文书离开小哈顿花园街93号，朝霍尔本方向走去。

===Original===
The constable on point duty bade them good night, and the plainclothes officer, who was then at the Holborn end of the thoroughfare, exchanged a word or two.

"You will be on duty all night?" asked Mr. Gilderheim as his clerk hailed a cab.

"Yes, sir," said the officer.

"Good!" said the merchant. "I'd like you to keep a special eye upon my place. I am rather nervous about leaving so large a sum in the safe."

===Chinese===
定点值勤的警察同他们道了晚安；这时正走到大街霍尔本一头的便衣警官，也同他们交谈了两句。

「你们要值一整夜的班吗？」吉尔德海姆先生问，他的文书正在叫马车。

「是的，先生。」警官说。

「好极了！」商人说，「我想请你特别照看照看我那铺子。这么大一笔钱留在保险柜里，我实在有点不放心。」

===Original===
The officer smiled.

"I don't think you need worry, sir," he said, and after the cab containing Mr. Gilderheim had driven off he walked back to No. 93.

But in that brief space of time between the diamond merchant leaving and the return of the detective many things had happened. Scarcely had Gilderheim reached the detective than two men walked briskly along the thoroughfare from the other end. Without hesitation the first turned into No. 93, opened the door with a key, and passed in. The second man followed. There was no hesitation, nothing furtive in their movements. They might have been lifelong tenants of the house, so confident were they in every action.

Not half a minute after the second man had entered a third came from the same direction, turned into the building, unlocked the door with that calm confidence which had distinguished the action of the first comer, and went in.

Three minutes later two of the three were upstairs.

===Chinese===
警官微微一笑。

「我看您不必担心，先生。」他说。等载着吉尔德海姆先生的马车驶远，他便走回93号去。

可就在钻石商离去与警官折返之间那一小会儿工夫，许多事情发生了。吉尔德海姆前脚刚走到警官跟前，两个男人便后脚从大街另一头快步走来。头一个毫不迟疑地拐进93号，用钥匙开了门，走进去。第二个跟着进去。他们的举动没有半点迟疑，没有半点鬼祟。他们简直像是在这宅子里住了一辈子的房客，每个动作都信心十足。

第二人进去不到半分钟，第三个又从同一方向走来，拐进那座楼房，带着头一位来客所特有的那种从容自信开了门锁，走了进去。

三分钟后，三人中有两个已在楼上。

===Original===
With extraordinary expedition one had produced two small iron bottles from his pockets and had deftly fixed the rubber tubes and adjusted the little blowpipe of his lamp, and the second had spread out on the floor a small kit of tools of delicate temper and beautiful finish.

Neither man spoke. They lay flat on the ground, making no attempt to extinguish the light which shone before the safe. They worked in silence for some little while, then the stouter of the two remarked, looking up at the reflector fixed at an angle to the ceiling and affording a view of the upper part of the safe to the passerby in the street below—

"Even the mirrors do not give us away, I suppose?"

===Chinese===
一个以惊人的麻利从口袋里掏出两只小铁瓶，灵巧地接上橡皮管，调好喷灯上的小吹管；另一个则在地板上摊开一小套工具，钢口精良，做工漂亮。

两人谁也不说话。他们平躺在地上，并不设法熄灭保险柜前那盏长明的灯。他们默默干了一阵，随后两人中较胖的那个开了口，抬头望着天花板上斜装的反光镜——街上过路的行人可以从镜中窥见保险柜的上半部——

「想必连这些镜子也出卖不了我们吧？」

===Original===
The second burglar was a slight, young-looking man with a shock of hair that suggested the musician.

He shook his head.

"Unless all the rules of optics have been specially reversed for the occasion," he said with just a trace of a foreign accent, "we cannot possibly be seen."

"I am relieved," said the first.

He half whistled, half hummed a little tune to himself as he plied the hissing flame to the steel door.

He was carefully burning out the lock, and had no doubt in his mind that he would succeed, for the safe was an old-fashioned one.

===Chinese===
第二个窃贼是个瘦小、显得年轻的人，一头浓密的乱发，颇有几分音乐家的派头。

他摇了摇头。

「除非光学定律今晚特意为咱们颠倒了，」他说，带着一丝外国口音，「否则绝不可能有人看得见我们。」

「那我就放心了。」头一个说。

他一边把嘶嘶作响的火焰对准钢柜门，一边半吹口哨半哼着一段小曲。

他在细心地烧熔锁芯，心里毫不怀疑自己定能得手，因为这保险柜是老式的。

===Original===
No further word was exchanged for half an hour. The man with the blowpipe continued in his work, the other watching with silent interest, ready to play his part when the operation was sufficiently advanced.

At the end of half an hour the elder of the two wiped his streaming forehead with the back of his hand, for the heat which the flame gave back from the steel door was fairly trying.

"Why did you make such a row closing the door?" he asked. "You are not usually so careless, Calli."

The other looked down at him in mild astonishment.

"I made no noise whatever, my dear George," he said. "If you had been standing in the passage you could not have heard it; in fact, I closed the door as noiselessly as I opened it."

===Chinese===
此后半个钟头，两人再没交谈一句。拿吹管的埋头干活，另一个不出声地看着，只等工程进展到火候，随时接手自己那份活计。

到半小时末了，两人中年长的那位用手背抹了抹汗涔涔的额头——钢柜门反射回来的火焰热力，着实难熬。

「关门的时候你干嘛弄出那么大动静？」他问，「你平时可没这么毛糙，卡利（Calli）。」

对方带着些许惊讶低头看他。

「我一点儿动静也没弄出来，亲爱的乔治，」他说，「你就是站在过道里也听不见；实话说，我关门跟开门一样悄没声儿。」

===Original===
The perspiring man on the ground smiled.

"That would be fairly noiseless," he said.

"Why?" asked the other.

"Because I did not close it. You walked in after me."

===Chinese===
地上的那位汗流满面，笑了。

「那可真够悄没声儿的了。」他说。

「此话怎讲？」对方问。

「因为我压根儿没关门。是你跟着我进来的。」

===Original===
Something in the silence which greeted his words made him look up. There was a puzzled look upon his companion's face.

"I opened the door with my own key," said the younger man slowly.

"You opened—" The man called George frowned. "I do not understand you, Callidino. I left the door open, and you walked in after me; I went straight up the stairs, and you followed."

Callidino looked at the other and shook his head.

===Chinese===
回应他这番话的那阵沉默有点异样，引得他抬起头来。同伴脸上是一副困惑的神情。

「门是我用自己的钥匙开的。」年轻人慢慢地说。

「你开的——」那位叫乔治的皱起了眉。「我听不懂了，卡利迪诺（Callidino）。我把门开着，你跟着我进来；我径直上楼，你也跟了上来。」

卡利迪诺望着对方，摇了摇头。

===Original===
"I opened the door myself with the key," he said quietly. "If anybody came in after you—why, it is up to us, George, to see who it is."

"You mean—?"

"I mean," said the little Italian, "that it would be extremely awkward if there is a third gentleman present on this inconvenient occasion."

"It would, indeed," said the other.

===Chinese===
「门是我自己用钥匙打开的。」他平静地说，「要真有人跟在你后头进来——那咱们俩就得去看看他是谁了，乔治。」

「你是说——？」

「我是说，」小个子意大利人说，「要是在这个不大方便的当口，还藏着第三位先生，那可就尴尬极了。」

「那可真是。」对方说。

===Original===
"Why?"

Both men turned with a start, for the voice that asked the question without any trace of emotion was the voice of a third man, and he stood in the doorway screened from all possibility of observation from the window by the angle of the room.

He was dressed in an evening suit, and he carried a light overcoat across his arm.

What manner of man he was, and how he looked, they had no means of judging, for from his chin to his forehead his face was covered by a black mask.

"Please do not move," he said, "and do not regard the revolver I am holding in the light of a menace. I merely carry it for self-defence, and you will admit that under the circumstances, and knowing the extreme delicacy of my position, I am fairly well justified in taking this precaution."

===Chinese===
「为什么？」

两人一惊，霍然转身——发出这一问的声音不带丝毫情绪，那正是第三个人的声音。他立在门口，借着房间的墙角，窗外的视线休想窥到他一丝一毫。

他穿着一身晚礼服，臂上搭着件薄大衣。

他是何等样人、长相如何，两人无从判断，因为从下巴到额头，他的整张脸都罩在黑色面罩里。

「请别动，」他说，「也别把我手里这支左轮手枪看作威胁。我带着它纯粹是为了自卫。你们得承认，眼下的情形，又考虑到我这处境微妙之极，我采取这点预防措施是完全说得过去的。」

===Original===
George Wallis laughed a little under his breath.

"Sir," he said, without shifting his position, "you may be a man after my own heart, but I shall know better when you have told me exactly what you want."

"I want to learn," said the stranger.

He stood there regarding the pair with obvious interest. The eyes which shone through the holes of the mask were alive and keen.

"Go on with your work, please," he said. "I should hate to interrupt you."

===Chinese===
乔治·沃利斯低声笑了。

「先生，」他说，身子纹丝没动，「您也许正合我的心意，不过等您说明白了到底要什么，我再下断语不迟。」

「我想讨教。」陌生人说。

他站在那儿，饶有兴致地打量这一对。面罩开孔里露出的那双眼睛，灵动而锐利。

「请继续干活，」他说，「我可不想搅了你们。」

===Original===
George Wallis picked up the blowpipe and addressed himself again to the safe door. He was a most adaptable man, and the situation in which he found himself nonplussed had yet to occur.

"Since," he said, "it makes absolutely no difference as to whether I leave off or whether I go on, if you are a representative of law and order, I may as well go on, because if you are not a representative of those two admirable, excellent and necessary qualities I might at least save half the swag with you."

"You may save the lot," said the man sharply. "I do not wish to share the proceeds of your robbery, but I want to know how you do it—that is all."

"You shall learn," said George Wallis, that most notorious of burglars, "and at the hands of an expert, I beg you to believe."

"That I know," said the other calmly.

===Chinese===
乔治·沃利斯拾起吹管，重新对付保险柜门。他是个极其随机应变的人，能叫他束手无策的场面，至今还没有出现过。

「既然，」他说，「我是住手还是接着干，结果毫无分别——您若是法律与秩序的代表，我不妨接着干；您若不是那两样可敬、可贵又不可或缺之品质的代表，我说不定还能跟您合伙保下一半赃物。」

「全数奉送。」那人厉声道，「我不打算分你们盗窃的赃款，我只想知道你们是怎么干的——仅此而已。」

「包您学到手，」窃贼中声名最著的乔治·沃利斯说，「而且是在行家手里学——这一点请您相信。」

「这我知道。」对方平静地说。

===Original===
Wallis went on with his task apparently undisturbed by this extraordinary interruption. The little Italian's hands had twitched nervously, and here might have been trouble, but the strength of the other man, who was evidently the leader of the two, and his self-possession had heartened his companion to accept whatever consequences the presence of this man might threaten. It was the masked stranger who broke the silence.

"Isn't it an extraordinary thing," he said, "that whilst technical schools exist for teaching every kind of trade, art and craft, there is none which engage in teaching the art of destruction. Believe me, I am very grateful that I have had this opportunity of sitting at the feet of a master."

His voice was not unpleasant, but there was a certain hardness which was not in harmony with the flippant tone he adopted.

The man on the floor went on with his work for a little while, then he said without turning his head—

===Chinese===
沃利斯继续干他的活，对这场离奇的搅局似乎无动于衷。小个子意大利人的双手紧张地抽动了一下，眼看要出乱子，但同伴——显然是两人中的头儿——的力量和沉着给他壮了胆，让他接受了此人光临可能带来的任何后果。打破沉默的是那位蒙面陌生人。

「岂不奇怪，」他说，「世上有的是传授百工技艺的专门学校，却没有一所开设破坏的艺术这门课。请相信，能有这么个机会列于大师门下受教，我感激得很。」

他的嗓音并不难听，但其中带着某种冷硬，同他那轻佻的口气并不相称。

地上那人又干了一阵活，随后头也不回地说——

===Original===
"I am anxious to know exactly how you got in."

"I followed close behind you," said the masked man. "I knew there would be a reasonable interval between the two of you. You see," he went on, "you have been watching this office for the greater part of a week; one of you has been on duty practically every night. You rented a small office higher up this street which offered a view of these premises. I gathered that you had chosen tonight because you brought your gas with you this morning. You were waiting in the dark hallway of the building in which your office is situated one of you watching for the light to go out and Mr. Gilderheim depart. When he had gone, you, sir"—he addressed the man on the floor—"came out immediately, your companion did not follow so soon. Moreover, he stopped to pick up a small bundle of letters which had apparently been dropped by some careless person, and since these letters included two sealed packets such as the merchants of Hatton Garden send to their clients. I was able to escape the observation of the second man and keep reasonably close to you."

Callidino laughed softly.

"That is true," he said, with a nod to the man on the floor. "It was very clever. I suppose you dropped the packet?"

The masked man inclined his head.

"Please go on," he said, "do not let me interrupt you."

===Chinese===
「我倒急着想知道，您究竟是怎么进来的。」

「我紧跟在您后面进来的。」蒙面人说，「我料定你们两位之间会隔出一段合理的时间差。您瞧，」他接着说，「你们监视这间商行已有大半个星期，几乎每夜都有一人值班。你们在这条街更靠上的位置租了间小写字间，正好望得见这座铺面。我由此断定你们选定了今晚下手，因为今天早上你们把煤气瓶带来了。你们守在写字间那座楼黑黢黢的门厅里，其中一人盯着灯熄、等吉尔德海姆先生离开。他一走，您，先生，」他对地上那人说道，「随即就出来了，您的同伴却没跟得那么紧。再说，他还停下来捡一小捆信——显然是哪个粗心人失落的——那捆信里还有两只封好的纸包，正是哈顿花园的商行寄给主顾的那种。我这才得以躲过第二位的眼睛，同您保持相当近的距离。」

卡利迪诺轻轻笑了。

「确实如此，」他朝地上那人点点头，「真高明。那包东西想必是您丢的？」

蒙面人微微颔首。

「请继续，」他说，「别让我搅了你们。」

===Original===
"What is going to happen when I have finished?" asked George, still keeping his face to the safe.

"As far as I am concerned, nothing. Just as soon as you have got through your work, and have extracted whatever booty there is to be extracted, I shall retire."

"You want your share, I suppose?"

"Not at all," said the other calmly. "I do not want my share by any means. I am not entitled to it. My position in society prevents me from going farther down the slippery path than to connive at your larceny."

"Felony," corrected the man on the floor.

"Felony," agreed the other.

===Chinese===
「等我干完了，会有什么事吗？」乔治问，脸仍朝着保险柜。

「就我而言，什么也不会有。等你们干完活，捞完该捞的油水，我自会退场。」

「您总归要一份吧，我猜？」

「完全不必。」对方平静地说，「我丝毫也不想分一份。我没有那份权利。我的社会地位不容许我在下坡路上走得太远——纵容你们行窃，已是极限。」

「是重罪。」地上那人纠正道。

「是重罪。」对方表示同意。

===Original===
He waited until without a sound the heavy door of the safe swung open and George had put his hand inside to extract the contents, and then, without a word, he passed through the door, closing it behind him.

The two men sat up tensely and listened. They heard nothing more until the soft thud of the outer door told them that their remarkable visitor had departed.

They exchanged glances—interest on the one face, amusement on the other.

"That is a remarkable man," said Callidino.

The other nodded.

"Most remarkable," he said, "and more remarkable will it be if we get out of Hatton Garden tonight with the loot."

It would seem that the "more than most" remarkable happening of all actually occurred, for none saw the jewel thieves go, and the smashing of Gilderheim's jewel safe provided an excellent alternative topic for conversation to the prospect of Sunstar for the Derby.

===Chinese===
他一直等到沉重的柜门悄无声息地敞开、乔治伸手进去取里面的东西，这才一言不发地穿过房门，随手把门带上。

两人紧张地坐起身，侧耳细听。直到临街大门轻轻一响，他们才知道，这位非同寻常的访客已经走了；此前再没听见任何动静。

两人交换了个眼色——一张脸上是兴味，另一张上是笑意。

「真是个了不得的人物。」卡利迪诺说。

对方点了点头。

「了不得极了，」他说，「不过更了不得的，还得数咱们今晚带着赃物全身退出哈顿花园。」

看来那件「比了不得更了不得」的事当真发生了——竟没有一个人瞧见珠宝窃贼们离去；于是，比起太阳星角逐德比大赛的前景，吉尔德海姆珠宝保险柜被砸一案，成了一样顶好的谈资。

## VII The Bank Smasher / 七　破库大盗

===Original===
Three men sat in the inner room of a City office. The outer door was locked, the door communicating between the outer office and the sanctum was wide open.

The men sat at a table, discussing a frugal lunch which had been brought in from a restaurant nearby, and talking together in low tones.

===Chinese===
三个人坐在伦敦城一间写字间的里屋。外门上了锁，连通外间办公室与这间内室的门却敞开着。

三人围桌而坐，一边吃着附近餐馆送来的一顿简便午餐，一边低声交谈。

===Original===
George Wallis, who spoke with such authority as to suggest that he held a leading position above and before the others, was a man of forty, inclined a little to stoutness, of middle height, and with no distinguishing features save the short bristling moustache and the jet-black eyebrows which gave his face a somewhat sinister appearance. His eyes were tired and lazy, his square jaw bespoke immense determination, and the hands which played idly with a pen were small but strong; they were the hands of an artist; and indeed George Wallis, under one name or another, was known as an artist in his particular profession in every police bureau on the Continent.

===Chinese===
乔治·沃利斯说起话来派头十足，俨然是凌驾于其余二人之上的头领。他四十岁上下，身材微微发福，中等个子，除了那撮又短又硬的小胡子和两道乌黑的浓眉——它们给他的脸平添几分阴鸷——之外，并无特别出奇之处。他的眼睛透着倦怠和懒散，方正的下巴显示出极大的决断，正漫不经心摆弄一支钢笔的那双手，小而有力；那是一双艺术家的手。的确，乔治·沃利斯——不管他化名什么——在欧洲大陆每一处警察局里，都被视为他那一行当里的一位艺术家。

===Original===
Callidino, the little Italian at his side, was neat and dapper. His hair was rather long, he suggested rather the musical enthusiast than the cool-headed man of business. And yet this dapper Italian was known as the most practical of the remarkable trio which for many years had been the terror of every bank president in France.

The third was Persh, a stout man with a pleasant, florid face, and a trim cavalry moustache, who, despite his bulk, was a man of extraordinary agility, and his escape from Devil's Island and his subsequent voyage to Australia in an open boat will be fresh in the minds of the average newspaper readers.

===Chinese===
坐在他身旁的卡利迪诺，就是那个小个子意大利人，干净利落，衣冠楚楚。他的头发留得颇长，看上去与其说是头脑冷静的生意人，不如说更像个音乐发烧友。然而正是这个衣冠楚楚的意大利人，在那声名赫赫的三人组里素有最务实之名——多年来，这个三人组是全法兰西每一位银行总裁的噩梦。

第三个是佩什（Persh），胖子一个，面色红润讨喜，唇上留着修剪齐整的骑兵式小胡子。别看块头大，他的身手却出奇地矫健；他从魔鬼岛（Devil's Island）脱身、随后乘一条敞舱船渡海去澳大利亚的壮举，普通读者想必还在报纸上记忆犹新。

===Original===
They made no disguise as to their identities, they did not evade the frank questioning which was their lot when the City Police smelt them out and came in to investigate the affairs of this "outside brokers'" establishment. The members of the City force were a little disappointed to discover that quite a legitimate business was being done. You cannot quarrel even with convicted bank robbers if they choose to get their living by any way which, however much discredited, is within the law; and beyond warning those of their clients with whom they could get in touch that the heads of this remarkable business were notorious criminals, the police must needs sit by and watch, satisfied that sooner or later the men would make a slip that would bring them within the scope of police action.

===Chinese===
他们对自己的身份不加掩饰；伦敦市警察嗅出他们的踪迹、进来查账时，他们也坦然接受直来直去的盘问。市警队的弟兄们有点失望地发现，这买卖做得规规矩矩、完全合法。就算是定过案的银行劫匪，只要他们选择的谋生之路虽名声不佳却终究在法律之内，你也拿他们没办法。警方充其量只能知会那些联系得上的主顾：这家奇特买卖的头面人物是臭名昭著的罪犯；除此之外，只好坐在一旁盯着，笃信这些人迟早失手一回，落入警方的法网。

===Original===
"And they will have to wait a jolly long time," said Wallis.

He looked round his "Board" with an amused smile.

"Have they been in today?" asked Callidino.

"They have been in today," said Wallis gravely. "They have searched our books and our desks and our clothes, and even the legs of our office stools."

"An indelicate proceeding," said Persh cheerfully.

===Chinese===
「那他们可有得等了。」沃利斯说。

他带着戏谑的笑意环视自己的「董事会」。

「他们今天来过了？」卡利迪诺问。

「今天来过了。」沃利斯一本正经地说，「他们搜了我们的账簿、书桌和衣裳，连办公凳的凳子腿都没放过。」

「真不讲究。」佩什乐呵呵地说。

===Original===
"And what did they find, George?"

George smiled.

"They found all there was to be found," he said.

"I suppose it was the burglary at the Bond Guarantees that I have been reading about that's excited them," said the Italian coolly.

"I suppose so," said Wallis, with grave indifference. "It is pretty terrible to have names such as we possess. Seriously," he went on, "I am not very much afraid of the police, even suppose there was anything to find. I haven't met one of them who has the intelligence of that cool devil we met at the Foreign Office, when I had to answer some questions about Persh's unique experiences on Devil's Island."

===Chinese===
「那他们找到什么了，乔治？」

乔治笑了笑。

「该找着的，全让他们找着了。」他说。

「我猜，是我在报上读到的那桩邦德担保公司失窃案把他们惹毛了。」意大利人淡淡地说。

「我猜也是。」沃利斯说，神情郑重而全不在乎，「顶着咱们这样的名头，可真够呛。说正经的，」他接着说，「我不怎么怕警察，哪怕真有什么可找的。他们里头我就没见过一个，脑力赶得上咱们在外交部碰见的那个冷静的魔鬼——那回我得就佩什在魔鬼岛上那段独一无二的经历回答几个问题。」

===Original===
"What was his name?" asked Persh, interested.

"Something associated in my mind with South Africa—oh, yes, Standerton. A cool beast—I met him at Epsom the other day," said Wallis. "He's lost in a place like the Foreign Office. Do you remember that quick run through he gave me, Persh?"

The other nodded.

"Before I knew where I was I admitted that I'd been in Huntingdonshire the same week as Lady Perkinton's jewels were taken. If he'd had another five minutes I guess he'd have known"—he lowered his voice to little more than a whisper—"all this hidden treasure which the English police are seeking was cached."

The men laughed as at some great joke.

===Chinese===
「他叫什么来着？」佩什来了兴致。

「一个让我联想到南非的名字——哦，对了，斯坦德顿。冷静的畜生——前几天我在埃普索姆还碰见他了。」沃利斯说，「他埋没在外交部那种地方，可惜了。佩什，你还记得他把我那一通飞快的盘问吧？」

对方点了点头。

「我还没回过神来，就承认了自己在珀金顿夫人（Lady Perkinton）珠宝失窃的那个星期正好在亨廷登郡。再给他五分钟，我估摸着他连——」他把声音压低到近乎耳语，「——英国警察正在搜寻的这批秘藏财宝藏在哪儿，都要知道了。」

三人大笑，仿佛听了个天大的笑话。

===Original===
"Talking of cool people," said Wallis, "do you recall that weird devil who held us up in Hatton Garden?"

"Have you found him?" asked Callidino.

George shook his head.

"No," he said slowly, "only I'm rather afraid of him."

Which was a remarkable confession for him to make. He changed the subject abruptly.

===Chinese===
「说起冷静的人，」沃利斯说，「你们还记得哈顿花园那个劫住咱们的古怪家伙吗？」

「你们找到他了？」卡利迪诺问。

乔治摇了摇头。

「没有，」他慢慢地说，「只是，我有点怕他。」

这话出自他之口，可是句了不起的供认。他猛地岔开了话题。

===Original===
"I suppose you people know," said Wallis, "that the police are particularly active just now? I've reason to be aware of the fact, because they have just concluded a most exhaustive search of my private belongings."

He did not exaggerate. The police were, indeed, most eager for some clue to associate these three known criminals with the acts of the past month.

Half an hour later Wallis left the building. He paused in the entrance hall of the big block of offices, lighted a cigar with an air that betokened his peace with the world and his approval of humanity.

===Chinese===
「你们几位想必知道，」沃利斯说，「眼下警察特别来劲？这事儿我有切身体会，因为他们刚把我的私人物品翻了个底朝天。」

他并未夸大。警方确实急红了眼，巴不得找出一条线索，把这三位挂了号的罪犯同过去一个月的案子挂上钩。

半小时后，沃利斯离开了这座楼。他在写字楼大堂里驻了驻足，点上一支雪茄，那副神气，透着他对世界的和睦、对人类的首肯。

===Original===
As his foot touched the pavement a tall man stepped to his side. Wallis looked up quickly and gave a little nod.

"I want you," said the tall man coldly.

"Do you indeed?" said Wallis with exaggerated interest. "And what may you want with me?"

"You come along with me, and not so much of your lip," said the man.

He called a cab, and the two men were rapidly driven to the nearest City police station. Wallis continued smoking his cigar, without any outward indication of apprehension. He would have chatted very gaily with the officer who had effected his arrest, but the officer himself was in no mood for light humour.

===Chinese===
他一脚刚踏上人行道，一个高个子男人便跨到他身旁。沃利斯迅速抬眼一瞥，微微点了点头。

「跟我走一趟。」高个子冷冷地说。

「是吗？」沃利斯做出一副大感兴趣的样子，「您找我又有何贵干？」

「跟我走，少贫嘴。」那人说。

他叫来一辆马车，两人很快被拉到最近的伦敦市警察分局。沃利斯照旧抽着他的雪茄，外表看不出一丝慌张。他本想同逮捕他的那位警官谈笑风生，可对方本人没那份闲情雅趣。

===Original===
He was hustled into the charge room and brought before the inspector's desk.

That officer looked up with a nod. He was more genial than his captor.

"Well, Wallis," he said with a smile, "we want some information from you."

"You always want information from somebody," said the man with cold insolence. "Have you had another burglary?"

The inspector nodded.

"Tut, tut!" said the prisoner with an affectation of distress, "how very annoying for you Mr. Whitling. I suppose you have got the culprit?" he asked blandly.

===Chinese===
他被推进登记室，带到探长的写字台前。

那位探长抬头点点头，比抓人的那位和气些。

「好啊，沃利斯，」他微笑道，「我们想从你这儿打听点事。」

「你们总想从什么人嘴里打听点什么。」那人冷冷地、带着傲慢说，「又失窃了？」

探长点了点头。

「哎呀呀！」被拘者装出一副痛心状，「这可真让您烦心了，惠特林先生。想必您已经抓到案犯了？」他懒洋洋地问。

===Original===
"I've got you at present," said the calm inspector. "I should not be surprised if I had also got the culprit. Can you explain where you were last night?"

"With the greatest of pleasure," said Wallis; "I was dining with a friend."

"His name?"

The other shrugged his shoulders. "His name is immaterial. I was dining with a friend whose name does not matter. Put that down, inspector."

"And where were you dining with this unknown friend?" asked the imperturbable official.

===Chinese===
「眼下我先拿到了你。」探长不动声色地说，「要是连案犯也一并拿到手，我不会吃惊。你能说说昨晚在哪儿吗？」

「乐意之至，」沃利斯说，「我在同一位朋友吃晚饭。」

「他叫什么？」

对方耸了耸肩。「他的名字无关紧要。我在同一位朋友吃晚饭，那位朋友的名字不值一提。就这么记下吧，探长先生。」

「同这位无名朋友吃饭，又在哪儿吃的呢？」这位不动声色的官员问。

===Original===
Wallis named a restaurant in Wardour Street.

"At what hour were you dining?" asked the inspector patiently.

"Between the hours of eight and eleven," said the man, "as the proprietor of the restaurant will testify."

The inspector smiled to himself. He knew the restaurant and knew the proprietor. His testimony would not carry a great deal of weight with a jury.

"Have you anybody respectable," he asked, "who will vouch for the fact that you were there, other than your unknown friend and Signor Villimicci?"

===Chinese===
沃利斯报了沃德街上的一家餐馆。

「几点用的饭？」探长耐心地问。

「八点到十一点之间，」那人说，「餐馆老板可以作证。」

探长暗自一笑。那家餐馆他知道，老板他也知道。这种证词在陪审团跟前没多少分量。

「除了那位无名朋友和维利米奇先生（Signor Villimicci），」他问，「你还有什么体面人，能证明你确实在那儿？」

===Original===
Wallis nodded.

"I might name, with due respect," he said, "Sergeant Colebrook, of the Central Investigation Department of Scotland Yard."

He was annoyingly bland. The inspector looked up sharply.

"Is he going to vouch for you?" he asked.

"He was watching me the whole of the time, disguised, I think, as a gentleman. At least, he was in evening dress, and he was quite different from the waiters. You see, he was sitting down."

===Chinese===
沃利斯点了点头。

「我倒可以恭恭敬敬地报出一个名字，」他说，「苏格兰场刑事部的科尔布鲁克巡佐（Sergeant Colebrook）。」

他那副温文尔雅实在气人。探长猛地抬起头来。

「他会替你作证？」他问。

「他一直盯着我呢，我想他是化装成了一位绅士。至少，他穿着燕尾服，同那些侍者全然两样。您瞧，他是坐着的。」

===Original===
"I see," said the inspector. He put down his pen.

"It was rather amusing to be watched by a real detective-sergeant, from that most awe-inspiring wilderness of bricks," the man continued. "I quite liked it, though I am afraid the poor fellow was bored sooner than I was."

"I understand," said the inspector, "that you were being watched from eight o'clock last night till—?"

He paused inquiringly.

"Till near midnight, I should imagine. Until our dress-suited detective, looking tragically like a detective all the time, had escorted me to the front door of my flat."

===Chinese===
「我明白了。」探长说着，放下了笔。

「叫一位如假包换的侦探警佐盯着——还是从那座最令人生畏的砖石迷宫里派出来的——倒也挺有意思。」那人接着说，「我相当受用，只是恐怕那位可怜的老兄比我先腻烦了。」

「我了解到，」探长说，「从昨晚八点起，就一直有人盯着你，盯到——？」

他询问似的顿了顿。

「我估摸着，到将近午夜。直到咱们那位燕尾服侦探——从头到尾一副活脱脱的侦探相，惨不忍睹——把我一路护送到我公寓的大门。」

===Original===
"I can verify that in a minute," said the inspector. "Go into the parade room."

Wallis strolled unconcernedly into the inner room whilst the inspector manipulated the telephone.

In five minutes the prisoner was sent for.

"You're all right," said the inspector. "Clean bill for you, Wallis."

"I am glad to hear it," said Wallis. "Very relieved indeed!" He sighed heavily. "Now that I am embarked upon what I might term a legalised form of thefts from the public, it is especially pleasing to me to know that my actions are approved by the police."

===Chinese===
「这个我一分钟就能核实。」探长说，「到集合室去等着。」

沃利斯若无其事地踱进里间，探长则操起了电话。

五分钟后，人把被拘者叫了回去。

「你没事了。」探长说，「给你开了张清白证明，沃利斯。」

「听你这么说我真高兴，」沃利斯说，「实在如释重负！」他长叹一声，「如今我干起了这门可以称之为『合法化公盗』的营生，得知自己的行为得到警方的嘉许，尤其令人快慰。」

===Original===
"We don't approve of everything you do," said the inspector.

He was an annoying man. Wallis thought; he would neither lose his temper nor be rude.

"You can go now—sorry to have bothered you."

"Don't mention it," said the polite man with a little bow.

"By the way, before you go," said the inspector, "just come into my inner office, will you?"

===Chinese===
「你干的每件事我们可并不都嘉许。」探长说。

这是个气人的角色，沃利斯想；他既不发火，也不失礼。

「你可以走了——打扰了，抱歉。」

「不敢当。」这位客气的人微微一躬身。

「对了，走之前，」探长说，「到我里间办公室来一趟，行吗？」

===Original===
Wallis followed him. The inspector closed the door behind them. They were alone.

"Wallis, do you know there is a reward of some twelve thousand pounds for the detection of the men engaged in these burglaries?"

"You surprise me," said Mr. Wallis, lifting his eyebrows.

"I don't surprise you," said the inspector; "in fact, you know much more about it than I do. And I tell you this, that we are prepared to go to any lengths to track this gang, or, at any rate, to put an end to its operations. Look here, George," he tapped the other on the chest with his strong, gnarled finger, "is it a scream?"

===Chinese===
沃利斯跟着他进去。探长随手关上门。屋里只剩他们两个。

「沃利斯，你可知道，侦破这几桩盗窃案，悬赏足足有一万两千英镑？」

「您真让我吃惊。」沃利斯先生挑起眉毛。

「我才惊不着你呢，」探长说，「说实在的，这事你知道得比我多得多。我告诉你：为了追查这伙人，我们什么手段都使得出来——至少，也要叫他们就此歇业。听我说，乔治，」他用粗壮多节的手指点点对方的胸口，「肯不肯『吱声』？」

===Original===
"A scream?" Mr. Wallis was puzzled innocence itself.

"Will you turn King's evidence?" said the other shortly.

"I should be most happy," said Wallis, with a helpless shrug, "but how can I turn King's evidence about a matter on which I am absolutely uninformed? The reward is monstrously tempting. If I had companions in crime I should need very little persuading. My conscience is a matter of constant adjustment. It is rather like the foot-rule which shoemakers employ to measure their customers' feet—terrifically adjustable. It has a sliding scale which goes up and down."

"I don't want to hear any more about your conscience," said the officer wearily. "Do you scream or don't you?"

"I don't scream," said Mr. Wallis emphatically.

===Chinese===
「吱声？」沃利斯先生一副全然不解的无辜相。

「你肯不肯转做国王的证人，供出同伙？」对方直截了当地说。

「我是求之不得，」沃利斯无奈地耸耸肩，「可对一件我一无所知的事，我怎么转做国王的证人？那笔悬赏诱人得要命。要真有同伙的话，怕是费不了几句口舌我就从了。我的良心是件随时调整的东西，很像鞋匠量客人脚用的那把折尺——灵便得很，上上下下有个滑动的刻度。」

「你的良心我不听了。」探长倦倦地说，「你到底吱不吱声？」

「我不吱声。」沃利斯先生斩钉截铁地说。

===Original===
The inspector jerked his head sideways, and with the bow which the invitation had interrupted, Mr. Wallis walked out into the street.

He knew, no one better, how completely every action of his was watched. He knew, even as he left the station, that the seemingly idle loafer on the corner of the street had picked him up, would follow him until he handed him over to yet another plainclothes officer for observation. From beat to beat, from one end of the City to another, those vigilant eyes would never leave him; whilst he slept, the door, back and front of his lodging would be watched. He could not move without all London—all the London that mattered as far as he was concerned—knowing everything about that move.

His home was the upper part of a house over a tobacconist's in a small street off Charing Cross Road. And to his maisonette he made a leisurely way, not hastening his steps any the more because he knew that on one side of the street an innocent commercial traveller, and on the other a sandwich man apparently trudging homeward with his board, were keeping him under observation. He stopped to buy some cigars in the Charing Cross Road, crossed near the Alhambra, and ten minutes later was unlocking the door of the narrow passage which ran by the side of the shop, and gave him private access to the suite above.

===Chinese===
探长朝旁边一摆头；沃利斯先生补完刚才被打断的那一躬，走出门去，上了大街。

他的每一举动都处于何等严密的监视之下，这他比谁都清楚。就在踏出警局的那一刻，他知道街角那个貌似游手好闲的闲汉已经咬上了他，会一路跟到底，直到把他移交给下一位盯梢的便衣。一段巡区接一段巡区，从伦敦城这头到那头，那些警觉的眼睛绝不离开他；他睡下的时候，寓所的前门后门都会有人把守。他动一动，整个伦敦——凡是他看得上眼的那个伦敦——就知道这一动的全部底细。

他的家在查令十字路旁一条小巷里，一家烟店楼上的部分。他悠哉游哉地朝他那套复式小公寓走去，并不因为知道街这边一个无辜的行商、街那边一个背着广告牌貌似回家去的挂牌广告人在监视他，就加快半分脚步。他在查令十字路停下买了些雪茄，在阿罕布拉剧院附近过了街，十分钟后便打开了店铺旁边那条窄过道的门——由那儿，他可以径自上楼，进他楼上的那套房间。

===Original===
It was a room comfortably furnished and giving evidence of some taste. Large divan chairs formed a feature of the furnishing, and the prints, though few were interesting by reason of their obvious rarity.

He did not trouble to make an examination of the room, or of the remainder of the maisonette he rented. If the police had been, they had been. If they had not, it did not matter. They could find nothing. He had a good conscience, so far as a man's conscience may be good who fears less for the consequence of his deeds than for the apparent, the obvious and the discoverable consequences.

He rang a bell, and after a little delay an old woman answered the call.

"Make me some tea, Mrs. Skard," he said. "Has anybody called?"

===Chinese===
屋里陈设舒适，看得出几分品味。几张宽大的沙发椅是陈设的特色；版画虽少，却因显然珍稀而耐人寻味。

他懒得检查这间屋子，也懒得检查租下的其余部分。警察来搜过，便搜过；没来，也无所谓。他们什么也找不到。他的良心是安宁的——当然，这安宁只是对这样一种人而言：他怕的不是自己所作所为的后果，而只是那看得见的、明显的、查得出的后果。

他按了铃，过了片刻，一个老太太应声前来。

「给我沏点茶，斯卡德太太（Mrs. Skard），」他说，「有人来过吗？」

===Original===
The old woman looked up to the ceiling for inspiration.

"Only the man about the gas," she said.

"Only the man about the gas," repeated George Wallis admiringly. "Wasn't he awfully surprised to find that we didn't have gas at all?"

The old lady looked at him in some amazement.

"He did say he had come to see about the gas," she said, "and then when he found we had no gas he said 'electricity'—a most absentminded young man."

===Chinese===
老太太望着天花板寻找灵感。

「只有个查煤气的。」她说。

「只有个查煤气的。」乔治·沃利斯佩服地重复道，「发现咱们根本不通煤气，他没吓一大跳吧？」

老太太一脸惊讶地看着他。

「他确实说他是来查煤气的，」她说，「后来发现咱们没有煤气，他又说『电』——真是个心不在焉的年轻人。」

===Original===
"They are that way, Mrs. Skard," said her master tolerantly; "they fall in love, don't you know, round about this season of the year, and when their minds become occupied with other and more pleasant thoughts than gas mantles and incandescent lights they become a little confused. I suppose he did not bother you—he told you you need not wait?" he suggested.

"Quite right, sir," said Mrs. Skard. "He said he would do all he had to do without assistance."

"And I will bet you he did it," said George Wallis with boisterous good humour.

Undisturbed by the knowledge that his rooms had been searched by an industrious detective, he sat for an hour reading an American magazine. At six o'clock a taxicab drove into the street and pulled up before the entrance of his flat. The driver, a stoutish man with a beard, looked helplessly up and down seeking a number, and one of the two detectives who had been keeping observation on the house walked across the road casually towards him.

===Chinese===
「他们就是这个样子，斯卡德太太，」她的主人宽容地说，「你可知道，每年一到这个时节，他们就要谈恋爱；心思一旦被煤气罩和白炽灯以外那些更愉快的念头占了，人就有点儿犯迷糊。我想他没有为难您吧——他叫您不必等着？」他提了一句。

「一点儿不错，先生，」斯卡德太太说，「他说他要做的事，不用人帮衬。」

「我敢跟你打赌，他都办妥了。」乔治·沃利斯兴高采烈地说。

明知自己的房间已被某位勤快的侦探搜查过，他也不动声色，坐着读了一小时美国杂志。六点钟，一辆出租汽车开进街来，停在他公寓的入口前。司机是个蓄胡子的胖大汉，正没头没脑地朝两头张望找门牌，监视这宅子的两名侦探之一便装出漫不经心的样子，穿过马路朝他走去。

===Original===
"Do you want to find a number, mate?" he asked.

"I want No. 43," said the cabman.

"That's it," said the officer.

He saw the cabman ring, and having observed that he entered the door, which was closed behind him, he walked back to his co-worker.

"George is going to take a little taxi drive," he said; "we will see where he goes."

===Chinese===
「找门牌吗，伙计？」他问。

「我找43号。」司机说。

「就是这儿。」警探说。

他看着司机按铃，又瞧着他进了门、门在他身后关上，这才走回同伴身边。

「乔治要坐出租汽车兜一小圈了，」他说，「咱们看看他去哪儿。」

===Original===
The man who had waited on the other side of the road nodded.

"I don't suppose he will go anywhere worth following, but I have the car waiting round the corner."

"I'll car him," said the second man bitterly. "Did you hear what he told Inspector Whitling of the City Police about me last night?"

The first detective was considerably interested.

"No, I should like to hear."

"Well," began the man, and then thought better of it. It was nothing to his credit that he should keep a man under observation three hours, and that the quarry should be aware all the time that he was being watched.

===Chinese===
守在街对面的那人点了点头。

「我料他去不了什么值得跟的地方，不过车我已经备在拐角了。」

「我倒要车他一程。」第二个人恨恨地说，「他昨晚跟伦敦市警察的惠特林探长说我什么，你听说了吗？」

头一个侦探顿时来了兴致。

「没有，我倒想听听。」

「这个嘛，」那人开了头，转念又觉得不妥。盯一个人盯了三个钟头，对方却从头到尾明知自己被盯——这实在算不上什么露脸的事。

===Original===
"Hullo!" he said as the door of No. 43 opened, "here is our man."

He threw a swift glance along the street, and saw that the hired motorcar which had been provided for his use was waiting.

"Here he comes," he said, but it was not the man he expected. The bearded chauffeur came out alone, waved a farewell to somebody in the hallway whom they could not see, and having started his engine with great deliberation, got upon his seat, and the taxicab moved slowly away.

"George is not going," said the detective. "That means that we shall have to stay here for another two or three hours—there is his light."

===Chinese===
「哎！」43号的门开了，他说，「咱们的人来了。」

他飞快地朝街两头扫了一眼，看见给他备用的那辆租来的汽车正候着。

「他出来了。」他说——可出来的不是他等的那位。络腮胡司机独自走了出来，朝门厅里某个他们看不见的人挥了挥手，不慌不忙地发动引擎，登上驾驶座，出租汽车缓缓驶走了。

「乔治不去了。」侦探说，「这就是说，咱们还得在这儿守上两三个钟头——他的灯亮了。」

===Original===
For four long hours they kept their vigil, and never once was a pair of eyes taken from the only door through which George Wallis could make his exit. There was no other way by which he could leave, of that they were assured.

Behind the house was a high wall, and unless the man was working in collusion with half the respectable householders, not only in that street but of Charing Cross Road, he could not by any possible chance leave his flat.

At half-past ten the taxicab they had seen drove back to the door of the flat, and the driver was admitted. He evidently did not expect to stay long, for he did not switch off his engine; as a matter of fact, he was not absent from his car longer than thirty seconds. He came back almost immediately, climbed up on to his seat and drove away.

===Chinese===
漫长的四个钟头，他们一直守着，一双眼睛始终没有离开乔治·沃利斯唯一能够脱身的那个门。其余的路是没有的——这一点他们笃信无疑。

屋后是一道高墙；除非此人买通了好几十个体面的住户——不但在那条街上，还包括查令十字路的——否则他绝无可能离开公寓。

十点半，他们见过的那辆出租汽车驶回公寓门口，司机进去了。他显然没打算久留，因为引擎始终没熄火；事实上，他离开车子不超过三十秒。他几乎立刻就出来了，爬上驾驶座，把车开走。

===Original===
"I wonder what the game is?" asked the detective, a little puzzled.

"He has been to take a message somewhere," said the other. "I think we ought to have found out."

Ten minutes later Inspector Goldberg, of Scotland Yard, drove into the street and sprang from his car opposite the men.

"Has Wallis returned?" he asked quickly.

"Returned!" repeated the puzzled detective, "he has not gone out yet."

===Chinese===
「这是玩的哪一出？」侦探有点纳闷地问。

「他是进去捎了个信儿。」另一人说，「咱们本该查个明白。」

十分钟后，苏格兰场的戈德堡探长（Inspector Goldberg）驱车驶进街来，在两人对面跳下车。

「沃利斯回来了吗？」他急急地问。

「回来？」困惑的侦探重复道，「他还没出去过呢。」

===Original===
"Has not gone out?" repeated the inspector with a gasp. "A man answering to his description was seen leaving the City branch of the Goldsmiths' Guild half an hour ago. The safe has been forced and twenty thousand pounds' worth of jewelry has been taken."

There was a little silence.

"Well, sir," said the subordinate doggedly, "one thing I will swear, and it is that George Wallis has not left this house tonight."

"That's true, sir," said the second man. "The sergeant and I have not left this place since Wallis went in."

===Chinese===
「还没出去？」探长倒抽一口气，「半小时前，有人看见一个同他相貌相符的男子，走出金匠公会的伦敦城分行。保险柜被撬了，价值两万英镑的珠宝被劫走。」

一时无人说话。

「可是，长官，」部下执拗地说，「有一件事我可以起誓：乔治·沃利斯今晚没有离开过这座房子。」

「千真万确，长官，」第二个人说，「从沃利斯进去起，我和巡佐就没离开过这地方。」

===Original===
"But," said the bewildered detective-inspector, "it must be Wallis, no other man could have done the job as he did it."

"It could not have been, sir," persisted the watcher.

"Then who in the name of Heaven did the job?" snapped the inspector.

His underlings wisely offered no solution.

===Chinese===
「可是，」困惑的探长说，「必定是沃利斯，那活儿干得那么地道，旁人做不到。」

「不可能是他，长官。」盯梢的人咬定。

「那到底是谁，看在老天的份上，干的这票活？」探长厉声道。

他的部下们很识相，没有提出任何解释。

## XI The Fourth Man / XI 第四个人

===Original===
On the night of Gilbert Standerton’s little dinner party the black-bearded taxi driver, who had called at the house off Charing Cross Road for instructions, came to the door of No. 43, and was duly observed by the detective on duty. He went into the house, was absent five minutes, and came out again, driving off without a fare.

Ten minutes later, at a signal from the detective, the house was visited by three C.I.D. men from Scotland Yard, and the mystery of the taxicab driver was cleared up forever.

For, instead of George Wallis, they discovered sitting at his ease in the drawing-room upstairs, and reading a novel with evident relish, that same black-bearded chauffeur.

===Chinese===
吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿（Gilbert Standerton）举行小型晚宴的那天夜里，那个黑胡子的出租车司机——他先前曾到查令十字路（Charing Cross Road）旁那座宅子来领过吩咐——来到四十三号门前，自然被值班侦探看在眼里。他走进屋去，消失了五分钟，又走了出来，没载一个客人就把车开走了。

十分钟后，接到侦探的信号，苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）刑事部（C.I.D.）的三名探员造访了这座房子，出租车司机之谜就此永远解开了。

因为他们发现，楼上的客厅里悠然自得、正津津有味读着小说的，不是别人，正是那个黑胡子司机，而不是乔治·沃利斯（George Wallis）。

===Original===
“It is very simple,” said Inspector Goldberg, “the driver comes up and George Wallis is waiting inside made up exactly like him. The moment he enters the door and closes it Wallis opens it, and steps out on to the car and drives off. You people watching thought it was the same driver returned.”

He looked at his prisoner.

“Well, what are you going to do?” asked the bearded man.

“I am afraid there is nothing we can do with you,” said Goldberg regretfully. “Have you got a licence?”

“You bet your life I have,” said the driver cheerfully, and produced it.

===Chinese===
“很简单，”戈德堡探长（Inspector Goldberg）说，“司机上了门，乔治·沃利斯正在屋里等着，装扮得跟他一模一样。司机一进门把门关上，沃利斯就开了门，跨上车把车开走。你们在外面盯梢的还以为是同一个司机回来了呢。”

他打量着被扣下的那个人。

“怎么样，你们打算怎么处置？”黑胡子问。

“恐怕拿你没办法。”戈德堡不无遗憾地说，“你有驾照吗？”

“这还用问，当然有。”司机痛快地应着，掏出了驾照。

===Original===
“I can take you for consorting with criminals.”

“A difficult charge to prove,” said the bearded one, “more difficult to get a conviction on, and possibly it would absolutely spoil your chance of bagging George in the end.”

“That is true,” said Goldberg; “anyway, I’m going to look for your taxicab. I can at least pull George in for driving without a licence.”

The man shook his head.

“I am sorry to disappoint you,” he said with mock regret, “but George has a licence too.”

“The devil he has,” said the baffled inspector.

===Chinese===
“我可以以结交罪犯的罪名抓你。”

“这个罪名不好证实，”黑胡子说，“要定罪更难，而且说不定会彻底坏了你们最后抓住乔治的机会。”

“这倒是实话。”戈德堡说，“不管怎样，我要去找你那辆车。至少我可以以无证驾驶的罪名逮捕乔治。”

那人摇了摇头。

“很抱歉让你失望，”他假装遗憾地说，“不过乔治也有驾照。”

“见鬼，他竟然有。”被难住的探长说。

===Original===
“Funny, isn’t it,” said the bearded man. “George is awfully thorough.”

“Come now, Smith,” said the detective genially, “what is the game? How deep in this are you?”

“In what?” asked the puzzled man.

Goldberg gave him up for a bad job. He knew that Wallis had chosen his associates with considerable care.

“Anyway, I will go after George,” he said. “You are probably putting up a little bluff on me about the licence. Once I get him inside the jug there are lots of little things I might be able to discover.”

“Do,” said the driver earnestly. “You will find him standing on the Haymarket rank at about half-past ten tonight.”

===Chinese===
“有意思，是不是？”黑胡子说，“乔治办事可周到得很。”

“行了，史密斯（Smith），”探长和气地说，“到底在搞什么名堂？你陷得有多深？”

“陷在什么里？”那人一头雾水。

戈德堡认定这家伙榨不出东西来，就此作罢。他知道沃利斯挑选同伙是相当谨慎的。

“不管怎么说，我要去追乔治，”他说，“驾照的事你多半是在唬我。一旦把他弄进局子，许多小事情我或许就能查出来了。”

“请便。”司机诚恳地说，“今晚十点半左右，你会在海马克（Haymarket）的出租车候客处找到他。”

===Original===
“Yes, I know,” said the detective sardonically.

He had no charge and no warrant, save the search warrant which gave him the right of entry.

Smith, the driver, was sent about his business, and a detective put on to shadow him.

===Chinese===
“是，我知道。”探长讥诮地说。

他手里没有指控、没有逮捕令，只有一张赋予他入宅权利的搜查令。

司机史密斯被打发走人，另派了一名侦探去盯他的梢。

===Original===
With what success this shadowing was done may be gathered from the fact that at half-past ten that night Inspector Goldberg discovered the cab he was seeking, and to his amazement found it in the very place where Smith had told him to expect it. And there the bearded driver was sitting with all the aplomb of one who was nearing the end of a virtuous and well-rewarded day.

“Now, George,” said the inspector jocularly, “come down off that perch and let me have a look at your licence; if it is not made out in your name I am going to pull you.”

The man did not descend, but he put his hand in his pocket and produced a little leather wallet.

The inspector opened it and read.

“Ah!” he said exultantly, “as I thought, this is made out in the name of Smith.”

“I am Smith,” said the driver calmly.

===Chinese===
这次盯梢成效如何，从下面的事实可见一斑：当晚十点半，戈德堡探长找到了他要找的那辆车，而且令他惊愕的是，车正停在史密斯所说的地方。黑胡子司机就坐在那里，一副从容自若的神气，活像一个规规矩矩干完了一天活、报酬丰厚、正要收工的人。

“好了，乔治，”探长打趣道，“从那个高座上下来，让我瞧瞧你的驾照；要是不在你的名下，我就要抓你了。”

那人没有下来，只把手伸进口袋，掏出一个小皮夹。

探长打开来念了念。

“啊哈！”他得意地说，“果然不出我所料，这驾照开的是史密斯的名字。”

“我就是史密斯。”司机平静地说。

===Original===
“Get down,” said the inspector.

The man obeyed. There was no question as to his identity.

“You see,” he explained, “when you put your flat-footed splits on to follow me I had no intention of bothering George. He is big enough to look after himself, and, by the way, his licence is made out in his own name, so you need not trouble about that.

===Chinese===
“下来。”探长说。

那人依言下了车。他的身份毫无问题。

“你瞧，”他解释道，“你们派那两个扁平脚探子来盯我的时候，我本不想去麻烦乔治。他自己完全照顾得了自己。顺便说一句，他的驾照开的是他自己的名字，你不必在那上头费心了。

===Original===
“But as soon as I saw you did not trust me,” he said reproachfully, “why, I sort of got on my metal. I slipped your busy fellow in Oxford Street, and came on and took my cab from the desperate criminal you are chasing.”

“Where is he now?” asked Goldberg.

“In his flat, and in bed I trust at this hour,” said the bearded man virtuously.

===Chinese===
“可我一看出你们信不过我，”他责怪地说，“嘿，我就来了劲。我在牛津街（Oxford Street）甩掉了你们那位忙人，接着赶过来，从你们追捕的那个凶险罪犯手里接回了我的车。”

“他现在在哪儿？”戈德堡问。

“在他的公寓里，我想这个钟点已经上床了。”黑胡子一本正经地说。

===Original===
With this the inspector had to be content. To make absolutely sure, he went back to the house off Charing Cross Road, and found, as he feared, Mr. George Wallis, if not in bed, at least in his dressing-gown, and the end of his silk pyjamas flapped over his great woollen slippers.

“My dear good chap,” he expostulated wearily, “am I never to be left in quiet? Must the unfortunate record which I bear still pursue me, penitent as I am, and striving, as I may be, to lead that unoffending life which the State demands of its citizens?”

“Do not make a song about it, George,” grumbled Goldberg. “You have kept me busy all the night looking after you. Where have you been?”

“I have been to a picture palace,” said the calm man, “observing with sympathetic interest the struggles of a poor but honest bank clerk to secure the daughter of his rich and evil boss. I have been watching cowboys shooting off their revolvers and sheriffs galloping madly across plains. I have, in fact, run through the whole gamut of emotions which the healthy picture palace excites.”

===Chinese===
探长只好就此满足。为了绝对保险，他回到查令十字路旁那座宅子，发现——正如他所担心的——乔治·沃利斯先生就算没上床，至少也穿上了晨衣，真丝睡衣的裤脚耷拉在那双大号毛料拖鞋上。

“我亲爱的好人儿，”他倦意十足地抗议道，“就不能让人清静清静吗？我纵然已经悔过，纵然正努力过着国家要求公民过的那种与世无争的生活，我背上那段不幸的记录就非得一辈子缠着我不放吗？”

“别唱高调了，乔治。”戈德堡嘟囔道，“为了看住你，我忙了一整夜。你到底去哪儿了？”

“我去电影院了。”这位心平气和的人说，“怀着同情的兴味，看一个贫穷但诚实的银行小职员如何历尽艰辛，赢得他那位富有而邪恶的老板的女儿。我看了牛仔们砰砰放枪，看了警长们疯也似的策马驰过平原。实际上，我把这座有益身心的影院所能激发的全部情感都经历了一遍。”

===Original===
“You talk too much,” said the inspector.

He did not waste any further time, and left Mr. Wallis stifling a sleepy yawn; but the door had hardly closed behind the detective when Wallis’s dressing-gown was thrown aside, his pyjamas and woollen slippers discarded, and in a few seconds the man was fully dressed. From the front window he saw the little knot of detectives discussing the matter, and watched them as they moved slowly to the end of the street. There would be a further discussion there, and then one of them would come back to his vigil; but before they had reached the end of the street he was out of the house and walking rapidly in the opposite direction to that which they had taken.

===Chinese===
“你的话太多了。”探长说。

他不再多耽搁，丢下强忍睡意、呵欠连天的沃利斯先生走了。可是侦探前脚刚出门，沃利斯后脚就甩掉晨衣，褪下睡衣和毛料拖鞋，几秒钟工夫便穿戴整齐。从前窗望出去，他看见那一小群侦探正在商议，又看着他们缓缓朝街尾走去。他们还会在那里再商量一阵，然后其中一个会回来继续守夜；可不等他们走到街尾，他已经出了宅子，朝着他们去向的相反方向快步走去。

===Original===
He had left a light burning to encourage the watcher. He must take his chance about getting back without being observed. He made his way quickly in the direction of the tube station, and a quarter of an hour later, by judicious transfers, he was in the vicinity of Hampstead. He walked down the hill towards Belsize Park and picked up a taxicab. He had stopped at the station to telephone, and had made three distinct calls.

Soon after eleven he was met at Chalk Farm Station by his two confederates. Thereafter all trace was lost of them. So far, in a vague and unsatisfactory way, Inspector Goldberg had kept a record of Wallis’s movements that night.

He had to guess much, and to take something on trust, for the quarry had very cleverly covered his tracks.

===Chinese===
他留了一盏灯亮着，好让守望的人安心。回去时能不能不被察觉，只好碰运气了。他快步朝地铁站走去，一刻钟后，经过几次巧妙的换乘，他已到了汉普斯特德（Hampstead）附近。他顺着山坡往贝尔塞斯公园（Belsize Park）方向走去，招了一辆出租车。他中途曾在车站停车打了电话，一共拨了三个不同的号码。

十一点刚过，他的两个同伙在乔克法姆车站（Chalk Farm Station）与他会合。此后他们的踪迹便全部消失。至于那天夜里沃利斯的行踪，戈德堡探长至此只记下了一份含糊而无法令人满意的账。

他只好多处猜测，部分凭想当然采信，因为这头猎物把行迹掩盖得十分巧妙。

===Original===
At midnight the guard in the Bank of the Northern Provinces was making his round, and was ascending the stone steps which led from the vault below, when three men sprang at him, gagged him and bound him with incredible swiftness. They did not make any attempt to injure him, but with scientific thoroughness they placed him in such a position that he was quite incapable of offering resistance or of summoning assistance to his aid. They locked him in a small room usually occupied by the assistant bank manager, and proceeded to their work downstairs.

“This is going to be a stiff job,” said Wallis, and he put his electric lamp over the steel grating which led to the entrance to the strong room.

===Chinese===
午夜时分，北方诸省银行（Bank of the Northern Provinces）的守夜人正在巡查，沿着从地下金库上来的石阶往上走，三条人影猛地扑到他身上，以不可思议的迅捷堵住他的嘴、捆住他的手脚。他们无意伤害他，而是以行家式的周密把他摆布到一个全然无法反抗、也无法呼救的姿势，随后把他锁进银行经理助理平时使用的一个小房间里，便下楼干他们的活去了。

“这可是块硬骨头。”沃利斯说着，把电筒照向通往保险库入口的钢栅。

===Original===
Persh, the stout man who was with him, nodded.

“The grating is nothing,” he said, “I can get this open.”

“Look for the bells, Callidino,” said Wallis.

The little Italian was an expert in the matter of alarms, and he examined the door scientifically.

“There is nothing here,” he said definitely.

===Chinese===
同他在一起的壮胖男子佩什（Persh）点了点头。

“这栅栏不算什么，”他说，“我能弄开。”

“查查有没有警铃，卡利迪诺（Callidino）。”沃利斯说。

那个矮小的意大利人是报警装置方面的行家，他有条不紊地检查了那扇门。

“这儿没有。”他言之凿凿地说。

===Original===
Persh, who was the best lock man in the world, set to work, and in a quarter of an hour the gate swung open. Beyond this, at the end of the passage, was a plain green door, offering no purchase whatever to any of the instruments they had brought. Moreover, the lock was a remarkable one, since it was not in the surface of the door itself, but in a small steel cabinet in the room overhead. But the blowpipe was got to work expeditiously. Wallis had the plan of the door carefully drawn to scale, and he knew exactly where the vital spot in the massive steel covering was to be found. For an hour and a half they worked, then Persh stopped suddenly.

“What was that?” he said.

Without another word the three men raced back along the passage, up the stairs to the big office on the ground floor, Persh leading.

===Chinese===
佩什是天下第一的开锁好手。他动手干了起来，一刻钟后，铁门洞开。门后是走廊尽头一扇素面的绿门，他们带来的任何工具在它上面都无处着力。而且那把锁非同寻常——锁芯不在门面上，而在楼上房间里的一个小钢柜里。于是他们麻利地动用了喷焰管。沃利斯手里有按比例精心绘制的这扇门的图纸，对厚钢板上的要害位置了如指掌。他们干了一个半钟头，佩什突然停了下来。

“那是什么声音？”他说。

三人一言不发，飞奔回走廊，冲上楼梯直奔底楼的大办公室，佩什冲在最前。

===Original===
As he made his appearance from the stairway a shot rang out, and he staggered. He thought he saw a figure moving in the shadow of the wall, and fired at it.

“You fool!” said Wallis, “you will have the whole place surrounded.”

Again a shot was fired, and this time there was no doubt as to who was the assailant. Wallis threw the powerful gleam of his lamp in the direction of the office. With one hand free and the other holding a revolver, there crouched near the door the guard they had left secure. Wallis doused his light as the man fired again.

“Out of this, quick!” he cried.

===Chinese===
他刚从楼梯口露面，就有一声枪响，他踉跄了一下。他仿佛看见墙影里有个人影在动，便朝那里开了一枪。

“蠢货！”沃利斯说，“你会招来一大帮人把这儿团团围住的。”

又是一声枪响，这回开枪的是谁已毫无疑问。沃利斯把强有力的灯光扫向办公室方向。只见门边蹲着那个他们自以为捆得结结实实的守夜人，一只手已挣脱出来，另一只手握着左轮枪。那人再次开枪时，沃利斯赶紧熄了灯。

“快撤！”他喊道。

===Original===
Through the back way they sped, up the little ladder then through the skylight where they had entered, across the narrow ledge, and through the hosier’s establishment which had been the means of entrance. Persh was mortally wounded, though he made the supreme and final effort of his life. They saw people running in the direction of the Bank, and heard a police whistle blow; but they came out of the hosier’s shop together, quietly and without fuss, three respectable gentlemen, one apparently a little the worse for drink.

Wallis hailed a taxicab, and gave elaborate directions. He made no attempt to hurry whilst Callidino assisted the big man into the vehicle, then they drove off leisurely. As the cab moved Persh collapsed into one corner.

“Were you hit?” asked Wallis anxiously.

“I am done for, George, I think,” whispered the man.

===Chinese===
他们从后路飞奔而出，爬上小梯，钻进当初进来的那扇天窗，跨过窄窄的窗沿，穿过了作为进路的那家内衣袜店。佩什受了致命伤，却仍拼出了平生最后一把力气。他们看见人们朝银行方向跑去，听见警笛声起；可他们三人一起从容走出内衣袜店，不慌不忙，三位体面绅士，其中一位看样子还微带醉意。

沃利斯招呼了一辆出租车，细细交代了路线。卡利迪诺搀那位大块头上车时，他也丝毫没有露出匆忙的样子，随后车子从容开动。车一上路，佩什便瘫倒在角落里。

“你中枪了？”沃利斯焦急地问。

“我完了，乔治，我看是。”那人低声说。

===Original===
George made a careful examination with his lamp and gasped. He was leaning his head out of the window.

“What are you doing?” asked Persh weakly.

“I am going to take you to the hospital,” said Wallis.

“You will do nothing of the kind,” said the other hoarsely. “For God’s sake do not jeopardise the whole crowd for me. I tell you I am finished. I can﻿—”

He said no other word, every muscle in his frame seemed at that moment to relax, and he slid in a loose heap to the floor.

===Chinese===
乔治用灯仔细照了照，倒抽一口冷气。他把头探出车窗外。

“你在干什么？”佩什虚弱地问。

“我要送你去医院。”沃利斯说。

“千万别那样做。”那人声音嘶哑地说，“看在上帝份上，别为我一个人连累大伙儿。我告诉你，我不行了。我可以——”

他再没说出第二句话。那一刻，他浑身的肌肉仿佛都松弛了下来，整个人软软地瘫滑到车底板上。

===Original===
They lifted him up.

“My God!” said Wallis, “he is dead.”

And dead, indeed, was Persh, that amiable and florid man.

===Chinese===
他们把他扶了起来。

“天哪！”沃利斯说，“他死了。”

佩什，那个和气红润的汉子，确实死了。

---

===Original===
“The burglary at the Northern Provinces Bank continues to excite a great deal of comment in city circles,” wrote the representative of the *Daily Monitor*.

“The police have made a number of interesting discoveries. There can be no doubt whatever that the miscreants escaped by way of” (here followed a fairly accurate description of the method of departure). “What interests the police, however, is the evidence they are able to secure as to the presence of another man in the bank who is as yet unaccounted for. The fourth man seems to have taken no part in the robbery, and to have been present without the knowledge or without the goodwill of the burglars. The bank guard who was interviewed this morning by our representative, was naturally reticent in the interest of his employers, but he confirmed the rumour that the fourth man, whoever he was, was not antagonistic so far as he (the guard) was concerned. It now transpires that the guard had been hastily bound and gagged by the burglars, who probably, without any intention, had left their victim in some serious danger, as the gag had been fixed in such a manner that the unfortunate man nearly died.

===Chinese===
“北方诸省银行失窃案继续在金融圈里引起众多议论。”《每日监察报》（Daily Monitor）的记者写道。

“警方已有一系列有趣的发现。歹徒们确凿无疑是经由以下途径逃脱的”（此处是一段相当准确的逃跑方式描述）。“不过警方真正关心的，是他们掌握的证据所显示的另一件事：银行里还有第四个人，至今下落不明。这第四个人似乎并未参与劫案，他的在场也显然不为窃贼所知，或未得窃贼的善意。本报记者今晨采访了银行守夜人，他出于顾全东家的立场自然讳莫如深，但他证实了那个传闻：无论这第四个人是谁，就他（守夜人）而言，对方并无敌意。现在得知，守夜人曾被窃贼仓促捆缚、堵口，而窃贼很可能在无意之间把受害者置于极危险的境地——口中的堵物塞得那样死，这位不幸的人险些丧命。

===Original===
“Then when he was almost *in extremis* there had appeared on the scene the fourth individual, who had loosened the gag, and made him more comfortable. It was obvious that he was not a member of the original burglar gang.

“The theory is offered that on the night in question two separate and independent sets of burglars were operating against the bank. Whether that is so or not, a tribute must be paid to the humanity of number four.”

===Chinese===
“而就在他几乎不省人事、命悬一线之际，第四个人出现了，替他松开了堵物，让他好受了一些。显然，此人并非窃贼一伙。

“有一种说法认为，案发当夜，有两伙互不相干的窃贼同时盯上了这家银行。无论真相如何，第四位先生的仁义之举，都值得致以敬意。”

---

===Original===
“So that was it.” Wallis read the account in his paper that morning without resentment. Though the evening had ended disastrously for him, he had cause for satisfaction. “I should never have forgiven myself if we had killed that guard,” he said to his companion.

His eyes were tired, and his face was unusually pale. He had spent a strenuous evening. He sat now in his bucket-shop office, and his sole companion was Callidino.

“I suppose poor old Persh will catch us,” he said.

===Chinese===
“原来如此。”那天早晨，沃利斯读着报纸上的这篇报道，心无怨怼。尽管那晚的结局对他来说是场灾难，他仍有感到满意的地方。“要是咱们把那守夜人弄死了，我一辈子都不会原谅自己。”他对同伴说。

他两眼疲惫，脸色白得反常。他度过了一个劳碌的夜晚。此刻他坐在自己那间投机商号办公室里，唯一的同伴是卡利迪诺。

“我猜可怜的老佩什会把我们给连累出来。”他说。

===Original===
“Why Persh?” asked the other.

“The taxi driver will be able to identify us as having been his companions. I wonder they have not come before. There is no use in running away. Do you know,” he asked suddenly, “that no man ever escapes the English police if he is known. It saves a lot of trouble to await developments.”

“I thought you had been to the station,” said Callidino in surprise.

“I have,” said Wallis, “I went there the first thing﻿—in fact, the moment I had an excuse﻿—to identify Persh. There is no sense in pretending we did not know him. The only thing to do is to prove the necessary alibis. As for me, I was in bed and asleep.”

===Chinese===
“这跟佩什有什么关系？”对方问。

“那个出租车司机能指认我们当晚曾同他在一起。奇怪他们怎么还没上门来。逃跑没有用。你可知道，”他突然发问，“一个出了名的人，从来逃不出英国警察的手心。坐等事态发展反倒省去许多麻烦。”

“我还以为你去过警察局了。”卡利迪诺愕然道。

“我去过了。”沃利斯说，“头一件事就是去认尸——一找到借口就去了，认的是佩什。装作不认识他毫无意义。眼下唯一要紧的是把必要的不在场证明做好。至于我，我当时正躺在床上睡觉。”

===Original===
“Did anybody see you get back?” asked Callidino.

Wallis shook his head.

“No,” he said, “they left one man to look after me, and he did a very natural thing, he walked up and down the street. There was nothing easier than to walk the way he was going behind his back and slip in just when I wanted to.”

Shadowing is a most tiring business, and what very few realise is the physical strain of remaining in one position, having one object in view. Even the trained police may be caught napping in the most simple manner, and as Wallis said, he had found no difficulty in making his way back to the house without observation. The only danger had been that during his absence somebody had called.

===Chinese===
“有人看见你回去了吗？”卡利迪诺问。

沃利斯摇了摇头。

“没有，”他说，“他们留了一个人盯我，那人干了件再自然不过的事——在街上来回踱步。我只需趁他背过身去，跟在他身后走，想什么时候溜进屋，就什么时候溜进屋，再容易不过了。”

盯梢是件极其累人的差事，而极少有人体会到，长时间保持一个姿势、盯死一个目标，对身体是多大的煎熬。即便是训练有素的警察，也可能以最简单的方式被人钻了空子。正如沃利斯所说，他不费吹灰之力就回了家，没被任何人瞧见。唯一的风险，是他不在的时候有人登门。

===Original===
“What about you?”

Callidino smiled.

“My alibi is more complex,” he said, “and yet more simple. My excellent compatriots will swear for me. They lie very readily these Neapolitans.”

“Aren’t you a Neapolitan?”

“Sicilian,” smiled the other. “Neapolitan!”

The contempt in his tone amused Wallis.

===Chinese===
“那你呢？”

卡利迪诺笑了。

“我的不在场证明更复杂，”他说，“却又更简单。我那些好同乡会替我发誓作证。这些那不勒斯人撒起谎来毫不费力。”

“你不就是那不勒斯人吗？”

“西西里人。”对方微笑着，“那不勒斯人！”

他话音里的轻蔑把沃利斯逗乐了。

===Original===
“Who is the fourth man?” Callidino asked suddenly.

“Our mysterious stranger, I am certain of that,” said George Wallis moodily. “But who the devil is he? I have never killed a man in my life so far, but I shall have to take unusual measures to settle my curiosity in this respect.

“There will have to be a division of the loot,” he said after a while, “I will go into it today. Persh has relations somewhere in the world, a daughter or a sister, she must have her share. There is a fake solicitor in Southwark who will do the work for us. We shall have to invent an uncle who died.”

Callidino nodded.

===Chinese===
“第四个人到底是谁？”卡利迪诺突然问。

“准是我们那位神秘的陌生客，这一点我确信无疑。”乔治·沃利斯郁郁地说，“可他究竟是哪个魔鬼？我这辈子到现在还没杀过人，不过要解开这份好奇，我恐怕得采取点非常手段了。

“赃物得分一分了。”过了一会儿他说，“今天我就去办。佩什在世上总有亲人，一个女儿或是一个姐妹，她那一份必须给她。萨瑟克（Southwark）有个冒牌律师可以替我们办这事。我们得编造一位去世的叔叔。”

卡利迪诺点点头。

===Original===
“As for me,” he said, rising and stretching himself, “already the vineyards of the South are appealing to me. I shall build me a villa in Montecatini and drink the wines, and another on Lake Maggiore and bathe in the waters. I shall do nothing for the rest of my life save eat and drink and bathe.”

“A perfectly ghastly idea!” said Wallis.

===Chinese===
“至于我嘛，”他站起身，伸展了一下腰背，“南方的葡萄园已经在召唤我了。我要在蒙特卡蒂尼（Montecatini）给自己盖一座别墅，品那里的美酒，再在马焦雷湖（Lake Maggiore）畔盖一座，泡那里的湖水。下半辈子，我除了吃吃喝喝、泡澡游泳，什么都不干。”

“这主意糟透了！”沃利斯说。

===Original===
The question of the fourth man troubled him more than he confessed. It was shaking his nerves. The police he understood, and was prepared for, could even combat, but here was the fourth man as cunning as they, who knew their plans, who followed them, who kept them under observation. Why? What object had he? He did not doubt that the fourth man was he who had watched them in Hatton Garden.

If it was a hobby it was a most extraordinary hobby, and the man must be mad. If he had an object in view, why did he not come out into the daylight and admit it?

“I wonder how I can get hold of him?” he said half aloud.

“Advertise for him,” said Callidino.

A sharp retort rose to the other’s lips, but he checked it. After all, there was something in that. One could do many things through the columns of the daily press.

===Chinese===
第四个人的问题比他嘴上承认的更让他心烦。这正在动摇他的神经。警察那一套他懂，他有防备，甚至能周旋；可这第四个人跟警察一样狡猾，知道他们的计划，跟踪他们，监视他们。为什么？他图的是什么？他毫不怀疑，第四个人就是那个在哈顿花园（Hatton Garden）盯过他们梢的人。

如果这只是个癖好，那也是天下少有的怪癖，这人准是疯了。如果他别有所图，为何不站到光天化日之下来承认呢？

“怎么才能抓到他呢？”他半自言自语地说。

“登报找他呀。”卡利迪诺说。

一句尖刻的反驳已到嘴边，又被他咽了回去。毕竟，这话不无道理。借着日报的版面，人能做成许多事。

## XIII The Maker of Wills / XIII 遗嘱制造者

===Original===
General Sir John Standerton was a man of hateful and irascible temper. The excuse was urged for him that he had spent the greater portion of his life in India, a country calculated to undermine the sweetest disposition. He was a bachelor and lived alone, save for a small army of servants. He had renamed the country mansion he had purchased twenty years before: it was now known from one end of the country to the other as The Residency, and here he maintained an almost feudal state.

His enemies said that he kept his battalion of servants at full strength so that he might always have somebody handy to swear at, but that was obviously spite. It was said, too, that every year a fresh firm of solicitors acted for him, and it is certain that he changed his banks with extraordinary rapidity.

===Chinese===
约翰·斯坦德顿爵士将军（General Sir John Standerton）脾气恶劣，动辄暴怒。有人替他开脱，说他一生大半在印度度过——那是个足以磨掉最温和性子的国度。他终身未娶，独居乡间，身边只有一支小小的仆役大军。他把二十年前购置的乡间宅邸改了名字：如今从国境这头到那头，人人都管它叫「官邸」（The Residency），他在这里维持着近乎封建领主般的排场。

他的仇人说，他之所以把那一营仆役维持在满员状态，无非是为了随时有人可供他咒骂，不过这显然是恶意中伤。据说他还每年换一家新的律师事务所替他办事，而可以肯定的是，他换银行的勤快程度也非同寻常。

===Original===
Leslie Frankfort was breakfasting with his brother one morning in his little Mayfair house. Jack Frankfort was a rising young solicitor, and a member of that firm which at the moment was acting for Sir John Standerton.

“By the way,” said Jack Frankfort, “I am going to see an old friend of yours this afternoon.”

“Who is my old friend?”

“Old Standerton.”

“Gilbert?”

Jack Frankfort smiled.

===Chinese===
一天早晨，莱斯利·弗兰克福特（Leslie Frankfort）在他梅费尔（Mayfair）的小宅子里同兄弟一起用早餐。杰克·弗兰克福特（Jack Frankfort）是一位前程看好的年轻律师，正供职于眼下替约翰·斯坦德顿爵士办事的那家事务所。

“对了，”杰克·弗兰克福特说，“今天下午我要去见你的一位老朋友。”

“我哪来的老朋友？”

“老斯坦德顿。”

“吉尔伯特？”

杰克·弗兰克福特笑了。

===Original===
“No, Gilbert’s terrible uncle; we are acting for him just now.”

“What is the object of the visit?”

“A will, my boy; we are going to make a will.”

“I wonder how many wills the old man has made?” mused Leslie. “Poor Gilbert!”

“Why poor Gilbert?” asked the other, helping himself to the marmalade.

“Why, he was his uncle’s heir for about ten minutes.”

===Chinese===
“不是，是吉尔伯特那位可怕的叔叔，眼下我们正替他办事。”

“去见他做什么？”

“立遗嘱哪，老兄，我们要去立一份遗嘱。”

“真不知道这老头儿立过多少份遗嘱了？”莱斯利若有所思，“可怜的吉尔伯特！”

“吉尔伯特有什么可怜的？”对方一边问，一边给自己抹橘子酱。

“嘿，他当他叔叔的继承人，统共当过十来分钟。”

===Original===
Jack grinned.

“Everybody is old Standerton’s heir for ten minutes,” he said.

“I verily believe he has endowed every hospital, every dogs’ home, every freakish institution that the world has ever heard of, in the course of the last twenty years, and he is making another will today.”

“Put in a good word for Gilbert,” said Leslie with a smile.

The other growled.

“There is not a chance of putting in a good word for anybody. Old Tomlins, who acted for him last, said that the greater difficulty in making a will for the old beggar is to finish one before the old man has thought out another. Anyway, he is keen on a will just now, and I am going down to see him. Come along?”

===Chinese===
杰克咧嘴一笑。

“谁当老斯坦德顿的继承人，都是十分钟的份儿。”他说。

“老实说，过去二十年间，他把世上听说过的每一家医院、每一座流浪狗之家、每一个稀奇古怪的机构统统捐遍了，而今天他又要再立一份遗嘱。”

“替吉尔伯特说说好话。”莱斯利微笑道。

对方咕哝了一声。

“给谁说好话都没门儿。上回替他办事的老汤林斯（Tomlins）说过，给这老家伙立遗嘱，最大的难处就是得赶在他想出下一份之前，把这一份立完。总之，他现在正惦记着遗嘱的事，我这就下乡去见他。一起去吗？”

===Original===
“You know the old gentleman?”

“Not on your life,” said the other hastily. “I know him indeed, and he knows me! He knows I am a pal of Gilbert’s. I stayed once with him for about two days. For the Lord’s sake do not confess that you are my brother, or he will find another firm of solicitors.”

“I do not usually boast of my relationship with you,” said Jack.

“You are an offensive devil,” said the other admiringly. “But I suppose you have to be, being a solicitor.”

===Chinese===
“你认识那位老先生？”

“可别这么说。”对方急忙道，“我千真万确认识他，他也认得我！他知道我是吉尔伯特的朋友。我在他府上住过一回，住了两天光景。看在主的份上，千万别承认你是我兄弟，不然他立马就会另换一家事务所。”

“我向来不拿跟你的关系吹嘘。”杰克说。

“你这张嘴真够损的。”对方佩服地说，“不过干律师这行的，大概也非得这样不可。”

===Original===
Jack Frankfort journeyed down to Huntingdon that afternoon in the company of a pleasant man, with whom he found himself in conversation without any of that awkwardness of introductions which makes the average English passenger so impossible.

===Chinese===
当天下午，杰克·弗兰克福特下乡去亨廷登（Huntingdon），同行的是一位和气的男人。两人攀谈起来，全然没有那种引荐寒暄的尴尬——正是这种尴尬，让寻常的英国旅客没法与人接近。

===Original===
This gentleman had evidently been all parts of the world, and knew a great many people whom Jack knew. He chatted interestingly for an hour on the strange places of the earth, and when the train drew up at the little station at which Mr. Frankfort was alighting, the other accompanied him.

“What an extraordinary coincidence,” said the stranger heartily. “I am getting out here too. This is a rum little town, isn’t it?”

It might be described as “rum,” but it was very pleasant, and it contained one of the most comfortable hostelries in England.

The fellow-passengers found themselves placed in adjoining rooms.

Jack Frankfort had hoped to conclude his business before the evening and return to London by a late train, but he knew that it would be unwise to depend upon the old man’s expedition.

===Chinese===
这位先生显然走遍天下，认识许多杰克也认识的人。他就着世上的奇域异乡娓娓谈了一个钟头，等火车停在小站、弗兰克福特先生该下车的时候，他也跟着下了车。

“真是巧极了。”陌生人热情地说，“我也在这儿下。这小城挺古怪，是不是？”

说它“古怪”倒也罢了，不过这地方十分宜人，城里还有全英格兰最舒适的旅店之一。

这对旅伴发现自己被安排在相邻的两个房间。

杰克·弗兰克福特本指望天黑前办完事，搭晚班车回伦敦，但他知道，指望老头儿办事麻利是不明智的。

===Original===
As a matter of fact, he had hardly been in the hotel a quarter of an hour before he received an intimation from The Residency that Sir John could not be seen until ten o’clock that evening.

“That settles all idea of going back to London,” said Jack despairingly.

He met his fellow-passenger at dinner.

===Chinese===
果不其然，他进旅馆还不到一刻钟，就接到「官邸」传来的话：约翰爵士十点之前不见客。

“回伦敦的念想算是断了。”杰克绝望地说。

晚餐时他又遇到了那位旅伴。

===Original===
Though he was not particularly well acquainted with the habits of Sir John, he knew that one of his fads was to dine late, and since he had no desire to spend a hungry evening, he advanced the normal dinner hour of the little hotel by thirty minutes.

He explained this apologetically to the comfortable man who sat opposite him, as they discussed a perfectly roasted capon.

“It suits me very well,” said the other, “I have a lot of work to do in the neighbourhood. You see,” he explained, “I am the proprietor of the Safe Agency.”

“Safe Agency,” repeated the other wonderingly.

The man nodded.

“It seems a queer business, but it is a fairly extensive one,” he said. “We deal principally in safes and strong rooms, secondhand or new. We have a pretty large establishment in London; but I am not going to overstep the bounds of politeness”﻿—he smiled﻿—“and try to sell you some of my stock.”

===Chinese===
他虽然对约翰爵士的习性谈不上特别熟悉，却知道此人的怪癖之一是晚餐吃得极晚；他可不想饿一晚上肚子，便把小旅馆通常的开饭时间提前了半个钟头。

他为此向坐在对面那位安逸富态的先生连声致歉，两人一面享用一只烤得恰到好处的阉鸡。

“这正合我意，”对方说，“我在这附近有不少活儿要干。是这样的，”他解释道，“我是保险柜商行（Safe Agency）的老板。”

“保险柜商行。”对方惊奇地重复了一遍。

那人点了点头。

“这行当看着古怪，生意却做得相当大，”他说，“我们主要买卖保险柜和保险库，新货二手都做。我们在伦敦有一家挺大的铺面；不过我可不打算失了礼数——”他笑了笑——“硬向你推销我的存货。”

===Original===
Frankfort was amused.

“Safe Agency,” he said; “one never realises that there can be money in that sort of thing.”

“One cannot realise that there is money in any branch of commerce,” said the other. “The moneymaking concerns which appeal are those where one sees brains being turned into actual cash.”

“Such as﻿—?”

“Such as a lawyer’s business,” smiled the other. “Oh, yes, I know you are a lawyer, you are the type, and I should have known your trade if I had not seen your dispatch case, and then your name.”

Jack Frankfort laughed.

“You are sharp enough to be a lawyer yourself,” he suggested.

“You are paying yourself a compliment,” said the other.

===Chinese===
弗兰克福特觉得有趣。

“保险柜商行，”他说，“谁也想不到这种买卖里居然有银子可赚。”

“哪一行里有银子可赚，谁也想不明白。”对方说，“真正叫人心动的赚钱买卖，是那种眼看着脑瓜子变成真金白银的行当。”

“比如说——？”

“比如说律师这一行。”对方微笑道，“哦，对了，我知道你是律师，一看就是这路人物。就算没瞧见你的公文包，随后再一听你的大名，我也能猜出你的行当。”

杰克·弗兰克福特笑了起来。

“你这份精明，自己当律师都够了。”他揶揄道。

“你这是在夸你自己呢。”对方说。

===Original===
Later, in the High Street, when he was calling a fly to drive him to The Residency, Jack noticed a big covered motor lorry, bearing only the simple inscription on its side: “The St. Bride’s Safe Company.”

He saw also his pleasant companion speaking earnestly with the black-bearded chauffeur.

A little later the lorry moved on through the narrow streets of the town and took the London Road.

===Chinese===
后来，在大街上，杰克正叫一辆单马马车送他去「官邸」时，注意到一辆带篷的大卡车，车身只有一行简单的字：「圣布莱德保险柜公司」（The St. Bride’s Safe Company）。

他还看见他那位和气的旅伴，正跟那个黑胡子司机热切地交谈。

过了一会儿，卡车穿过小城狭窄的街道，驶上了伦敦路（London Road）。

===Original===
Jack Frankfort had no time to speculate upon the opportunities for safe selling which the little town offered, for five minutes later he was in Sir John Standerton’s study.

The old General was of the type which is frequently depicted in humorous papers. He was stout and red of face, and wore a close-cut strip of white whisker, which ended abruptly below his ear, and was continued in a wild streak of white moustache across his face. He was bald, save for a little fringe of white hair which ran from temple to temple via the occiput, and his conversation might be described as a succession of explosions.

He stared up from under his ferocious eyebrow, as the young man entered the study, and took stock of him.

===Chinese===
小城能卖出多少保险柜，这样的机会问题杰克·弗兰克福特无暇细想，因为五分钟后，他已站在约翰·斯坦德顿爵士的书房里了。

老将军正是滑稽画报上常画的那一类人物。他身材肥胖，满面红光，腮边蓄着一条修得紧贴的白颊须，到耳朵下面戛然而止，又化作一撇桀骜不驯的白胡子横贯面孔。他头顶光秃，只余一圈白发从左鬓绕过后脑勺连到右鬓，说起话来可谓一连串的轰雷爆炸。

年轻人走进书房时，他从凶悍的眉毛底下抬眼盯住他，把他上下打量了一番。

===Original===
He was used to lawyers. He had had every variety, and had divided them into two distinct classes﻿—they were either rogues or fools. There was no intermediate stage with this old man, and he had no doubt in his mind that Jack Frankfort, a shrewd-looking young man, was to be classed in the former category. He bullied him into a seat.

“I want to see you about my will,” he said. “I have been seriously thinking lately of rearranging the distribution of my property.”

This was his invariable formula. It was intended to convey the impression that he had arrived at this present state of mind after very long and careful consideration, and that the making of wills was a serious and an important business to be undertaken, perhaps, once or twice in a man’s lifetime.

===Chinese===
律师他见得多了。各色各样都打过交道，被他分作截然的两类——不是恶棍就是蠢货。在老头儿这里没有中间地带，而且他毫不动摇地认定，杰克·弗兰克福特这个看上去精明的年轻人属于前一类。他连吼带喝地把年轻人按进了椅子。

“我找你是为了遗嘱的事。”他说，“近来我认真考虑重新安排财产的分配。”

这是他雷打不动的开场白，意在给人这样一种印象：他是经过长期深思熟虑才达到眼下这个心境的，而立遗嘱乃是一桩严肃的大事，一个人一辈子兴许才郑重其事地做上一两回。

===Original===
Jack nodded.

“Very good. General,” he said. “Have you a draft?”

“I have no draft,” snapped the other. “I have a will which has already been prepared, and here is a copy.”

He threw it across to his solicitor.

“I do not know whether you have seen this?”

===Chinese===
杰克点了点头。

“很好，将军。”他说，“您有草稿吗？”

“我没什么草稿。”对方厉声道，“我有一份已经拟好的遗嘱，这儿是抄件。”

他把它丢给自己的律师。

“不知道你见过这份没有？”

===Original===
“I think I have one in my bag,” said Jack.

“What the devil do you mean by carrying my will about in your bag?” snarled the other.

“That is the only place I could think of,” said the young man, calmly. “You would not like me to carry it about in my trousers’ pocket, would you?”

The General stared.

“Do not be impertinent, young man,” he said ominously.

===Chinese===
“我想我包里就有一份。”杰克说。

“你把我这份遗嘱装在包里到处跑，到底是什么意思？”对方咆哮道。

“我只想到这么个地方。”年轻人不慌不忙地说，“总不见得您愿意我把它揣在裤兜里到处走吧？”

将军瞪着他。

“放规矩点，年轻人。”他不祥地警告道。

===Original===
It was not a good beginning, but Jack knew that every method had been tried, from the sycophantic to the pompous, but none had succeeded, and the end of all endeavours, so far as the solicitors were concerned, had been the closing of their association with the General’s estate.

He was rather a valuable client if he could only be retained. No human solicitor had discovered a method of retaining him.

“Very well,” said the General at last. “Now please jot down exactly what my wishes are, and have the will drafted accordingly. In the first place, I revoke all former wills.”

Jack, with a sheet of paper and a pencil, nodded and noted the fact.

===Chinese===
开局不妙，但杰克知道，从阿谀奉承到装腔作势，什么法子都有人试过了，无一成功，而就历届律师而言，一切努力的下场，都是与将军的产业一拍两散。

这位主顾要是留得住，油水着实可观。只可惜还没有哪位人类律师找到过留住他的法子。

“好吧。”将军终于开口，“现在请把我的意愿一字不差地记下来，照着拟成遗嘱。第一，我撤销此前所有遗嘱。”

杰克拿过一张纸、一支铅笔，点点头，记下了这一条。

===Original===
“In the second place I want you to make absolutely certain that not a penny of my money goes to Dr. Sundle’s Dogs’ Home. The man has been insolent to me, and I hate dogs, anyhow. Not a penny of my money is to go to any hospital or to any charitable institution whatever.”

The old sinner declaimed this with relish.

“I had intended leaving a very large sum of money to a hospital fund,” he explained, “but after the behaviour of this infernal Government﻿—”

Jack might have asked in what way the old man expected to get even with the offending Government by denying support to all institutions designed to help the poor, but wisely kept the question in the background.

===Chinese===
“第二，你要给我保证，我一分钱也不许落到桑德尔博士（Dr. Sundle）的流浪狗之家。那家伙对我无礼，再说我本来就讨厌狗。我的钱一个子儿也不许捐给任何医院或任何慈善机构。”

老家伙咂着滋味，慷慨陈词。

“我本来打算给一家医院基金留下一大笔钱，”他解释道，“可这该死的政府干了那些事之后——”

杰克本可以问一句：断了对一切济贫机构的捐助，老头儿打算怎样跟那得罪他的政府算账？但他明智地把这个问题咽了回去。

===Original===
“No charitable institution whatever.”

The old man spoke slowly, emphatically, thumping the table with every other word.

“A hundred pounds to the Army Temperance Association, though I think it is a jackass of an institution. A hundred pounds to the Soldiers’ Home at Aldershot, and a thousand pounds if they make it nonsectarian.” He grinned and added: “It will be Church of England to everlasting doomsday, so that money’s safe! And,” he added, “no money to the Cottage Hospital here﻿—do not let that bequest creep in. That stupid maniac of a doctor﻿—I forget his beastly name﻿—led the agitation for opening a right-o’-way across my estate. I will ‘right-o’-way’ him!” he said viciously.

===Chinese===
“任何慈善机构都不给。”

老头儿说得又慢又重，说一个词便拍一下桌子。

“给陆军禁酒协会一百镑——不过依我看那是个蠢驴机构。给奥尔德肖特（Aldershot）的军人之家一百镑，要是他们肯改成不限教派，就给一千镑。”他咧嘴一笑，补充道：“他们到世界末日也改不掉英国国教的牌子，所以那一千镑稳当着呢！还有，”他接着说，“本地这所乡村医院一个子儿也不给——别让那份遗赠混进来。那个蠢货疯子医生——我忘了他那鬼名字——带头鼓噪，要在我庄园里开一条通行路。我倒要‘通行’他一下！”他狠狠地说。

===Original===
He spent half an hour specifying the people who were not to benefit by his will, and the total amount of his reluctant bequests during that period did not exceed a thousand pounds.

When he had finished he stared hopelessly at the young lawyer, and a momentary glint of humour came in the hard old blue eyes.

“I think we have disposed of everybody,” he said, “without disposing of anything. Do you know my nephew?” he asked suddenly.

“I know a friend of your nephew.”

===Chinese===
他花了半个钟头，把不得从遗嘱中受益的人一一数了个遍，这期间他勉强施舍的遗赠加起来，总共没超过一千镑。

数完了，他绝望地瞪着年轻律师，那双苍老而冷酷的蓝眼睛里闪过一丝转瞬即逝的笑意。

“我想咱们已经把所有人都打发掉了，”他说，“却什么也没打发掉。你认识我侄子吗？”他突然问。

“我认识您侄子的一个朋友。”

===Original===
“Are you related to that grinning idiot Leslie Frankfort?” roared the old man.

“He is my brother,” said the other calmly.

“Humph,” said the General, “I thought I recognised the face. Have you met Gilbert Standerton?” he asked suddenly.
