“I have met him once or twice,” said Jack Frankfort carelessly, “as you may have met people, just to say ‘how do you do?’ and that sort of thing.”

“I have never met people to say ‘how do you do?’ and that sort of thing,” protested the old man with a snort. “What sort of fellow do you think he is?” he asked after a pause.

===Chinese===
“你跟那个咧嘴傻笑的蠢货莱斯利·弗兰克福特沾亲带故吗？”老头儿吼道。

“他是我兄弟。”对方平静地说。

“哼。”将军说，“我就说我认得这张脸。你见过吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿吗？”他突然问。

“见过一两面。”杰克·弗兰克福特漫不经心地说，“就像人们相互认识那样，无非说声‘您好’之类。”

“我见人从来不为说声‘您好’之类。”老头儿哼了一声，抗议道。顿了顿，他又问：“你觉得他是个什么样的人？”

===Original===
The injunction of Leslie to “say a good word for Gilbert” came to the young man’s mind.

“I think he is a very decent sort of fellow,” he said, “though somewhat reserved and a little standoffish.”

The old man glowered at him.

“My nephew standoffish?” he snapped, “Of course he is standoffish. Do you think a Standerton is everybody’s money? There is nothing Tommyish or Dickish or Harryish about our family, sir. We are all standoffish, thank God! I am the most standoffish man you ever met in your life.”

“That I can well believe,” thought Jack, but did not give utterance to his thought.

===Chinese===
年轻人想起莱斯利“替吉尔伯特说句好话”的嘱托。

“我觉得他是个相当正派的人，”他说，“只是有些内敛，还有点冷淡矜持。”

老头儿狠狠瞪了他一眼。

“我侄子冷淡矜持？”他厉声道，“他当然矜持。你以为斯坦德顿家的人，是谁想沾就能沾的光吗？先生，我们家族里可没有张三气、李四气、王二麻子气。我们全家个个矜持，感谢上帝！我是你这辈子见过的最矜持的人。”

“这个我完全信。”杰克心想，却没有说出口。

===Original===
Instead he pursued the subject in his own cunning way.

“He is the sort of man,” he said innocently “whom I should think money would be rather wasted on.”

“Why?” asked the General with rising wrath.

Jack shrugged his shoulders.

“Well, he makes no great show, does not attempt to keep any particular place in London Society. In fact, he treats Society as though he were superior to it.”

===Chinese===
他转而用自己的狡黠办法，继续深挖这个话题。

“他是那种人，”他一脸无辜地说，“依我看，钱花在他身上多少有些白费。”

“为什么？”将军的火气直往上撞。

杰克耸了耸肩。

“这个嘛，他从不讲排场，也不想在伦敦社交界里占什么地位。实际上，他对社交界的态度，仿佛他比它还高一等。”

===Original===
“And so he is,” growled the General, “we are all superior to Society. Do you think, sir, that I care a damn about any of the people in this county? Do you think I am impressed by my Lord of High Towers and my Lady of the Grange, and the various upstart parvenu aristocrats that swarm over this country like﻿—like﻿—field mice? No sir! And I trust my nephew is in the same mind. Society as it is at present constituted is not worth that!” He snapped his fingers in Jack’s impassive face. “That settles it,” said the General with decision. He pointed his finger at the notes which the other was taking. “The residue of my property I leave to Gilbert Standerton. Make a note of that.”

===Chinese===
“他本来就更高等。”将军低吼道，“我们个个都比社交界高等。先生，你以为我会在乎本郡的任何人吗？你以为我那位高塔勋爵、那位庄园夫人，还有那些像——像——像田鼠一样满国乱窜的暴发户新贵族，能唬得住我吗？不，先生！我相信我侄子也是一样的想法。如今这副模样的社交界，值不了这个！”他在杰克毫无表情的脸前打了个响指。“就这么定了。”将军斩钉截铁地说。他指着对方正在记的笔记。“我的财产余数遗赠给吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿。记下来。”

===Original===
Twice had he uttered the same words in his lifetime, and twice had he changed his mind. It might well be that he would change his mind again. If the reputation he bore was justified, the morning would find him in another frame of mind.

“Stay over tomorrow,” he said at parting. “Bring me the draft at breakfast time.”

“At what hour?” asked Jack politely.

“At breakfast time,” roared the old man.

“What is your breakfast hour?”

“The same hour as every other civilised human being,” snapped the General “at twenty-five minutes to one. What time do you breakfast, for Heaven’s sake?”

“At twenty to one,” said Jack sweetly, and was pleased with himself all the way back to the hotel.

===Chinese===
这话他这辈子已经说过两回，也改过两回主意。他完全可能再改主意。倘若他素有的名声名不虚传，到了明早，他的心思又会是另一副样子。

“明天别走。”临别时他说，“吃早饭的时候把草稿给我带来。”

“几点？”杰克客气地问。

“吃早饭的时候。”老头儿吼道。

“那您吃早饭是几点？”

“跟所有文明人一样的钟点。”将军厉声说，“一点差二十五分。看在老天的份上，你几点吃早饭？”

“一点差二十分。”杰克甜甜地答道，回旅馆的一路上心里美滋滋的。

===Original===
He did not see his train companion that night, but met him at breakfast the next morning at the Christian hour of half-past eight.

Something had happened in the meantime to change the equable and cheery character of the other. He was sombre and silent, and he looked worried, almost ill, Jack thought. Possibly there was a bad time for safe selling, as there was a bad time for every other department of trade.

Thinking this, he kept off the subject of business, and scarcely half a dozen sentences were exchanged between the two during the meal.

===Chinese===
那天夜里他没有见到火车上的旅伴，直到第二天早晨八点半——一个体面人才用的钟点——吃早餐时才碰到他。

这中间出了什么事，把那位旅伴平和快活的性子全变了。他面色阴沉，一言不发，看样子忧心忡忡，几乎像病了，杰克想。也许保险柜生意也有淡季，正如其他各行当都有淡季一样。

这样想着，他便绝口不谈生意，一顿饭下来，两人统共没说上六句话。

===Original===
Returning to The Residency, Jack Frankfort found with surprise that the old man had not changed his mind over night. He was still of the same opinion; seemed more emphatically so. Indeed, Jack had the greatest difficulty in preventing him from striking off a miserable hundred pounds bequest which he had made to a northern dispensary.

“The whole of the money should be kept in the family,” said the General shortly; “it is absurd to fritter away little hundreds like this, it handicaps a man. I do not suppose he will have the handling of the money for many years yet, but ‘forethought,’ sir, is the motto of our family.”

It was all to Gilbert’s advantage that the lawyer persisted in demanding the restoration of the dispensary bequest. In the end the General cut out every bequest in the will, and in the shortest document which he had ever signed bequeathed the whole of his property, movable and immovable, to “my dear nephew” absolutely.

===Chinese===
回到「官邸」，杰克·弗兰克福特惊讶地发现，老头儿一夜之间竟没有改主意。他还是原先那套想法，而且似乎更加坚决了。的确，杰克费了九牛二虎之力，才拦住他没把捐给北方一家施诊所的那可怜的一百镑遗赠划掉。

“钱就该一个子儿不落地留在家里。”将军简短地说，“像这样一百一百地零散撒出去，成何体统，这是给人拖后腿。我看他也不见得这几年就经手这笔钱，可是‘未雨绸缪’，先生，这是我们家族的座右铭。”

律师执意要求恢复施诊所那笔遗赠，结果反倒全便宜了吉尔伯特。将军最后索性把遗嘱里的遗赠一笔一笔全删了，在他此生签过的最短的一份文件里，把全部动产不动产一概无条件遗赠给“我亲爱的侄子”。

===Original===
“He is married isn’t he?” he asked.

“I believe he is,” said Jack Frankfort.

“You believe! Now what is the good of your believing?” protested the old man. “You are my lawyer, and your business is to know everything. Find out if he is married, who his wife is, where she came from, and ask them up to dinner.”

“When?” demanded the startled lawyer.

“Tonight,” said the old man. “There is a man coming down from Yorkshire to see me, my doctor; we will make a jolly party. Is she pretty?”

“I believe she is.”

===Chinese===
“他结了婚的，是吧？”他问。

“我想是结了。”杰克·弗兰克福特说。

“你想！你想有什么用？”老头儿抗议道，“你是我的律师，你的本分就是把什么都打听清楚。去查清他结没结婚，太太是谁，什么出身，然后请他们上来吃晚饭。”

“什么时候？”律师吓了一跳。

“今晚。”老头儿说，“有个人要从约克郡（Yorkshire）下来看我，我的医生；咱们凑一桌热闹热闹。她漂亮吗？”

“我想是漂亮的。”

===Original===
Jack hesitated, for he was honestly in doubt. He knew very little about Gilbert Standerton or his affairs.

“If she is pretty, and she is a lady,” said the old General slowly, “I will also make provision for her separately.”

Jack’s heart sank. Would this mean another will? For good or ill, the wires were dispatched.

Edith received hers and read it in wonder.

Gilbert’s remained on the hall table, for he had not been home the previous night nor during that day.

The tear-reddened eyes of the girl offered eloquent testimony to the interest she displayed in his movements.

===Chinese===
杰克迟疑了，他是真心没底。他对吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿其人其事都知之甚少。

“要是她漂亮，又是个大家闺秀，”老将军慢条斯理地说，“我就另行给她置一份产业。”

杰克的心往下一沉。这岂不意味着又要立一份遗嘱？不管吉凶如何，电报已经拍出去了。

伊迪丝（Edith）收到了她的那份电报，纳罕地读着。

吉尔伯特的那封还搁在门厅的桌上，因为他前一晚没回家，这一天也没回来过。

姑娘那双哭得通红的眼睛，雄辩地证明了她对他的行踪何等挂心。

## V The Man Who Desired Wealth / V 渴求财富的人

===Original===
Leslie Frankfort was one of a group of three who stood in the inner office of Messrs. Warrell & Bird before a huge safe. There was plenty to attract and hold their attention, for the floor was littered with tools of every shape and description.

The safe itself bore evidence of a determined assault. A semicircle of holes had been burnt in its solid iron door about the lock.

===Chinese===
莱斯利·弗兰克福特是三人小组中的一员，他们正站在沃雷尔-伯德律师事务所（Messrs. Warrell & Bird）的里间办公室里，面对一只巨大的保险柜。有的是吸引并攫住他们目光的东西，因为地板上撒满了五花八门、各式各样的工具。

保险柜本身留着一场坚决攻击的痕迹。坚实的铁门上，围绕锁孔烧出了一圈半圆的孔洞。

===Original===
“They did that with an oxyhydrogen blowpipe,” said one of the men.

He indicated a number of iron tubes which lay upon the ground with the rest of the paraphernalia. “They made a thorough job of it. I wonder what disturbed them.”

The eldest of the men shook his head.

“I expect the night watchman may have alarmed them,” he said. “What do you think, Frankfort?”

===Chinese===
“这是用氧氢吹管干的。”其中一人说。

他指了指混在其余什物中间、躺在地上的几根铁管。“他们干得真彻底。不知道是什么惊动了他们。”

三人中年纪最长的那位摇了摇头。

“我看多半是守夜人惊动了他们，”他说，“你怎么看，弗兰克福特？”

===Original===
“I haven’t got over my admiration for their thoroughness yet,” said Leslie. “Why, the beggars must have used about a couple of hundred pounds’ worth of tools.”

He pointed to the kit on the ground. The detective’s gaze followed his extended finger. He smiled.

“Yes,” he said quietly, “these people are pretty thorough. You say you’ve lost nothing?”

Mr. Warrell shook his head.

===Chinese===
“我对他们那份彻底劲儿还是佩服得不行。”莱斯利说，“瞧，这帮家伙用的工具，怎么也值个两百来镑。”

他指了指地上的那套家伙。侦探的目光顺着他伸出的手指看去，笑了。

“是啊，”他不动声色地说，“这伙人确实够彻底的。你是说，你们什么都没丢？”

沃雷尔先生摇了摇头。

===Original===
“Yes and no,” he said carefully. “There was a diamond necklace which was deposited there last week by a client of ours—that has gone. I am anxious for the moment that this loss should not be reported.”

The detective looked at him wonderingly.

“That is rather a curious request,” he said, with a smile; “and you don’t usually have diamond necklaces in a stockbroker’s office—if I may be allowed to make that critical remark.”

Mr. Warrell smiled.

===Chinese===
“也可以说丢了，也可以说没丢。”他字斟句酌地说，“有位客户上星期寄存了一条钻石项链——那条项链不见了。眼下我最希望的是，这笔损失先别声张出去。”

侦探诧异地看着他。

“这个要求可有点奇怪，”他微笑着说，“再说，证券经纪行的办公室里，通常可不大见得到钻石项链——请恕我冒昧说这么一句挑剔的话。”

沃雷尔先生笑了。

===Original===
“It isn’t usual,” he said, “but a client of ours who went abroad last week came in just twenty minutes before the train left, and asked us to take care of the jewel cases.”

Mr. Warrell said this carelessly. He did not explain to the detective that they were held as security against the very large difference which the client had incurred; nor did he think it necessary to explain that he had kept the jewels in the office in the hope that the embarrassed lady might be able to redeem them.

===Chinese===
“确实不常有，”他说，“可有位客户上星期要出国，开车前二十分钟才赶到我们这儿，托我们照管她的首饰盒。”

沃雷尔先生说得很随意。他没有向侦探解释，这些首饰是押品，抵着那位客户欠下的一大笔亏空；他也不觉得有必要解释：他把首饰留在办公室里，是指望这位窘迫的夫人日后还能赎回去。

===Original===
“Did anybody know they were there except yourself and your partners?”

Warrell shook his head.

“I don’t think so. I have never mentioned it to anybody. Have you, Leslie?”

Leslie hesitated.

===Chinese===
“除了您本人和您的合伙人，还有谁知道首饰在这儿吗？”

沃雷尔摇了摇头。

“我想没有。我从没对任何人提起过。你呢，莱斯利？”

莱斯利迟疑了。

===Original===
“Well, I’m bound to admit that I did,” he confessed, “though it was to somebody who would not repeat it.”

“Who was it?” asked Warrell.

“To Gilbert Standerton. I certainly mentioned the matter when we were discussing safe robberies.”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，我不得不承认，我说过，”他坦白道，“不过是对一个不会往外传的人。”

“是谁？”沃雷尔问。

“吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿。我们那天讨论保险柜劫案的时候，我确实提到过这事。”

===Original===
The elder man nodded.

“I hardly think he is the sort of person who is likely to burgle a safe.”

He smiled.

===Chinese===
年长的那位点了点头。

“我很难认为他是那种会撬保险柜的人。”

他笑了笑。

===Original===
“It is a very curious coincidence,” said Leslie reflectively, “that he and I were talking about this very gang only a couple of days ago before he was married. I suppose,” he asked the detective suddenly, “there is no doubt that this is the work of your international friend?”

Chief Inspector Goldberg nodded his head.

“No doubt whatever, sir,” he said. “There is only one gang in England which could do this, and I could lay my hands on them today, but it would be a million pounds to one against my being able to secure at the same time evidence to convict them.”

===Chinese===
“说来也真是巧得蹊跷，”莱斯利若有所思地说，“就在他结婚前两三天，我们俩还谈起过正是这伙人。我猜，”他突然问侦探，“这案子是您那位国际朋友干的，这该没有疑问吧？”

戈德堡总探长（Chief Inspector Goldberg）点了点头。

“毫无疑问，先生。”他说，“全英格兰能干成这一手的只有一伙人，我今天就能把手按在他们头上，可要同时拿到能给他们定罪的证据，赔率是一百万镑对一，赌我办不到。”

===Original===
Leslie nodded brightly.

“That is what I was telling Gilbert,” he said, turning to his partner. “Isn’t it extraordinary that these things can be in the twentieth century? Here we have three or four men who are known—you told me their names, Inspector, after the last attempt—and yet the police are powerless to bring home their guilt to them. It does seem curious, doesn’t it?”

Inspector Goldberg was not amused, but he permitted himself to smile politely.

“But then you’ve got to remember how difficult it is to collect evidence against men who work on such a huge scale as do these bank smashers. What I can’t understand,” he said, “is what attraction your safe has for them. This second attempt is a much more formidable one than the last.”

“Yes, this is really a burglary,” said Mr. Warrell. “In the last case there was nothing so elaborate in their preparations, though they were much more successful, in so far as they were able to open the safe.”

===Chinese===
莱斯利轻快地点点头。

“我正是这么跟吉尔伯特说的。”他转向合伙人，“都到二十世纪了，居然还有这种事，岂不咄咄怪事？三四个人，姓名都是明摆着的——上次案发之后，探长，您还把他们的名字告诉了我——可警察偏偏无力把罪责钉在他们身上。这确实古怪，是不是？”

戈德堡探长并不觉得好笑，但还是客气地笑了笑。

“可你也得记住，要对付这种大规模作案的砸银行好手，收集证据有多难。我不明白的是，”他说，“你们的保险柜到底哪点吸引他们。这第二次下手，比上回气势凶猛得多了。”

“是啊，这回才算得上真正的盗窃。”沃雷尔先生说，“上回他们的准备远没有这么周密，倒是更成功，至少保险柜被他们打开了。”

===Original===
“I suppose you don’t want more of this to get in the papers than you can help,” said the Inspector.

Mr. Warrell shook his head.

“I don’t want any of it to get in till I have seen my client,” he said; “but I am entirely in your hands, and you must make such arrangements as you deem necessary.”

“Very good,” said the detective. “For the moment I do not think it is necessary to make any statement at all. If the reporters get hold of it, you had better tell them as much of the truth as you want to tell them, but the chances are that they won’t even get to hear of it as you communicated directly to the Yard.”

===Chinese===
“我想，能不上报的，你们就尽量别让这事见报。”探长说。

沃雷尔先生摇了摇头。

“在见到我的客户之前，我不想让报纸透露半个字，”他说，“不过我全听您的，必要与否，您看着安排就是。”

“很好。”侦探说，“眼下我认为完全没有必要发表任何声明。要是记者嗅到了风声，实话实说多少，随您的便；不过既然你们是直接报的苏格兰场，多半他们连听都听不到。”

===Original===
The police officer spent half an hour collecting and making notes of such data as he was able to secure. At the end of that time the old Jewry sent a contingent of plainclothes policemen to remove the tools.

The burglars had evidently entered the office after closing hours on the previous night, and had worked through the greater part of the evening, and possibly far into the night, in their successful attempt to cut out the lock of the safe. That they had been disturbed in their work was evident from the presence of the tools. This was not their first burglary in the City of London. During the previous six months the City had been startled by a succession of daring robberies, the majority of which had been successful.

The men had shown extraordinary knowledge of the safe’s contents, and it was this fact which had induced the police to narrow their circle of inquiry to three apparently innocent members of an outside broker’s firm. But try as they might, no evidence could be secured which might even remotely associate them with the crime.

===Chinese===
这位警官花了半个钟头，把能搜集到的资料一一记录在案。到头来，老朱瑞（Old Jewry，伦敦市警察厅所在地）方面派来一队便衣警察，把工具运走了。

窃贼显然是前一天晚上下班之后进的办公室，干了小半夜，说不定一直干到深夜，成功地割开了保险柜的锁。工具还留在现场，可见他们的活儿被打断了。这并不是他们在伦敦城里的第一次作案。此前六个月里，接二连三的大胆劫案令全城为之震动，其中大多数都得手了。

这伙人对保险柜里的东西显示出非同寻常的了解，正是这一点，促使警方把排查圈子缩小到一家场外经纪行的三名表面上清白无辜的成员身上。可是任凭他们怎么查，就是拿不到能把这三人跟案子哪怕沾上一丝边的证据。

===Original===
Leslie remembered now that he had laughingly challenged Gilbert Standerton to qualify for the big reward which two firms at least had offered for the recovery of their stolen goods.

“After all,” he said, “with your taste and genius, you would make an ideal thief-catcher.”

“Or a thief,” Gilbert had answered moodily. It had been one of his bad days, a day on which his altered prospects had preyed upon him.

===Chinese===
莱斯利这时想起来，他曾笑着撺掇吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿去挣那笔巨额赏金——至少有两家商行悬赏追回失窃货物。

“说到底，”他说，“凭你的品味和天才，你当个抓贼的能手再合适不过了。”

“或者是当个贼。”吉尔伯特当时闷闷地答道。那是他情绪低落的日子之一，命运陡转的阴云整天压在他心头。

===Original===
A telegram was waiting for Leslie when he entered the narrow portals of the City Proscenium Club. He took it down and opened it leisurely, and read its contents. A puzzled frown gathered on his forehead. It ran:﻿

> “I must see you this afternoon. Meet me at Charing Cross Station four o’clock.—Gilbert.”

===Chinese===
莱斯利走进普罗西尼恩俱乐部（City Proscenium Club）狭窄的大门时，一份电报正等着他。他取下电报，不紧不慢地拆开，读了内容，眉头困惑地皱了起来。电文是：——

> “今天下午我必须见你。四点在查令十字车站（Charing Cross Station）等我。——吉尔伯特”

===Original===
Punctually to the minute Leslie reached the terminus. He found Gilbert pacing to and fro beneath the clock, and was shocked at his appearance.

“What on earth is the matter with you?” he asked.

“Matter with me?” demanded the other hardly, “what do you think is the matter with me?”

“Are you in trouble?” asked Leslie anxiously.

===Chinese===
莱斯利分秒不差地到了车站。他看见吉尔伯特在大钟底下来回踱步，被他那副样子吓了一跳。

“你到底怎么了？”他问。

“我怎么了？”对方生硬地反问，“你倒说说，我怎么了？”

“你遇到麻烦了？”莱斯利焦急地问。

===Original===
He was genuinely fond of this friend of his.

“Trouble?” Gilbert laughed bitterly. “My dear good chap, I am always in trouble. Haven’t I been in trouble since the first day I met you? I want you to do something for me,” he went on briskly. “You were talking the other day about money. I have recognised the tragedy of my own dependence. I have got to get money, and get it quick.”

===Chinese===
他是真心疼爱这位朋友。

“麻烦？”吉尔伯特苦笑一声，“我亲爱的好人儿，我永远都在麻烦里。从认识你头一天起，我不就在麻烦里吗？我要你替我做点事。”他随即干脆利落地说下去，“前两天你还谈到钱。我已经认清了自己仰人鼻息的悲剧。我必须弄到钱，而且要快。”

===Original===
He spoke briskly, and in a matter-of-fact tone, but Leslie heard a determination which had never formed part of his friend’s equipment.

“I want to know something about shares and stocks and things of that sort,” Gilbert went on. “You’ll have to instruct me. I don’t suppose you know much about it yourself”—he smiled, with a return to the old good-humour—“but what little you know you’ve got to impart to me.”

“My dear chap,” protested the other, “why the devil are you worrying about a thing like that for on your honeymoon? Where is your wife, by the way?”

“Oh, she’s at the house,” said the other shortly. He did not feel inclined to discuss her, and Leslie, in his amazement, had sufficient tact to pass over the subject.

===Chinese===
他说得干脆，语气平平常常，可莱斯利听出了一种他这位朋友的禀性里从不曾有过的决绝。

“我想了解股票证券这一类事情。”吉尔伯特接着说，“你得给我当先生。我想你自己也懂不了多少——”他笑了笑，又恢复了往日的好脾气——“可你懂的那一点点，也必须倾囊相授。”

“我亲爱的好人儿，”对方抗议道，“你蜜月还没过完，鬼迷心窍操这种闲心干什么？对了，你太太呢？”

“哦，她在家里。”对方简短地说。他不想谈她，而莱斯利尽管满心惊讶，也还算识趣，把这个话头岔了过去。

===Original===
“I can tell you all I know now, if you want a tip,” he said.

“I want something bigger than a tip—I want investments. I want you to tell me something that will bring in about twelve thousand a year.”

Leslie stopped and looked at the other.

“Are you quite—?” he began.

===Chinese===
“你要是想听个门道，我现在就可以把我知道的全告诉你。”他说。

“我要的不是门道，是比门道大的东西——我要投资。我要你告诉我一些能带来每年一万二千镑左右收益的东西。”

莱斯利停下脚步，看着对方。

“你脑子还——？”他开口道。

===Original===
Gilbert smiled, a crooked little smile.

“Am I right in my head?” he finished. “Oh, yes, I am quite sane.”

“But don’t you see,” said the other, “you would want a little over a quarter of a million to bring in that interest.”

Gilbert nodded.

===Chinese===
吉尔伯特笑了，笑得有点歪。

“还清醒吗，是不是？”他替他说完，“哦，放心，我清醒得很。”

“可你算过没有，”对方说，“要有那份收益，你得拿出一笔二十五万还多的本钱。”

吉尔伯特点了点头。

===Original===
“I had an idea that some such amount was required. I want you to get me out between tonight and tomorrow a list of securities in which I can invest and which must be gilt-edged, and must, as I say, secure for me, or for my heirs, the sum I have mentioned.”

“And did you,” asked the indignant Leslie, “bring me to this beastly place on a hot afternoon in June to pull my leg about your dream investments?”

But something in Gilbert’s face checked his humour.

“Seriously, do you mean this?” he asked.

“Seriously, I mean it.”

===Chinese===
“我原就估摸着差不多得这个数。我要你在今晚到明天之间，给我开出一张可投资证券的清单，必须是金边证券，必须——我再重复一遍——稳稳保住我方才说的那笔进项，或者保给我继承人。”

“这么说，”莱斯利气不打一处来，“你把我叫到这个鬼地方，在六月个大热天的下午，就为了拿你的白日梦投资寻我开心？”

可吉尔伯特脸上有什么东西，让他的玩笑话咽了回去。

“说正经的，你是当真的？”他问。

“当真，我是认真的。”

===Original===
“Well, then, I’ll give you the list like a shot. What has happened—has uncle relented?”

Gilbert shook his head.

“He is not likely to relent,” he said. “As a matter of fact, I had a note today from his secretary to tell me that he is pretty ill. I’m awfully sorry.” There was a genuine note of regret in his tone. “He is a decent old chap.”

===Chinese===
“那好，这清单我立马给你开。出什么事了——叔叔回心转意了？”

吉尔伯特摇了摇头。

“他不可能回心转意了。”他说，“实际情况是，今天我收到他秘书的字条，说他病得不轻。我难过得很。”他的语气里有真诚的惋惜，“他是位不错的老先生。”

===Original===
“There’s no reason why he should hand over his wealth to the ‘demnition bow-wows,’ ” quoted Leslie indignantly. “But why did you meet me here, my son? Your club is round the corner.”

“I know,” said Gilbert; “but the club is—well, to tell you the truth,” he said, “I am giving up the club.”

“Giving up your club?” He stood squarely before the taller man. “Now just tell me,” he asked deliberately, “what the Dickens all this means? You’re giving up your club, you’ll be giving up your Foreign Office job next, my Croesus!”

Gilbert nodded.

===Chinese===
“确实没道理让他把万贯家财交给‘那帮天杀的狗东西’。”莱斯利愤愤地引了一句书上的话，“可你干吗约在这儿见我，我的儿？你的俱乐部就在拐角上。”

“我知道，”吉尔伯特说，“可俱乐部它——这样吧，跟你说实话，”他说，“我正打算退了俱乐部。”

“退俱乐部？”他端端正正站在这位高个子面前，“那你倒给我说说，”他一字一顿地问，“这一切究竟是怎么回事？你要退俱乐部，下一步怕是要辞外交部（Foreign Office）的差事了吧，我的大财神！”

吉尔伯特点了点头。

===Original===
“I have given up the Foreign Office work,” he said quietly. “I want all the time I can get,” he went on, speaking rapidly. “I want every moment of the day for my own plans and my own schemes. You don’t know what it’s all about, my dear chap”—he laid his hand affectionately on the other’s shoulder—“but just believe that I am in urgent need of all the advice you can give me, and I only want the advice for which I ask.”

“Which means that I am not to poke my nose in your business unless I have a special invitation card all printed and decorated. Very good,” laughed Leslie. “Now come along to my club. I suppose as a result of your brief married life you haven’t conceived a dislike to all clubs?”

===Chinese===
“外交部的工作，我已经辞了。”他平静地说，“我需要能腾出来的全部时间。”他接着飞快地说，“我一天的每分每秒，都要用在我自己的计划和打算上。这一切究竟为了什么，你不知道，我亲爱的好人儿——”他把手亲热地搭在对方肩上——“你只管相信，我急需你能给的一切指教，而且我只想要我问到的那部分。”

“也就是说，除非收到一张烫金印花、专程印好的请柬，我休想掺和你的事。行。”莱斯利笑道，“走，去我的俱乐部。想必短短几天婚姻生活，还不至于让你恨屋及乌，连一切俱乐部都厌恶了吧？”

===Original===
Gilbert made no answer, nor did they return again to the subject until they were ensconced in the spacious smoking-room of the Junior Terriers.

For two hours the men sat there, Gilbert questioning eagerly, pointedly, jotting down notes upon a sheet of paper. The other answered, often with some difficulty, the running fire of questions which his friend put.

“I didn’t know how little I knew,” confessed the young man ruefully, as Gilbert wrote down the last answer to the very last question. “What an encyclopædic questioner you are; you’re a born examiner, Gilbert.”

Gilbert smiled faintly as he slipped the sheet of paper into his pocket.

===Chinese===
吉尔伯特没有作声。直到两人在小猎犬俱乐部（Junior Terriers）宽敞的吸烟室里落了座，他们才又回到这个话题。

两人在那里坐了两个钟头，吉尔伯特提问提得又急又刁，在一张纸上飞快地记着。另一位则常常费着劲，招架朋友连珠炮似的发问。

“我都不知道自己知道得这么少。”等吉尔伯特把最后一问的答案记完，年轻人懊丧地坦白道，“你这提问的可真是百科全书式的考官；吉尔伯特，你天生是块当主考的料。”

吉尔伯特把那张纸塞进口袋，淡淡一笑。

===Original===
“By the way,” he said, as they were leaving the club, “I made my will this morning and I want you to be my executor.”

Leslie pushed his hat back with a groan.

“You’re the most cheerless bird I’ve met for quite a long time,” he said in exasperation. “You were married yesterday, you’re wandering round today with a face as long as an undertaker’s tout—I understand such interesting and picturesque individuals exist in the East End of London—you’ve chucked up the billet that’s bringing you in quite a lot of money, you’ve discussed investments, and you’ve made your will. You’re a most depressing devil!”

===Chinese===
“对了，”临出俱乐部时他说，“今天早上我立了遗嘱，我想请你做我的遗嘱执行人。”

莱斯利把帽子往后一推，呻吟了一声。

“你可真是我许久以来见过的最没喜兴的鸟人。”他气恼地说，“昨天刚结的婚，今天就拉着一张殡葬掮客似的长脸满街晃——据说伦敦东区真有这类有趣而生动的角色——把一份数目可观的差事给辞了，张口闭口谈投资，还立了遗嘱。你这个家伙，丧气透了！”

===Original===
Again Gilbert smiled: he was grimly amused. He shook hands with the young man before the club and called a taxicab to him.

“I’m going to St. John’s Wood. I suppose you’re not going my way?”

“I am relieved to hear that you are going to St. John’s Wood,” said the other with mock politeness. “I feared you were going to the nearest crematorium.”

===Chinese===
吉尔伯特又笑了：他给逗得想笑，笑里却带着冷。他在俱乐部门前同年轻人握手，替他叫了一辆出租车。

“我去圣约翰林（St. John’s Wood）。你不顺路吧？”

“听说你是去圣约翰林，我就放心了。”对方假装客气地说，“我还当你要去最近的火葬场呢。”

===Original===
Gilbert found his wife in the study on his return. She was sitting on the big settee reading. The stress of the previous night had left no mark upon her beautiful face. She favoured him with a smile. Instinctively they had both adopted the attitude which best met the circumstances. Her respect for him had increased, even in that short space of time; he had so well mastered himself in that moment of terror—terror which in an indefinable way had communicated itself to her. He had met her the next morning at breakfast cheerfully; but she did not doubt that he had spent a sleepless night, for his eyes were heavy and tired, and in spite of his geniality his voice was sharp, as are the voices of men who have cheated Nature.

===Chinese===
吉尔伯特回到家，在书房里找到了妻子。她正坐在大沙发上读书。前一夜的风波没在她美丽的脸上留下任何痕迹。她朝他嫣然一笑。两人都不约而同地摆出了最合乎眼下处境的姿态。即便在这样短的时日里，她对他的敬意也加深了；在那惊恐的一刻——那股说不清道不明地传染到她身上的惊恐——他把自制力保持得那么好。第二天早晨吃早餐时，他谈笑自若地见了她；可她毫不怀疑他一夜无眠，因为他的眼泡沉重倦怠，而且尽管他一团和气，嗓音却发紧，像所有违逆了自然、亏欠了睡眠的人那样。

===Original===
He walked straight to his desk now.

“Do you want to be alone?” she asked.

He looked up with a start.

“No, no,” he said hastily, “I’ve no wish to be alone. I’ve a little work to do, but you won’t bother me. You ought to know,” he said with an affectation of carelessness, “that I am resigning my post.”

“Your post!” she repeated.

===Chinese===
这会儿他径直走向书桌。

“你想一个人待着吗？”她问。

他一惊，抬起头来。

“不，不，”他赶紧说，“我不想一个人待着。我有点事要办，你不会打扰我的。你该知道了，”他装出满不在乎的样子说，“我要辞去公职了。”

“你的公职！”她重复道。

===Original===
“Yes; I find I have so much to do, and the Foreign Office takes up so much of my time that I really can’t spare, that it came to a question of giving up that or something else.”

He did not enlighten her as to what that “something else” was, nor could she guess. Already he was an enigma to her; she found, strange though it seemed to her, a new interest in him. That there was some tragedy in his life, a tragedy unsuspected by her, she did not doubt. He had told her calmly and categorically the story of his disinheritance; at his request, she had put the whole of that story into a letter which she had addressed to her mother. She felt no qualms, no inward quaking, at the prospect of the inevitable encounter, though Mrs. Cathcart would be enraged beyond reason.

===Chinese===
“是的；我发现要办的事太多，外交部占去我太多时间，实在匀不出来，于是就只剩一个问题：要么舍那个，要么舍别的。”

至于那“别的”是什么，他没有点破，她也猜不着。他在她眼里已是一团谜；她发觉——尽管自己都觉得奇怪——自己对他生出了新的兴味。她毫不怀疑，他的生活里藏着某桩悲剧，一桩她无从窥见的悲剧。他把被剥夺继承权的原委平静而条理分明地告诉了她；应他的要求，她把整个经过写成一封信，寄给了她的母亲。一想到那场免不了的交锋，她心里既无愧疚，也不发怵，尽管卡思卡特夫人（Mrs. Cathcart）准会气得失去理智。

===Original===
Edith smiled a little to herself as she had stuck down the flap of the envelope. This was poetic justice, though she herself might be a lifelong sufferer by reason of her worldly parent’s schemings. She had hoped that as a result of that letter, posted early in the morning, her mother would have called and the interview would have been finished before her husband returned. But Gilbert had been in the house half an hour when the blow fell. The tinkle of the hall bell brought the girl to her feet: she had been waiting, her ears strained, for that aggressive ring.

===Chinese===
粘上信封口的时候，伊迪丝独自微微笑了。这真是一场因果报应，尽管因为这位满肚子世俗算计的母亲的谋划，她自己也许要做一辈子的受难者。她原本指望，这封一早寄出的信能让母亲赶在丈夫回来之前登门，把那场会面了结掉。可吉尔伯特到家才半个钟头，灾祸就砸了下来。门铃一响，姑娘霍地站了起来：她一直竖着耳朵，等着那挑衅的铃声。

===Original===
She herself flew down the stairs to open the door.

Mrs. Cathcart entered without a word, and as the girl closed the door behind her she turned.

“Where is that precious husband of yours?” she asked in a choked voice.

“My husband is in his study,” said the girl calmly. “Do you want him, mother?”

“Do I want him?” she repeated in a choked voice.

Edith saw the glare in the woman’s eyes, saw, too, the pinched and haggard cheek. For one brief moment she pitied this woman, who had seen all her dreams shattered at a moment when she had hoped that their realisation was inevitable.

===Chinese===
她亲自飞奔下楼去开门。

卡思卡特夫人一言不发地进了门，等姑娘在她身后关上门，她转过身来。

“你那位金贵的丈夫在哪儿？”她声音哽咽地问。

“我丈夫在书房里。”姑娘平静地说，“您要见他吗，母亲？”

“我要见他？”她声音哽咽地又问了一遍。

伊迪丝看见这女人眼里喷着火，也看见那枯瘦憔悴的面颊。有一瞬，她可怜起这个女人来——就在她以为美梦成真已是板上钉钉的时刻，所有的梦都碎了。

===Original===
“Does he know I am coming?”

“I think he rather expects you,” said the girl dryly.

“I will see him by myself,” said Mrs. Cathcart, turning halfway up the stairs.

“You will see him with me, mother, or you will not see him at all,” said the girl.

“You will do as I tell you, Edith,” stormed the woman.

The girl smiled.

===Chinese===
“他知道我要来吗？”

“我想他正等着您呢。”姑娘冷冷地说。

“我一个人去见他。”卡思卡特夫人说着，在楼梯半腰转过身。

“您要么跟我一起见他，母亲，要么干脆别见。”姑娘说。

“你给我听命行事，伊迪丝。”女人勃然大怒。

姑娘笑了。

===Original===
“Mother,” she said gently, “you have ceased to have any right to direct me. You have handed me over to another guardian whose claims are greater than yours.”

It was not a good preparation for the interview that was to follow. Edith recognised this even as she opened the door and ushered her mother in.

===Chinese===
“母亲，”她温柔地说，“您已经无权再支配我了。您把我交给了另一位监护人，他的权利比您的大。”

这对接下来那场会面可不是什么好的铺垫。开门引母亲进去的时候，伊迪丝就明白这一点。

===Original===
When Gilbert saw who his visitor was he rose with a little bow. He did not offer his hand. He knew something of what this woman was feeling.

“Won’t you sit down, Mrs. Cathcart?” he said.

“I’ll stand for what I have to say,” she snapped. “Now, what is the meaning of this?” She threw down the letter which the girl had written, and which she had read and reread until every word was engraven on her mind. “Is it true,” she asked fiercely, “that you are a poor man? That you have deceived us? That you have lied your way into a marriage—”

===Chinese===
吉尔伯特看清来客是谁，起身微微一躬。他没有伸出手去。这女人的心情，他多少能体会。

“您不坐吗，卡思卡特夫人？”他说。

“我要说的话，站着说。”她厉声道，“我问你，这是什么意思？”她把那封信摔了下来——正是姑娘写的那封，她读了又读，每个字都已刻进脑子。“是不是真的，”她凶狠地质问，“你是个穷光蛋？你骗了我们？你是靠撒谎骗进这门婚事的——”

===Original===
He held up his hand.

“You seem to forget, Mrs. Cathcart,” he said with dignity, “that the question of my position has already been discussed by you and me, and you have been most emphatic in impressing upon me the fact that no worldly considerations would weigh with you.”

“Worldly!” she sneered. “What do you mean by worldly, Mr. Standerton? Are you not in the world? Do you not live in a house and eat bread and butter that costs money? Do you not use motorcars that require money for their upkeep? Whilst I am living in the world and you are living in the world worldly considerations will always count. I thought you were a rich man; you’re a beggar.”

===Chinese===
他举起一只手。

“您似乎忘了，卡思卡特夫人，”他庄重地说，“我的境况问题，你我早已谈过，而且您曾经再三向我强调：任何世俗的考量都左右不了您。”

“世俗！”她冷笑，“你说的世俗是什么意思，斯坦德顿先生？你不活在世上吗？你不住房子、不吃要花钱买的黄油面包吗？你不用要花钱养的汽车吗？只要我还活在世上、你也活在世上，世俗的考量就永远作数。我以为你是个有钱人，你是个叫花子。”

===Original===
He smiled a little contemptuously.

“A pretty mess you’ve made of it,” she said harshly. “You’ve got a woman who doesn’t love you—I suppose you know that?”

He bowed.

===Chinese===
他微露讥色地笑了笑。

“你可把事情弄得一团糟。”她刻薄地说，“你娶了个不爱你的女人——这个我料你是知道的吧？”

他躬了躬身。

===Original===
“I know all that, Mrs. Cathcart,” he said. “I knew the worst when I learnt that. The fact that you so obviously planned the marriage because you thought that I was Sir John Standerton’s heir does not hurt me, because I have met so many women like you, only”—he shrugged his shoulders—“I must confess that I thought you were a little different to the rest of worldly mothers—forgive me if I use that word again. But you are not any better—you may be a little worse,” he said, his thoughtful eyes upon her face.

He was looking at her with a curious something which the woman could not quite understand in his eyes. She had seen that look somewhere, and in spite of herself she shivered. The anger died away in fear.

“I wanted you to postpone this wedding,” he went on softly. “I had an especial reason, a reason I will not give you, but which will interest you in a few months’ time. But you were fearful of losing your rich son-in-law. I didn’t realise then that that was your fear. I have satisfied myself—it really doesn’t matter how,” he said steadily, “that you are more responsible than I for this good match.”

===Chinese===
“这些我全知道，卡思卡特夫人。”他说，“知道这一点时，最坏的我都明白了。您当年那样明显地筹谋这门婚事，是因为把我当成了约翰·斯坦德顿爵士的继承人，这一事实伤不着我，因为我见过的您这样的女人太多了。只是——”他耸了耸肩——“我必须承认，我原以为您跟其余那些世俗的母亲们有点不同——恕我又用了这个词。可您并不比她们强——说不定还更糟些。”他说着，若有所思的目光停在她脸上。

他望着她，眼里有一种这女人捉摸不透的东西。她在哪儿见过这种眼神，不由自主地打了个寒噤。怒气在恐惧中熄灭了。

“我原是要你推迟这门婚事的。”他柔声接着说，“我有一个特别的理由，一个我不会告诉你的理由，不过几个月之后，它自会引起你的兴味。可你当时生怕丢了这位阔女婿。我那时还没意识到你怕的是这个。如今我已经弄清楚了——用什么法子弄清楚的无关紧要，”他语气平稳地说，“这门‘好亲事’，你比我的责任更大。”

===Original===
He was a changed man. Mrs. Cathcart in her gusty rage could recognise this: there was a new soul, a new spirit, a new determination, and—that was it!—a new and terrible ferocity which shone from his eyes and for the moment hardened his face till it was almost terrible to look upon.

“Your daughter married me under a misapprehension. She told you all that I had to tell—almost all,” he corrected himself, “and I anticipated this visit. Had you not come I should have sent for you. Your daughter is as free as the air as far as I am concerned. I suppose your worldliness extends to a knowledge of the law? She can sue for a divorce tomorrow, and attain it without any difficulty and with little publicity.”

===Chinese===
他已换了一个人。卡思卡特夫人纵然怒火翻腾，也看得出这一点：一个新的灵魂、一种新的精神、一股新的决绝，还有——对了，就是这个！——一种新的、骇人的凶悍，从他眼里透出来，一时间绷紧了他的脸，绷得几乎令人生畏。

“你女儿是在误会之下嫁给我的。她已把我该说的一切都告诉了你——几乎是一切。”他更正道，“这次来访我也料到了。你今天不来，我也要打发人去请你。就我而言，你女儿自由得像空气一样。想必你的世故也包含一点法律常识？她明天就可以起诉离婚，不费吹灰之力就能判下来，还不至于闹得满城风雨。”

===Original===
A gleam of hope came to the woman’s face.

“I never thought of that,” she said half to herself. She turned quickly to her daughter, for she was a woman of action. “Get your things and come with me.”

Edith did not stir. She stood the other side of the table, half facing her husband and wholly facing her mother.

“You hear what Mr. Standerton says,” said Mrs. Cathcart irritably. “He has opened a way of escape to you. What he says is true. A divorce can be obtained with no difficulty. Come with me. I will send for your clothes.”

Edith still did not move.

===Chinese===
这女人脸上掠过一线希望。

“这个我倒没想到。”她半是自语地说，随即飞快转向女儿，她本是个说干就干的女人，“收拾你的东西，跟我走。”

伊迪丝纹丝不动。她站在桌子另一侧，半侧着身子对丈夫，正面对着母亲。

“你听见斯坦德顿先生的话了。”卡思卡特夫人焦躁地说，“他给你开了一条脱身的路。他说的不假。离婚不费事就能办成。跟我走吧，你的衣裳我打发人来取。”

伊迪丝依然不动。

===Original===
Mrs. Cathcart, watching her, saw her features soften one by one, saw the lips part in a smile and the head fall back as peal after peal of clear laughter rang through the room.

“Oh, mother!” The infinite contempt of the voice struck the woman like the lash of a whip. “You don’t know me! Go back with you? Divorce him? You’re mad! If he had been a rich man indeed I might; but for the time being, though I do not love him, and though I should not blame him and do not blame him if he does not love me, my lot is cast with his, my place is here.”

“Melodrama!” said the elder woman angrily.

“There’s a lot of truth and no end of decency in melodrama, Mrs. Cathcart,” said Gilbert.

===Chinese===
卡思卡特夫人盯着她，只见她的五官一处一处舒展开来，只见她唇角绽出笑意，头向后一仰，一阵阵清亮的笑声在屋里回荡。

“哦，母亲！”话音里那无尽的轻蔑，像鞭子一样抽在这女人身上，“你不了解我！跟你回去？跟他离婚？你疯了！他若真是富人，我说不定会的；可眼下，虽然我不爱他，虽然他若不爱我，我无权怪他、也确实不怪他，我的命已同他系在一起，我的位置在这里。”

“真是戏文！”年长的女人怒道。

“戏文里也大有真话，更不缺体面，卡思卡特夫人。”吉尔伯特说。

===Original===
His mother-in-law stood livid with rage, then turning, flung out of the room, and they heard the front door slam behind her.

They looked at each other, this strangely-married pair, for the space of a few seconds, and then Gilbert held out his hand.

“Thank you,” he said.

The girl dropped her eyes.

“You have nothing to thank me for,” she said listlessly. “I have done you too much wrong for one little act to wipe out all the effects of my selfishness.”

===Chinese===
他的岳母气得面色铁青，站了片刻，猛地转身冲出房间，接着他们听见前门在她身后砰地关上。

这对结得离奇的夫妻彼此对望了几秒钟，然后吉尔伯特伸出了手。

“谢谢你。”他说。

姑娘垂下了眼睛。

“你没有什么可谢我的。”她无精打采地说，“我欠你的太多，区区一个小举动，抹不掉我自私自利的一切后果。”

## IV The “Melody in F” / IV 《F大调旋律》

===Original===
Gilbert Standerton was dressing slowly before his glass when Leslie was announced. That individual was radiant and beautiful to behold as became the best man at the wedding of an old friend.

Leslie Frankfort was one of those fortunate individuals who combine congenial work with the enjoyment of a private income. He was the junior partner of a firm of big stockbrokers in the City, a firm which dealt only with the gilt-edged markets of finance. He enjoyed in common with Gilbert a taste for classical music, and this was the bond which had first drawn the two men together.

He came into the room, deposited his silk hat carefully upon a chair, and sat on the edge of the bed, offering critical suggestions to the prospective bridegroom.

===Chinese===
仆人通报莱斯利到了时，吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿正对着穿衣镜慢条斯理地着装。这位仁兄容光焕发、仪表堂堂，正是老朋友婚礼上的伴郎该有的模样。

莱斯利·弗兰克福特属于那种幸运儿：既有合意的营生，又坐享私人进项。他是伦敦城里一家大证券经纪行的初级合伙人，这家商行只做金融界的金边市场。他与吉尔伯特同好古典音乐，正是这条纽带最早把两人拉到了一起。

他走进房间，把大礼帽小心地搁在椅子上，坐到床沿上，对准新郎官评头论足地提起建议来。

===Original===
“By the way,” he said suddenly, “I saw that old man of yours yesterday.”

Gilbert looked round.

“You mean Springs, the musician?”

The other nodded.

“He was playing for the amusement of a theatre queue—a fine old chap.”

“Very,” said Gilbert absently.

===Chinese===
“对了，”他突然说，“昨天我看见你那位老头儿了。”

吉尔伯特回过头来。

“你是说斯普林斯（Springs），那位乐师？”

对方点了点头。

“他在剧院门口给排队的人拉琴取乐——位挺好的老先生。”

“是挺好。”吉尔伯特心不在焉地说。

===Original===
He paused in his dressing, took up a letter from the table, and handed it to the other.

“Am I to read it?” asked Leslie.

Gilbert nodded.

“There’s nothing to read, as a matter of fact,” he said; “it’s my uncle’s wedding present.”

The young man opened the envelope and extracted the pink slip. He looked at the amount and whistled.

“One hundred pounds,” he said. “Good Lord! That won’t pay the upkeep of your car for a quarter. I suppose you told Mrs. Cathcart?”

===Chinese===
他停下穿衣的手，从桌上拿起一封信，递给对方。

“要我读吗？”莱斯利问。

吉尔伯特点了点头。

“其实没什么可读的，”他说，“这是我叔叔的结婚礼物。”

年轻人拆开信封，抽出那张粉色单据。他看了看数额，打了个呼哨。

“一百镑。”他说，“老天爷！这都不够你那辆车一个季度的开销。想必你告诉过卡思卡特夫人（Mrs. Cathcart）了吧？”

===Original===
Gilbert shook his head.

“No,” he said shortly, “I intended telling her but I haven’t. I am perfectly satisfied in my own mind, Leslie, that we are doing her an injustice. She has been so emphatic about money. And after all, I’m not a pauper,” he said with a smile.

“You’re worse than a pauper,” said Leslie earnestly; “a man with six hundred a year is the worst kind of pauper I know.”

“Why?”

“You’ll never bring your tastes below a couple of thousand, you’ll never raise your income above six hundred—plus your Foreign Office job, that’s only another six hundred.”

===Chinese===
吉尔伯特摇了摇头。

“没有，”他简短地说，“本打算告诉她，可还没告诉。莱斯利，我心里完全相信，我们是冤枉她了。她一直把金钱的事说得那样斩钉截铁。再说，我到底不是个叫花子。”他微笑着说。

“你比叫花子还惨。”莱斯利一本正经地说，“年入六百镑的人，是我所知道的最惨的一种叫花子。”

“此话怎讲？”

“你的品位永远降不到两千镑以下，你的进项却永远涨不到六百镑以上——加上外交部（Foreign Office）那份差事，也不过再添六百。”

===Original===
“Work,” said the other.

“Work!” said the other scornfully, “you don’t earn money by work. You earn money by scheming, by getting the better of the other fellow. You’re too softhearted to make money, my son.”

“You seem to make money,” said Gilbert with a little smile.

Leslie shook his head vigorously.

“I’ve never made a penny in my life,” he confessed with some enjoyment. “No, I have got some very stout, unimaginative senior partners who do all the moneymaking. I merely take dividends at various periods of the year. But then I was in luck. What is your money, by the way?”

===Chinese===
“还可以工作嘛。”对方说。

“工作！”对方嗤之以鼻，“钱不是靠工作挣来的。钱靠的是算计，是压过别人一头。你心肠太软，挣不了钱，我的儿。”

“你好像就挣到了钱。”吉尔伯特微微一笑。

莱斯利使劲摇头。

“我这辈子一个子儿都没挣过。”他坦白得还挺享受，“不，我有几位非常结实、毫无想象力的资深合伙人，赚钱的活儿全归他们。我不过一年里分几回红利罢了。可我这叫走运。对了，你那笔钱是怎么回事？”

===Original===
Gilbert was in the act of tying his cravat. He looked up with a little frown.

“What do you mean?” he asked.

“I mean, is it in securities—does it continue after your death?”

The little frown still knit the brows of the other.

“No,” he said shortly, “after my death there is scarcely enough to bring in a hundred and fifty a year. I am only enjoying a life interest on this particular property.”

Leslie whistled.

“Well, I hope, old son, that you’re well insured.”

===Chinese===
吉尔伯特正打着领结。他微微皱眉，抬起头来。

“你指什么？”他问。

“我是说，它是证券形式的吗——你死后还继续有吗？”

那缕愁云仍锁在吉尔伯特的眉间。

“不，”他简短地说，“我死后，那份产业每年带来的还不足一百五十镑。我享有的只是这份产业的终身受益权。”

莱斯利打了个呼哨。

“那么，老伙计，我只希望你保险买得足足的。”

===Original===
The other man made no attempt to interrupt as Leslie, arguing with great fluency and skill on the duties and responsibilities of heads of families, delivered himself of his views on insurance and upon the uninsured.

“Some Johnnies are so improvident,” he said. “I knew a man—”

He stopped suddenly. He had caught a reflection of Gilbert’s face in the glass. It was haggard and drawn, it seemed the face of a man in mortal agony. Leslie sprang up.

“What on earth is the matter, my dear chap?” he cried. He came to the other’s side and laid his hand on his shoulder.

“Oh, it’s nothing—nothing, Leslie,” said Gilbert.

===Chinese===
莱斯利就一家之长的义务与责任滔滔雄辩、头头是道，把他对保险以及对不买保险之人的见解倾囊倒出，对方一次也没有打断他。

“有些家伙就是这么不顾将来，”他说，“我认识一个人——”

他突然住了口。他从镜子里瞥见了吉尔伯特的脸。那张脸枯槁扭曲，像是一个承受着死亡剧痛的人。莱斯利腾地站了起来。

“你到底怎么了，亲爱的？”他喊道，走到对方面前，把手搭上他的肩头。

“哦，没什么——没什么，莱斯利。”吉尔伯特说。

===Original===
He passed his hand before his eyes as though to wipe away some ugly vision.

“I’m afraid I’ve been rather a careless devil. You see, I depended too much upon uncle’s money. I ought to be insured.”

“That isn’t worrying you surely?” asked the other in astonishment.

“It worries me a bit,” said Gilbert moodily. “One never knows, you know—”

He stood looking thoughtfully at the other, his hands thrust into his pockets.

“I wish to heaven this wedding had been postponed!”

Leslie laughed.

===Chinese===
他抬手在眼前抹了一把，仿佛要拂去某个狰狞的幻象。

“恐怕我这个人一直太不知检点了。你也知道，我把叔叔的钱指望得太狠了。我早该买保险的。”

“你该不会在为这个发愁吧？”对方愕然问。

“是有几分愁。”吉尔伯特郁郁地说，“谁知道呢，你说是不是——”

他站在那儿，双手插兜，若有所思地望着对方。

“这婚礼要是推迟了才好！”

莱斯利笑了。

===Original===
“It’s about time you were married,” he said. “What a jumpy ass you are.”

He looked at his watch.

“You’d better hurry up, or you’ll be losing this bride of yours. After all, this isn’t a day for gloom; it’s the day of days, my friend.”

He saw the soft look that came into Gilbert’s eyes, and felt satisfied with his work.

“Yes, there is that,” said Gilbert Standerton softly. “I forgot all my blessings. God bless her!” he said under his breath.

===Chinese===
“你也早该结婚了。”他说，“瞧你这副神经兮兮的蠢样。”

他看了看表。

“你最好抓紧点，不然新娘子可要跑了。再说，今天可不是愁眉苦脸的日子；这是一生中千载难逢的日子，我的朋友。”

他看见吉尔伯特眼里浮起一抹温柔，对自己的工作成果颇感满意。

“是啊，倒也是。”吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿柔声说，“我把自己的福气全忘了。上帝保佑她！”他低声说。

===Original===
As they were leaving the house, Gilbert asked—

“I suppose you have a list of the guests who are to be present?”

“Yes,” said the other, “Mrs. Cathcart was most duteous.”

“Will Dr. Barclay-Seymour be there?” asked the other carelessly.

“Barclay-Seymour—no, he won’t be there,” replied Leslie, “he’s the Leeds Johnnie, isn’t he? He went up from London last night. What’s this talk of your having run away the other night?”

“It was an important engagement,” said Gilbert hurriedly, “I had a man to see; I couldn’t very well put him off—”

Leslie realised that he had asked an embarrassing question and changed the subject.

===Chinese===
两人正要出门时，吉尔伯特问——

“出席宾客的名单，你手头有吧？”

“有，”对方说，“卡思卡特夫人办事最是尽心。”

“巴克利-西摩医生（Dr. Barclay-Seymour）会去吗？”对方随口问。

“巴克利-西摩——不，他去不了。”莱斯利答道，“就是利兹那位老兄，对吧？他昨晚就从伦敦北上了。对了，外头传你前天夜里离家出走，是怎么回事？”

“有个要紧的约会。”吉尔伯特急忙说，“我得见一个人；实在不便推掉——”

莱斯利意识到自己问了个让人难堪的问题，便转了话头。

===Original===
“By the way,” he said, “I shouldn’t mention this matter of the money to Mrs. Cathcart till after you’ve both settled down.”

“I won’t,” said Gilbert grimly.

On the way to the church he reviewed all the troubles that were besetting him and faced them squarely. Perhaps it would not be as bad as he thought. He was ever prone to take an exaggerated and a worrying view of troubles. He had anticipated dangers, and time and time again his fears had been groundless. He had lived too long alone. A man ought to be married before he was thirty-two. That was his age. He had become cranky. He found consolation in uncomplimentary analysis till the church was reached.

===Chinese===
“对了，”他说，“钱的事，我看你们小两口安顿下来之前，先别跟卡思卡特夫人提。”

“我不会提的。”吉尔伯特沉着脸说。

去教堂的路上，他把缠身的种种烦恼一一过了一遍，正面直视它们。也许事情没有他想的那样糟。他就是有这么个毛病，总把烦恼往大处看、往坏处想。他预想过种种凶险，而他的恐惧一次次都落了空。他独身太久了。男人三十二岁前就该成家。三十二，正是他现在的年纪。他都变得古怪了。他一路靠这种不留情面的自我剖析聊以自慰，直到教堂在望。

===Original===
It was a dream, that ceremony: the crowded pews, the organ, the white-robed choir, the rector and his assistants; the coming of Edith, so beautiful, so ethereal in her bridal robes; the responses, the kneeling and the rising—it was all unreal.

He had thought that the music would have made a lasting impression on him; he had been at some pains to choose it, and had had several consultations with the organist. But at the end of the service when he began to walk, still in his dream, towards the vestry, he could not recall one single bar. He had a dim recollection of the fact that above the altar was a stained glass window, one tiny pane of which had been removed, evidently on account of a breakage.

===Chinese===
那场仪式恍如一梦：座无虚席的长椅，管风琴，白袍的诗班，主任牧师和他的助手们；伊迪丝款款而来，一身新娘礼服，那样美，那样飘逸出尘；应答，跪下，起立——一切都恍恍惚惚，不像真的。

他原以为那音乐会给他留下难忘的印象；选曲时他颇费了一番心思，还与琴师磋商过好几回。可礼拜结束时，他仍恍恍惚惚地朝法衣室走去，脑中竟连一个小节都回忆不起来。他只依稀记得，祭坛上方有一扇彩绘玻璃窗，其中一小格玻璃被卸了下来，显然是因为破了。

===Original===
He was back in the house, sitting at the be-flowered table, listening in some confusion to the speeches and the bursts of laughter which assailed each speaker as he made his point: now he was on his feet, talking easily, without effort, but what words he used, or why people applauded, or why they smiled he could not say.

Once in its course he had looked down at the delicate face by his side, and had met those solemn eyes of hers, less fearful today, it seemed, than ever he had seen them. He had felt for her hand and had held it, cold and unresponsive, in his.﻿﻿

“An excellent speech,” said Leslie.

They were in the drawing-room after the breakfast.

“You’re quite an orator.”

“Am I?” said Gilbert.

===Chinese===
他又回到了宅子里，坐在摆满鲜花的婚宴桌前，恍恍惚惚地听着一场场致辞，听着每位发言者说到妙处时爆出的哄堂大笑：转眼他自己站了起来，谈笑自若，毫不费力，可他说了些什么，人们为什么鼓掌，为什么微笑，他一概说不上来。

宴席间，他曾低头看身边那张清秀的脸，迎上她那双庄重的眼睛——今天看来，比他以往见过的任何时候都少了几分惊惧。他摸索到她的手，把它握在自己手里，那只手凉凉的，毫无回应。……

“一段好致辞。”莱斯利说。

婚宴之后，他们都在客厅里。

“你活脱脱一位演说家。”

“是吗？”吉尔伯特说。

===Original===
He was beginning to wake again. The drawing-room was real, these people were real, the jokes, the badinage, and the wit which flew from tongue to tongue—all these things were of a life he knew.

“Whew!” He wiped his forehead and breathed a deep sigh. He felt like a man who had regained consciousness after an anaesthetic that did not quite take effect. A painless and a beautiful experience, but of another world, and it was not he, so he told himself, who had knelt at the altar rail.

===Chinese===
他开始重新醒过来。客厅是真的，这些人是真的，那些玩笑、打趣、唇枪舌剑间飞来飞去的妙语——这一切都属于他所熟悉的那个生活。

“嚯！”他抹了把额头，长长吐出一口气。他觉得自己像刚从一场没完全见效的麻醉中苏醒过来的人。一场无痛而美妙的经历，却恍如隔世，而且他对自己说，跪在祭坛栏杆前的，并不是他。

---

===Original===
Officially the honeymoon was to be spent at Harrogate, actually it was to be spent in London. They preserved the pretence of catching a train, and drove to King’s Cross.

No word was spoken throughout that journey. Gilbert felt the restriction, and did not challenge it or seek to overcome it. The girl was naturally silent. She had so much to say in the proper place and at the proper time. He saw the old fear come back to her eyes, was hurt by the unconscious and involuntary shrinking when his hand touched hers.

The carriage was dismissed at King’s Cross. A taxicab was engaged, and they drove to the house in St. John’s Wood. It was empty, the servants had been sent away on a holiday, but it was a perfectly fitted little mansion. There were electric cookers, and every laboursaving appliance the mind of man could devise, or a young man with great expectations and no particular idea of the value of money could acquire.

===Chinese===
名义上，蜜月要在哈罗盖特（Harrogate）度过；实际上，将在伦敦度过。两人做足赶火车的样子，驱车到了国王十字车站（King’s Cross）。

一路上两人一言未发。吉尔伯特感到了这份拘束，却没有去打破它，也没有设法化解。姑娘天生沉默。她有那么多话，要在合适的地点、合适的时候说。他看见旧日的惊惧又回到她眼中；当他的手碰到她的手，她无意识的、不由自主的一缩，让他暗暗受伤。

马车在国王十字被打发走了。两人雇了辆出租车，驶向圣约翰林的那座宅子。宅子空着，仆人都被打发去休假了，但这是一座设施齐全的小公馆。屋里有电灶，有人类心智所能发明、或者说一个前程远大、又对钱的价值没什么概念的年轻人所能购置的每一件省力家什。

===Original===
This was to be one of the joys of the honeymoon, so Gilbert had told himself. She had willingly dispensed with her maid; he was ready to be man-of-all-work, to cook and to serve, leaving the rough work for the two new day servants he had employed to come in in the morning.

Yet it was with no sense of joyfulness that he led her from room to room, showed her the treasures of his household. A sense of apprehension of some coming trouble laid its hand upon his tongue, damped his spirit, and held him in temporary bondage.

The girl was self-possessed. She admired, criticised kindly, and rallied him gently upon his domesticity. But the strain was there all the time; there was a shadow which lay between them.

She went to her room to change. They had arranged to go out to dinner, and this programme they followed. Leslie Frankfort saw them in the dining hall of Princes, and pretended he didn’t know them. It was ten o’clock when they went back to their little house.

===Chinese===
蜜月的乐趣之一就该是这样，吉尔伯特曾这样对自己说。她欣然免去了随身女仆；他也乐意充当打杂的全仆，烧菜上菜，粗重活儿留给新雇的两个白班仆人，她们每天早晨上门来做。

然而，他领着她一间屋一间屋地走，向她展示家中的珍宝时，心里没有半点喜气。一种大祸将至的预感扼住了他的舌头，浇灭了他的兴致，把他暂时缚在了原地。

姑娘倒是镇定自若。她赞赏着，善意地品评着，还温柔地打趣他的居家男人做派。可那份紧张一直在；有一道阴影横在两人之间。

她回房换了衣服。两人本说好外出吃饭，也照办了。莱斯利·弗兰克福特在普林斯餐厅（Princes）的餐堂里望见了他们，假装不认识。他们回到小宅子时，已是十点钟。

===Original===
Gilbert went to his study; his wife had gone up to her room and had promised to come down for coffee. He went to work with all the skill which a pupil of Rahbat might be expected to display, and brewed two tiny little cups of Mocha. This he served on the table near the settee where she would sit﻿﻿… Then she came in.

He had been fast awakening from the dream of the morning. He was alive now. The dazement of that momentous ceremony had worn away. He rose and went a little way towards her. He would have taken her in his arms then and there, but this time the arm’s length was a reality. Her hand touched his breast, and the arm stiffened. He felt the rebuff in the act, and it seemed to him that his heart went cold, and that all the vague terrors of the previous days crystallised into one concrete and terrible truth. He knew all that she had to say before she spoke.

===Chinese===
吉尔伯特去了书房；妻子上楼回了自己房间，说好下来喝咖啡。他拿出拉巴特（Rahbat）门生该有的全部手艺，煮了两小杯摩卡，端到长沙发旁的桌上——她该坐的位置……这时她进来了。

他早已从早晨的梦里迅速醒来。他现在是清醒的。那场重大仪式带来的恍惚已经褪尽。他起身朝她迎上几步。他本想当场把她搂进怀里，可这一回，那一臂之隔成了真。她的手抵住他的胸口，那条胳膊僵住了。他从这动作里尝到了回绝，只觉得自己的心一下子凉了，连日来种种模糊的恐怖，霎时凝成一个具体而可怕的事实。她还没开口，他已知道她要说的全部。

===Original===
It was some time before she found the words she wanted, the opening was so difficult.

“Gilbert,” she said at last, “I am going to do a cowardly thing. It is only cowardly because I have not told you before.”

He motioned her to the settee.

He had woven a little romance for this moment, a dream scene which was never to be enacted. Here was the shattering.

“I won’t sit down,” she said, “I want all my strength to tell you what I have to tell you. If I hadn’t been an arrant coward I should have told you last night. I meant to tell you,” she said, “but you did not come.”

===Chinese===
过了好一会儿，她才找到想说的话，这开场实在太难了。

“吉尔伯特，”她终于开口，“我要做一件懦弱的事。它之所以懦弱，只因为我没有早告诉你。”

他示意她到长沙发上坐。

他曾为这一刻编织过一个小小的罗曼史，一场永远上演不了的梦境。此刻，梦碎了。

“我不坐。”她说，“我需要用尽全部力气，把该告诉你的事告诉你。昨晚我要不是个彻头彻尾的懦夫，就该告诉你了。我本打算告诉你的，”她说，“可你没有来。”

===Original===
He nodded.

“I know,” he said, almost impatiently. “I could not come. I did not wish—I could not come,” he repeated.

“You know what I have to tell you?” Her eyes were steadily fixed on his. “Gilbert, I do not love you.”

He nodded again.

“I know now,” he said.

“I never have loved you,” she said in tones of despair; “there never was any time when I regarded you as more than a dear friend. But—”

She wanted to tell him why, but a sense of loyalty to her mother kept her silent. She would take all the blame, for was she not blameworthy? For she, at least, was mistress of her own soul: had she wished, she could have taken a line of greater resistance than that which she had followed.

===Chinese===
他点了点头。

“我知道。”他说，语气近乎不耐烦，“我来不了。我不愿——我来不了。”他又说了一遍。

“你知道我要告诉你什么吗？”她的眼睛直直地望着他。“吉尔伯特，我不爱你。”

他又点了点头。

“我现在知道了。”他说。

“我从来就没有爱过你。”她的声音里满是绝望，“从来没有哪个时候，我把你看得比一位亲爱的朋友更多。可是——”

她想告诉他原委，可对母亲的一份忠心让她住了口。她甘愿领下全部罪责，难道她不正有可责之处吗？因为她至少是自己灵魂的主人：倘若她愿意，她本可以采取一条比这更有抵抗的姿态。

===Original===
“I married you,” she went on slowly, “because—because you are—rich—because you will be rich.”

Her voice dropped at the last word until it was husky. There was a hard fight going on within her. She wanted to tell the truth, and yet she did not want him to think so badly of her as that.

“For my money!” he repeated wonderingly.

“Yes, I—I wanted to marry a man with money. We have had—a very hard time.”

The confession came in little gasps; she had to frame every sentence before she spoke.

“You mustn’t blame mother, I was equally guilty; and I ought to have told you—I wanted to tell you.”

“I see,” he said calmly.

===Chinese===
“我嫁给你，”她缓缓接着说，“因为——因为你——富有——因为你将会富有。”

说到最后一个词，她的声音低哑了下去。她心里正进行着一场苦斗。她想说出真相，却又不愿他把她想得那样坏。

“为了我的钱！”他诧异地重复道。

“是的，我——我想嫁个有钱人。我们过了——很长一段苦日子。”

这番坦白是一口一口喘着气吐出来的；每说一句话，她都得先在心里打好腹稿。

“你别怪母亲，我同样有罪；我早就该告诉你——我也想告诉你的。”

“我明白了。”他平静地说。

===Original===
It is wonderful what reserves of strength come at a man’s bidding. In this terrible crisis, in this moment when the whole of his life’s happiness was shattered, when the fabric of his dream was crumbling like a house of paper, he could be judicial, almost phlegmatic.

He saw her sway, and springing to her side caught her.

“Sit down,” he said quietly.

She obeyed without protest. He settled her in the corner of the settee, pushed a cushion almost viciously behind her, and walked back to the fireplace.

“So you married me for my money,” he said, and laughed.

It was not without its amusing side, this situation.

“By Heaven, what a comedy—what a comedy!” He laughed again. “My poor child,” he said, with unaccustomed irony, “I am sorry for you, for you have secured neither husband nor money!”

===Chinese===
人的意志一召唤，竟能唤来何等惊人的力量储备，真是奇妙。在这可怕的关口，在他毕生幸福毁于一旦的时刻，在他梦的大厦如纸屋般崩塌的时刻，他竟能像法官一样冷静，近乎无动于衷。

他看见她身形一晃，纵身抢到她身边扶住了她。

“坐下。”他轻声说。

她顺从地坐下了，没有异议。他把她安置在长沙发的一角，几乎带点狠劲地把一只靠垫塞到她背后，然后走回壁炉前。

“这么说，你是为了我的钱才嫁给我的。”他说着，笑了。

这局面倒也不是没有滑稽的一面。

“老天，多好的一出喜剧——多好的喜剧！”他又笑了一声。“我可怜的孩子，”他以一种平日少有的讥诮说，“我为你难过，因为你丈夫和钱，一样都没捞着！”

===Original===
She looked up at him quickly.

“Nor money,” she repeated.

There was only interest that he saw in her eyes. There was no hint of disappointment. He knew the truth, more than she had told him: it was not she who desired a fortune, it was this mother of hers, this domineering, worldly woman.

“No husband and no money,” he repeated savagely, in spite of the almost yearning desire which was in him to spare her.

“And worse than that”—with two rapid strides he was at the desk which separated them, and bent across it, leaning heavily—“not only have you no husband, and not only is there no money, but—”

===Chinese===
她飞快地抬头看他。

“钱也没捞着。”她重复道。

他在她眼里看到的只有兴味，没有半点失望的影子。他知道真相，比她说的更多：渴望那笔财产的不是她，而是她这位母亲，这个专横、世俗的女人。

“没有丈夫，也没有钱。”他狠狠地又说了一遍，尽管他心里几乎渴念着想放过她。

“比这还糟——”他两大步跨到隔开两人的书桌前，重重撑着桌沿俯下身去，“你不但没有丈夫，不但没有钱，而且——”

===Original===
He stopped as if he had been shot.

The girl, looking at him, saw his face go drawn and grey, saw the eyes staring wildly past her, the mouth open in tragic dismay. She got up quickly.

“What is it? What is it?” she whispered in alarm.

“My God!”

His voice was cracked; it was the voice of a man in terror. She half bent her head, listening. From somewhere beneath the window arose the soft, melancholy strains of a violin. The music rose and fell, sobbing and pulsating with passion beneath the magic of the player’s fingers. She stepped to a window and looked out. On the edge of the pavement a girl was playing, a girl whose poverty of dress did not hide her singular beauty.

===Chinese===
他猛地顿住，像中了枪一样。

姑娘望着他，看见他的脸扭曲发灰，眼睛狂乱地瞪着她身后，嘴大张着，满是悲剧性的惊惶。她迅速站起来。

“怎么了？怎么了？”她惊慌地低声问。

“我的上帝！”

他的嗓音劈了；那是一个人在恐怖中的声音。她微微俯首，侧耳去听。窗下什么地方，响起小提琴轻柔而忧郁的旋律。乐声起伏着，如泣如诉，在演奏者魔指之下随激情搏动。她走到窗前向外望去。人行道边上，一个姑娘正在拉琴，寒素的衣衫掩不住她出众的美。

===Original===
The light from the street lamp fell upon her pale face, her eyes were fixed on the window where Gilbert was standing.

Edith looked at her husband. He was shaking like a man with fever.

“The ‘Melody in F,’ ” he whispered. “My God! The ‘Melody in F’—and on my wedding day!”

===Chinese===
街灯的光落在她苍白的脸上，她的眼睛死死盯着吉尔伯特站立的那扇窗。

伊迪丝看看丈夫。他抖得像个发高烧的人。

“《F大调旋律》，”他喃喃道，“我的上帝！《F大调旋律》——偏偏在我结婚的日子！”

## X The Necklace / X 项链

===Original===
Mrs. Cathcart was considerably surprised to receive an invitation to the dinner. She had that morning sent her daughter a cheque for three hundred pounds which she had received from her broker, but as their letters had crossed, one event had no connection with the other.

She did not immediately decide to accept the invitation, she was not sure as to the terms on which she desired to remain with her new son-in-law.

She was, however (whatever might be her faults), a good strategist, and there was nothing to be gained by declining the invitation, and there might be some advantage in accepting.

She was surprised to meet Mr. Warrell, surprised and a little embarrassed; but now that her daughter knew everything there was no reason in the world why she should feel uncomfortable.

She took him in charge, as was her wont, from the moment she met him in the little drawing-room at the St. John’s Wood house.

===Chinese===
收到晚宴请柬，卡思卡特夫人（Mrs. Cathcart）相当意外。那天早晨她刚把一张三百镑的支票寄给女儿——那是她从经纪人那里收来的；不过两家的信在路上错过了，两件事互不相干。

她没有立刻决定赴宴；她想跟这位新女婿维持一种什么样的关系，自己还没拿定主意。

不过，她无论有多少毛病，终究是个高明的战略家：谢绝这场宴请捞不到任何好处，接受倒可能有些便宜。

见到沃雷尔先生（Mr. Warrell）她吃了一惊，又吃惊又有点尴尬；可如今她女儿什么都知道了，她实在没有理由再感到不自在。

从在圣约翰林宅子的小客厅里见到他那一刻起，她就照老习惯把他攥在了手心里。

===Original===
It was a pleasant dinner. Gilbert made a perfect host, he seemed to have revived within himself something of the old gay spirit. Warrell, remembering all that Mrs. Cathcart had told him, was on the qui vive to discover some evidence of dissension between husband and wife, the more anxious, perhaps, since he was before everything a professional man, to find justification for Mrs. Cathcart’s suggestion, that all was not going well with Gilbert.

Leslie Frankfort, a member of the party, had been questioned by his partner without the elder man eliciting any information which might help to dispel the doubt that was in Warrell’s mind.

Leslie Frankfort, that cheerful youth, was as much in the dark as his partner. It gave him some satisfaction to discover that at any rate there was no immediate prospect of ruin in his friend’s ménage.

The dinner was perfect, the food rare and chosen by an epicure, which indeed it was, as Gilbert had assisted his wife to prepare the menu.

===Chinese===
这是顿愉快的晚宴。吉尔伯特做东做得无可挑剔，仿佛在他身上又复活了几分往日那种快活的神采。沃雷尔想起卡思卡特夫人对他说过的种种，处处留神，想找出些夫妻失和的迹象；他毕竟首先是个专业人士，也许正因如此，才更急于为卡思卡特夫人的说法——吉尔伯特家事不顺——找到佐证。

席间的莱斯利·弗兰克福特也被合伙人盘问过，可这位年长者没能套出半点有助于消解他心头疑云的消息。

莱斯利·弗兰克福特这乐天的年轻人，跟他的合伙人一样蒙在鼓里。让他略感欣慰的是，至少看不出朋友的家事有立即倾覆的迹象。

晚宴无可挑剔，菜色珍美，行家手笔——事实也确乎如此，因为菜单是吉尔伯特帮着妻子一起拟的。

===Original===
The talk drifted idly, as talk does, at such a dinner party, around the topics which men and women were discussing at a thousand other dinner tables in England, and in the natural course of events it turned upon the startling series of burglaries that had been committed recently in London. That the talk should take this drift was more natural, perhaps, because Mrs. Cathcart had very boldly introduced the subject with reference to the burglary at Warrell’s.

“No, indeed,” said Mr. Warrell, shaking his head, “I regret to say we have no clue. The police have the matter in hand, but I’m afraid we shall never find the man, or men, who perpetrated the crime.”

“I don’t suppose they would be of much service to you if you found them,” said Gilbert quietly.

“I don’t know,” demurred the other. “We might possibly get the jewels back.”

Gilbert Standerton laughed, but stopped in the middle of it.

“Jewels?” he said.

===Chinese===
席间闲谈也如所有晚宴一样漫无目的地飘着，绕着英格兰千百张餐桌上人们正谈着的话题打转；顺着自然的走势，话题落到了近来伦敦一连串骇人的入室盗窃案上。而话题之所以拐向这里，恐怕还因为卡思卡特夫人相当大胆地借着沃雷尔事务所那桩失窃案，把话头引了过来。

“没有，实在没有。”沃雷尔先生摇着头说，“遗憾的是我们毫无线索。警方在经办此案，可我恐怕，干下这桩罪案的无论一人还是几人，我们永远抓不着了。”

“就算抓着了，我估摸着对你们也没多大用处。”吉尔伯特不动声色地说。

“那可说不准。”对方不以为然，“没准还能把珠宝追回来呢。”

吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿笑出声来，却笑到一半煞住了。

“珠宝？”他说。

===Original===
“Don’t you remember, Gilbert?” Leslie broke in. “I told you that we had a necklace in the safe, the property of a client, one of those gambling ladies who patronise us.”

A warning glance from his partner arrested him. The gambling lady herself was rather red, and shot a malevolent glance at the indiscreet young man.

“The necklace was mine,” she said acidly.

“Oh!” said Leslie, and found the conversation of no great interest to him.

Gilbert did not smile at his friend’s embarrassment.

“A necklace,” he repeated, “how curious—yours?”

“Mine,” repeated Mrs. Cathcart. “I placed it with Warrell’s for security. Precious fine security it proved,” she added.

===Chinese===
“你不记得了吗，吉尔伯特？”莱斯利插话，“我告诉过你，我们保险柜里有一条项链，是一位客户的财产，光顾我们的那种好赌的太太之一。”

合伙人一道警示的眼色止住了他。那位好赌的太太本人脸红得厉害，朝这个口无遮拦的年轻人恶狠狠地剜了一眼。

“那条项链是我的。”她尖酸地说。

“哦！”莱斯利应了一声，顿觉这话题索然无味。

朋友这般难堪，吉尔伯特却没有笑。

“一条项链，”他重复道，“真奇怪——是您的？”

“是我的。”卡思卡特夫人重复道，“我押在沃雷尔那儿作担保。结果这担保可真‘牢靠’。”她又补了一句。

===Original===
Warrell was all apologies. He was embarrassed for more reasons than one. He was very annoyed indeed with the indiscreet youth who owed his preponderant interest in the firm the more by reason of his dead father’s shares in the business than to any extent to his intelligence or his usefulness.

“Exactly what kind of necklace was it?” continued Gilbert. “I did not see a description.”

“No description was given,” said Mr. Warrell, coming to the relief of his client, whom he knew from infallible signs was fast losing her temper.

“We wished to keep the matter quiet, so that it should not get into the papers.”

===Chinese===
沃雷尔连声致歉。他尴尬的缘由不止一桩。他也着实恼火这个口无遮拦的年轻人——这小子在事务所里占着那么大的份子，靠的是他亡父留下的股份，而不是他本人的才智或用处。

“那究竟是条什么样的项链？”吉尔伯特追问，“我没见过相关描述。”

“没有登过描述。”沃雷尔先生说，赶来给客户解围——他从种种不会走眼的迹象看出，她眼看就要发作了。

“我们想把事情压住，免得见报。”

===Original===
Edith tactfully turned the conversation, and in a few minutes they were deep in the discussion of a question which has never failed to excite great interest—the abstract problem of the church.

Mrs. Cathcart, it may be remarked in passing, was a churchwoman of some standing, a leader amongst a certain set, and an extreme ritualist. Add to this element the broad Nonconformity of Mr. Warrell, the frank scepticism of Leslie, and there were all the ingredients for an argument, which in less refined circles might develop to a sanguinary conclusion.

Edith at least was relieved, however drastic the remedy might be, and was quite prepared to disestablish the Church of Wales, or if necessary the Church of England, rather than see the folly of her mother exposed.

Despite argument, dogmatism of Mrs. Cathcart, philippic of Leslie, and the good-natured tolerance of Mr. Warrell, this latter a most trying attitude to combat, the dinner ended pleasantly, and they adjourned to the little drawing-room upstairs.

===Chinese===
伊迪丝机巧地把话头转开，几分钟后，众人已深深陷入一个从来不曾令人失兴的问题——教会这个抽象难题——的讨论。

顺带说一句，卡思卡特夫人在国教信徒中颇有身份，在某个圈子里算得头面人物，还是个极端的礼仪派。再把沃雷尔先生宽和的不从国教派立场、莱斯利坦率的怀疑论一并搀进来，一场论战所需的作料就齐了——这要换个斯文气少些的圈子，闹不好要以流血收场。

伊迪丝至少松了口气，不管这剂药多猛；哪怕要废除威尔士教会的国教地位，必要时连英格兰教会一并废除，她也全无不可，只要别让母亲的蠢态当众暴露。

尽管有卡思卡特夫人的武断、莱斯利的滔滔声讨，还有沃雷尔先生那副好脾气的宽容——后一种态度对付起来最磨人——晚宴还是在愉快的气氛中收了场，众人移步楼上的小客厅。

===Original===
“I’m afraid I shall have to leave you,” said Gilbert.

It was nearly ten o’clock, and he had already warned his wife of an engagement he had made for a later hour.

“I believe old Gilbert is a journalist in these days,” said Leslie. “I saw you the other night in Fleet Street, didn’t I?”

“No,” replied Gilbert shortly.

“Then it must have been your double,” said the other.

Edith had not followed the party upstairs. Just before dinner Gilbert had asked her, with some hesitation, to make him up a packet of sandwiches.

===Chinese===
“恐怕我得失陪了。”吉尔伯特说。

将近十点，他早跟妻子打过招呼，稍晚还有个约会。

“我看老吉尔伯特如今是当记者了。”莱斯利说，“前几天夜里我在舰队街（Fleet Street）瞧见你了，没瞧错吧？”

“没有。”吉尔伯特简短地答。

“那准是撞见你的替身了。”对方说。

伊迪丝没有跟众人上楼。晚餐前，吉尔伯特带着几分迟疑，请她给自己包一份三明治。

===Original===
“I may be out the greater part of the night,” he said. “A man wants me to motor down to Brighton to meet somebody.”

“Will you be out all night?” she had asked, a little alarmed.

He shook his head.

“No, I shall be back by four,” he said.

She might have thought it was an unusual hour to meet people, but she made no comment.

As her little party had gone upstairs she had remembered the sandwiches, and went down into the kitchen to see if cook had cut and laid them ready.

===Chinese===
“我大半夜可能都在外面。”他说，“有人要我开车去布莱顿（Brighton）接一个人。”

“要出去一整夜吗？”她当时问，有点惊慌。

他摇了摇头。

“不，四点前就回来。”他说。

她本可以想想，这个钟点接人未免离奇，可她什么也没说。

客人们上楼后，她想起了三明治，便下到厨房，看看厨娘切好摆好了没有。

===Original===
She wrapped them up for him and packed them into a little flat sandwich case she had, and then made her way back to the hall.

His coat was hanging on a rack, and she had to slip them into the pocket. There was a newspaper in the way; she pulled it out, and there was something else, something loose and uneven.

She smiled at his untidiness, and put in her hand to remove the debris.

Her face changed.

===Chinese===
她替他包好，装进她那只扁扁的小三明治盒，然后回到门厅。

他的外套挂在衣架上，她得把三明治塞进外套口袋。口袋里有张报纸碍事；她把报纸抽出来，发现里面还有别的东西，松松的，鼓鼓囊囊。

她笑他不修边幅，伸手进去要清理那些杂物。

她的脸色变了。

===Original===
What was it?

Her fingers closed round the object in the bottom of the pocket, and she drew it out.

There in the palm of her hand, clearly revealed by the electric lamp above her head, shone her diamond necklace!

For a moment the little hall swayed, but she steadied herself with an effort.

===Chinese===
这是什么？

她的手指拢住袋底那件东西，抽了出来。

在头顶电灯的照射下，她掌心里亮晶晶的，正是她那条钻石项链！

小小的门厅一时在她眼前打转，她用力站稳了。

===Original===
Her necklace!

There was no doubt—she turned it over with trembling fingers.

How had he got it? Where did it come from?

A thought had struck her, but it was too horrible for her to give it expression.

Gilbert a burglar! It was absurd. She tried to smile, but failed. Almost every night he had been out, every night in the week in which this burglary had been committed.

===Chinese===
她的项链！

不会有错——她用发抖的手指把它翻来覆去地看。

他怎么弄到它的？它从哪儿来的？

一个念头击中了她，可那念头太可怕，她不敢让它成形出口。

吉尔伯特是窃贼！荒唐。她试着笑一笑，却笑不出来。他几乎夜夜外出，失窃案发生的那一个星期，夜夜如此。

===Original===
She heard a footstep on the stairs, and thrust the necklace into the bosom of her dress.

It was Gilbert. He did not notice her face, then—

“Gilbert,” she said, and something in her voice warned him.

He turned, peering down at her.

“What is wrong?” he asked.

===Chinese===
她听见楼梯上的脚步声，一把将项链塞进衣襟里。

是吉尔伯特。他没有留意她的脸色，于是——

“吉尔伯特。”她开口，嗓音里有种东西让他警觉起来。

他转过身，低头端详她。

“出什么事了？”他问。

===Original===
“Will you come into the dining-room for a moment?” she said.

Her voice sounded far away to her.

She felt it was not she who was speaking, but some third person.

He opened the door of the dining-room and walked in. The table was spread with the debris of the dinner which had just been concluded. The rosy glow of the overhead lamp fell upon a pretty chaos of flowers and silver and glass.

He closed the door behind him.

===Chinese===
“你能到餐厅来一下吗？”她说。

在她自己听来，这声音像是从很远的地方来的。

她觉得说话的不是她，而是某个第三者。

他推开餐厅的门走进去。餐桌上还摊着刚散席的残局。头顶灯盏玫瑰色的光，洒在鲜花、银器、玻璃杯的一片好看的狼藉上。

他随手关上了门。

===Original===
“What is it?” he asked.

“This,” she replied quietly, and drew the necklace from her dress.

He looked at it. Not a muscle of his face moved.

“That?” he said. “Well, what is that?”

“My necklace!”

===Chinese===
“什么事？”他问。

“这个。”她平静地答，从衣襟里取出项链。

他看了看。脸上没有一块肌肉动弹。

“这个？”他说，“怎么，这是什么？”

“我的项链！”

===Original===
“Your necklace,” he repeated dully. “Is that the necklace that your mother lost?”

She nodded, not trusting herself to speak.

“How very curious.”

He reached out his hand and took it from her and examined the diamond pendant.

“And that is your necklace,” he said. “Well, that is a remarkable coincidence.”

===Chinese===
“你的项链。”他木然重复，“这就是你母亲丢的那条项链？”

她点了点头，不敢开口说话。

“真是稀奇。”

他伸出手，从她那里接过来，端详着那枚钻石坠饰。

“这就是你的项链，”他说，“嘿，这可真是个了不得的巧合。”

===Original===
“Where did you get it?” she asked.

He did not make any reply. He was looking at her with a stony stare in which there was neither expression nor encouragement for speculation.

“Where did I get it?” he repeated calmly. “Who told you that I’d got it?”

“I found it in your pocket,” she said breathlessly. “Oh, Gilbert, there is no use denying that you had it there or you knew it was there. Where did you get it?”

===Chinese===
“你从哪儿弄来的？”她问。

他没有回答。他望着她，目光如石，既无表情，也无意让人揣测。

“我从哪儿弄来的？”他平静地重复，“谁告诉你我拿了它？”

“我在你口袋里找到的。”她急促地说，“哦，吉尔伯特，它就在你口袋里，这抵赖不掉，你也知道它在哪儿。你到底从哪儿弄来的？”

===Original===
Another pause, then came the answer—

“I found it.”

It was lame and unconvincing, and he knew it.

She repeated the question.

“I am not prepared to tell you,” he said calmly. “You think I stole it, I suppose? You probably imagine that I am a burglar?”

He smiled, but the lips that curved in laughter were hard.

===Chinese===
又是一阵沉默，然后有了回答——

“我捡到的。”

这话苍白无力，难以服人，他自己也知道。

她把问题又问了一遍。

“我不打算告诉你。”他平静地说，“我猜，你认为是我偷的？你八成在想，我就是个窃贼？”

他笑了，可那含笑弯起的嘴唇是冷的。

===Original===
“I can see that in your eyes,” he went on. “You explain my absence from home, my retirement from the Foreign Office, by the fact that I have taken up a more lucrative profession.”

He laughed aloud.

“Well, I have,” he said. “It is not exactly burglary. I assure you,” he went on with mock solemnity, “that I have never burgled a safe in my life. I give you my word of honour that I have never stolen a single article of any—” He stopped himself—he might say too much.

===Chinese===
“这我从你眼里看得出来。”他接着说，“你用我改行干了一门更挣钱的营生，来解释我夜夜晚归，解释我退出外交部（Foreign Office）。”

他放声笑了。

“嘿，还真让你说着了。”他说，“不过不完全是盗窃。我向你保证，”他装出一本正经的样子接着说，“我平生从没撬过一只保险柜。我以名誉向你担保，我从没偷过任何一件——”他刹住了——再多说就要说漏了。

===Original===
But Edith grasped at the straw he offered her.

“Oh, you do mean that, don’t you?” she said eagerly, and laid her two hands on his breast. “You really mean it? I know it is stupid of me, foolish and horribly disloyal—common of me, anything you like, to suspect you of so awful a thing, but it did seem—it did, didn’t it?”

“It did,” he agreed gravely.

“Won’t you tell me how it came into your possession?” she pleaded.

===Chinese===
可伊迪丝一把抓住了他递来的这根救命稻草。

“哦，你是当真的，是不是？”她急切地说，把两只手贴上他的胸口，“你真的是这个意思？我知道是我蠢，又糊涂又不忠得吓人——庸俗，随你怎么说都行，居然疑心你干这种可怕的事，可它看上去确实——确实像，是不是？”

“是像。”他郑重地承认。

“你就不能告诉我，它怎么落到你手里的？”她恳求道。

===Original===
“I tell you I found it—that is true. I had no intention—” He stopped again. “It was—I picked it up in the road, in a country lane.”

“But weren’t you awfully surprised to find it, and didn’t you tell the police?”

He shook his head.

“No,” he said, “I was not surprised, and I did not tell the police. I intended restoring it, because, after all, jewels are of no value to me, are they?”

===Chinese===
“我告诉你，是我捡的——这是实话。我本来无意——”他又停住了，“事情是——我在路上捡的，在一条乡间小道上。”

“可你捡到它，不觉得离奇透顶吗？你没报告警察？”

他摇了摇头。

“没有，”他说，“我不觉得离奇，也没有报告警察。我本打算物归原主，因为说到底，珠宝对我毫无用处，不是吗？”

===Original===
“I don’t understand you, Gilbert.” She shook her head, a little bewildered. “Nothing is of any use except what belongs to you, is it?”

“That depends,” he said calmly. “But in this particular case I assure you that I brought this home tonight with the intention of putting it into a small box and addressing it to the Chief Commissioner of Police. You may believe that or not. That is why I thought it so extraordinary when you were talking at dinner that your mother should have lost a necklace, and that I should have found one.”

They stood looking at one another, he weighing the necklace on the palm of his hand, tossing it up and down mechanically.

===Chinese===
“我不明白你，吉尔伯特。”她摇了摇头，有些茫然，“除了属于你的东西，别的对你都毫无用处，是吗？”

“那得看情况。”他平静地说，“不过就这桩事而言，我向你保证，今晚我把它带回家，本打算装进一只小盒子，写上警察总监（Chief Commissioner of Police）收。信不信由你。所以晚餐时你们谈起话来，你母亲丢了一条项链、而我恰好捡到一条，我才觉得那么离奇。”

两人站着对望，他把项链托在掌心掂着，机械地抛上抛下。

===Original===
“What are we going to do with it now?” she asked. She was in a quandary. “I hardly know how to advise.” She hesitated. “Suppose you carry out your present intention and send it to the police.

“Oh!” she remembered with a little move of dismay, “I have practically stolen three hundred pounds.”

“Three hundred pounds!”

He looked at the jewel.

“It’s worth more than three hundred pounds.”

===Chinese===
“现在我们拿它怎么办？”她问。她左右为难，“我简直不知道该怎么出主意。”她迟疑着，“要不就照你原先的打算，把它寄给警察。”

“哦！”她想起一件事，不安地动了动，“那三百镑，我等于是偷来的。”

“三百镑！”

他看了看那件首饰。

“它可不止值三百镑。”

===Original===
In a few words she explained how the jewel came to be lost, and how it came to be deposited in the hands of Warrell’s.

“I’m glad to hear that your mother is the culprit. I was afraid you’d been gambling.”

“Would that worry you?” she asked quickly.

“A little,” he said; “it’s enough for one member of a family to gamble.”

“Do you gamble very much, Gilbert?” she asked seriously.

“A little,” he said.

“Not a little,” she corrected. “Stock Exchange business is gambling.”

“I am trying to make money for you,” he said brusquely.

===Chinese===
她三言两语讲了这条项链怎么会丢，又怎么会押到沃雷尔事务所手里。

“听说是你母亲干的，我就放心了。我还怕是你去赌了呢。”

“那会让你担心吗？”她飞快地问。

“有一点，”他说，“一家人里有一个好赌，就够了。”

“你赌得很凶吗，吉尔伯特？”她正色问。

“有一点。”他说。

“何止一点。”她纠正道，“证券交易所的买卖就是赌博。”

“我是想替你挣钱。”他生硬地说。

===Original===
It was the most brutal thing he had said to her in her short period of married life, and he saw he had hurt her.

“I am sorry,” he said gently. “I know I am a brute, but I did not mean to hurt you. I was just protesting in my heart against the unfairness of things. Will you take this, or shall I?”

“I will take it,” she said. “But won’t you tell the police where you found it? Possibly they might find the proceeds of other robberies nearby.”

“I think not,” he replied with a little smile. “I have no desire to incur the anger of this particular gang. I am satisfied in my mind that it is one of the most powerful and one of the most unscrupulous in existence. It is nearly half-past ten,” he said; “I must fly.”

===Chinese===
这是短短婚期内他对她说过的最狠的一句话，他看得出自己伤了她。

“对不起。”他温和下来，“我知道我是个粗人，可我没想伤你。我不过是在心里抗议世事不公。这东西你拿着，还是我拿着？”

“我拿着。”她说，“可你不愿告诉警察你在哪儿捡的吗？说不定他们能在附近找到其他劫案的赃物。”

“我看不必。”他微微笑道，“我可不想招这伙人的恨。我心里有数，这是现存最厉害、也最不择手段的一伙。快十点半了，”他说，“我得赶紧走了。”

===Original===
He held out his hand, and she took it. She held it for a moment longer than was her wont.

“Goodbye,” she said. “Good luck, whatever your business may be.”

“Thank you,” he said.

She went slowly back to her guests. It did not make the position any easier to understand. She believed her husband, and yet there was a certain reservation in what he had told her, a reservation which said as plainly as his guarded words could tell that there was much more he could have said had he been inclined.

===Chinese===
他伸出手，她握住了。她握的时间，比平素长了一刻。

“再见。”她说，“无论你干的是什么，祝你好运。”

“谢谢。”他说。

她慢慢回到客人那里去。眼下的处境并没有变得好懂一点。她相信丈夫，可他的话里留着某种保留，那保留说得几乎同他字斟句酌的话一样明白：他还有许多可说的，只是不愿意说罢了。

===Original===
She did not doubt his word when he told her that he had never stolen from—from whom was he going to say? She was more determined than ever to solve this mystery, and after her guests had gone she was busily engaged in writing letters. She was hardly in bed that night before she heard his foot on the stairs and listened.

He knocked at her door as he passed.

“Good night,” he said.

“Good night,” she replied.

===Chinese===
他说他从不曾偷过——他这话原是要说到谁头上的呢？她并不怀疑他的话。她比先前更下定决心要解开这个谜。客人走后，她忙着写了几封信。那晚她刚上床，就听见他的脚步上了楼梯，便侧耳听着。

他经过时敲了敲她的门。

“晚安。”他说。

“晚安。”她答。

===Original===
She heard his door close gently, and she waited for half an hour until she heard the click of his electric switch which told her that he was in bed, and that his light was extinguished.

Then she stole softly out of bed, wrapped her dressing-gown round her, and went softly down the stairs. Perhaps his coat was hanging in the hall.

It was a wild, fantastic idea of hers that he might possibly have brought some further evidence that would help her in her search for the truth, but the pockets were empty.

She felt something wet upon the sleeve, and gathered that it was raining. She went back to her room, closed the door noiselessly, and went to the window to look out into the street. It was a fine morning, and the streets were dry. She saw her hands. They were smeared with blood!

===Chinese===
她听见他的房门轻轻关上，又等了半个钟头，直到听见他电门咔哒一响——这告诉她他上了床，灯熄了。

这时她才轻手轻脚下床，裹上晨衣，悄悄下楼。他的外套兴许还挂在门厅里。

她异想天开地指望，他也许又带回了什么能助她追索真相的凭据，可口袋空空如也。

她摸到袖子上有什么湿乎乎的，心想外面在下雨。她回到自己房里，无声地关上门，走到窗前向街上望去。是个晴朗的早晨，街道干干的。她看见了自己的手。那上面满是血迹！

===Original===
She ran down the stairs again and turned on the light in the hall.

Yes, there it was on his sleeve. There were little drops of blood on the stair carpet. She could trace him all the way up the stairs by this. She went straight to his room and knocked.

He answered instantly.

“Who is that?”

“It is I. I want to see you.”

“I am rather tired,” he said.

===Chinese===
她又奔下楼，拧亮门厅的灯。

是的，就在他袖子上。楼梯地毯上有一滴滴小小的血点。靠这些，她能一路追他上楼。她径直走到他房门前，敲了敲门。

他立刻应声。

“谁？”

“是我。我要见你。”

“我有点累了。”他说。

===Original===
“Please let me in. I want to see you.”

She tried the door, but it was locked. Then she heard the bed creak as he moved. An instant later the bolt was slipped, and the light shone through the fanlight over the door.

He was almost fully dressed, she observed.

“What is the matter with your arm?” she asked.

It was carefully bandaged.

===Chinese===
“请让我进去。我要见你。”

她拧了拧门，锁着。接着她听见床响，他起身了。片刻之后门闩一滑，灯光从门楣窗里透了出来。

她注意到，他差不多衣装齐全。

“你的胳膊怎么了？”她问。

胳膊上仔细缠着绷带。

===Original===
“I hurt it. It is nothing very much.”

“How did you hurt it?” she asked impatiently.

She was nearing the end of her resources. She wanted him to say that it had happened in a taxicab smash or one of the street accidents to which city dwellers are liable, but he did not explain.

She asked to see the wound. He was unwilling, but she insisted. At last he unwrapped the bandage, and showed an ugly little gash on the forearm. It was too rough to be the clean-cut wound of a knife or of broken glass.

===Chinese===
“弄伤了。没什么大碍。”

“怎么弄伤的？”她不耐烦地问。

她的耐性快要耗尽了。她只盼他说一句，是出租车出了事，或是城里人难免碰上的哪种街头意外，可他不解释。

她要求看伤口。他不愿意，可她坚持。最后他解开绷带，前臂上露出一道难看的小裂口。伤口毛糙，不像是刀子或碎玻璃割出的那种利落伤口。

===Original===
There was a second wound about the size of a sixpence near the elbow.

“That looks like a bullet wound,” she said, and pointed. “It has glanced along your arm, and has caught you again near the elbow.”

He did not speak.

She procured warm water from the bathroom and bathed it, found a cool emollient in her room and dressed it as well as she could.

She did not again refer to the circumstances under which the injury had been sustained. This was not the time nor the place to discuss that.

===Chinese===
靠近肘部还有第二个伤口，约莫一枚六便士硬币大小。

“那看着像枪伤。”她指着说，“子弹擦着你的手臂掠过去，又在你肘弯附近咬了一口。”

他没有说话。

她从浴室取来温水，替他清洗伤口，又在自己房里找出清凉的药膏，尽力敷好包扎。

伤是怎么来的，她没有再提。此时此地，都不是谈论这个的场合。

===Original===
“There is an excellent nurse spoilt in you,” he said when she had finished.

“I am afraid there is an excellent man spoilt in you,” she answered in a low voice, “and I am rather inclined to think that I have done the spoiling.”

“Please get that out of your head altogether,” he said almost roughly. “A man is what he makes himself: you know the tag—the evil you do by two and two you answer for one by one; and even if you had any part in the influencing of my life for evil, I am firstly and lastly responsible.”

===Chinese===
“你身上埋没了一位顶好的护士。”她包扎完，他说。

“我怕你身上埋没的是一位顶好的男人。”她低声答道，“而且我倒倾向于认为，这埋没是我干下的。”

“这种念头，你趁早彻底打消。”他说得近乎粗暴，“一个人变成什么样，是他自己一手造成的。你知道那句老话——作恶两两而行，偿罪一一而领。哪怕你真对我的生活产生过什么坏影响，第一位的、最后的责任，都在我。”

===Original===
“I am not so sure of that,” said she.

She had made him a little sling in which to rest his arm.
