“You married me because you loved me, because you gave to me all that a right-thinking woman would hold precious and sacred and because you expected me to give something in return. I have given you nothing. I humiliated you at the very outset by telling you why I had married you. You have the dubious satisfaction of knowing that I bear your name. You have, perhaps, half a suspicion that you live with one who is everlastingly critical of your actions and your intentions. Have I no responsibilities?”

===Chinese===
“这话我可不敢说。”她说。

她给他做了个小吊带，好让胳膊歇着。

“你娶我，是因为你爱我，是因为你把一个正直女人视为珍贵与神圣的一切都给了我，是因为你指望我也回报一些什么。可我什么也没给你。一开始我就告诉了你我为什么嫁给你，羞辱了你。你得到的那份满足是说不清的：知道我顶着你的姓。你或许还有几分疑心，疑心跟你同住的这个人，永远在挑剔你的言行和打算。难道我就没有责任吗？”

===Original===
There was a long silence, then she said—

“Whatever you wish me to do I will always do.”

“I wish you to be happy, that is all,” he replied.

His voice was of the same hard, metallic tone which she had noted before.

She flushed a little. It had been an effort for her to say what she had, and he had rebuffed her. He was within his rights, she thought.

===Chinese===
长久的沉默，然后她说——

“无论你要我做什么，我都会去做。”

“我要你快乐，仅此而已。”他答道。

他的嗓音还是她先前注意到的那种冷硬的金属腔调。

她的脸微微红了。说出方才那番话，她已用尽力气，他却把她顶了回来。他这样也在理，她想。

===Original===
She left him, and did not see him till the morning when they met at breakfast. They exchanged a few words of greeting, and both turned their attention to their newspapers. Edith read hers in silence, read the one column which meant so much to her from end to end twice, then she laid the paper down.

“I see,” she said, “that our burglars rifled the Bank of the Northern Provinces last night.”

“So I read,” he said, without raising his eyes from his paper.

“And that one of them was shot by the armed guard of the bank.”

“I’ve also seen that,” said her husband.

“Shot,” she repeated, and looked at his bandaged arm.

He nodded.

===Chinese===
她离开了他，直到第二天早晨吃早餐才再见到他。两人寒暄了几句，便都埋头看报。伊迪丝默默读着，把那一个对她意义重大的专栏从头到尾读了两遍，然后放下了报纸。

“我看到了，”她说，“昨晚我们的窃贼洗劫了北方诸省银行（Bank of the Northern Provinces）。”

“我也读到了。”他说，眼睛没离开报纸。

“其中一个还被银行的武装守卫打中了。”

“这个我也看到了。”她丈夫说。

“打中了。”她重复道，目光落在他缠着绷带的胳膊上。

他点了点头。

===Original===
“I think my paper is a later edition than yours,” he said gently. “The man that was shot was killed. They found his body in a taxicab. His name is not given, but I happen to know that it was a very pleasant florid gentleman named Persh. Poor fellow,” he mused, “it was poetic justice.”

“Why?” she asked.

“He did this,” said Gilbert Standerton, and pointed to his arm with a grim smile.

===Chinese===
“我想我的报纸比你那版晚些。”他温和地说，“中枪的那个人死了。人们在一辆出租车里发现了他的尸体。报上没登名字，不过我碰巧知道，那是位和气红润的绅士，名叫佩什（Persh）。可怜的人，”他若有所思，“这真是因果报应。”

“为什么？”她问。

“这是他干的。”吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿说着，指了指自己的胳膊，脸上带着一丝冷笑。

## XII The Place Where the Loot Was Stored / XII 赃物藏匿之处

===Original===
> “Will the Hatton Garden intruder communicate with the man who lay on the floor, and arrange a meeting. The man on the floor has a proposition to make, and promises no harm to intruder.”

Gilbert Standerton read the advertisement when he was taking his breakfast, and a little smile gathered at the corners of his lips.

Edith saw the smile.

“What is amusing you, Gilbert?” she asked.

“A thought,” he said. “I think these advertisements are so funny.”

===Chinese===
> “请哈顿花园（Hatton Garden）的那位不速之客与躺在地板上的先生取得联系，并安排会面。躺在地板上的先生有一项提议，并保证不伤害不速之客。”

吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿（Gilbert Standerton）用早餐时读到了这则广告，嘴角浮起一丝笑意。

伊迪丝（Edith）看见了这笑容。

“有什么好笑的，吉尔伯特？”她问。

“一个念头，”他说，“我觉得这些广告真有意思。”

===Original===
She had seen the direction of his eyes, carefully noted the page of the paper, and waited for an opportunity to examine for herself the cause of his amusement.

“By the way,” he said carelessly, “I am putting some money to your credit at the bank today.”

“Mine?” she asked.

He nodded.

“Yes, I have been rather fortunate on the Stock Exchange lately—I made twelve thousand pounds out of American rails.”

===Chinese===
她早留意到他目光的落点，暗暗记下了报纸的版面，只等机会亲自查明他发笑的缘由。

“对了，”他漫不经心地说，“我今天要在银行给你名下存一笔钱。”

“我的户头？”她问。

他点点头。

“是的，我近来在证券交易所（Stock Exchange）上颇为走运——炒美国铁路股赚了一万二千镑。”

===Original===
She looked at him steadily.

“Do you mean that?” she asked.

“What else could I mean?” he demanded. “You see, American rails have been rather jumpy of late, and so have I.” He smiled again. “I jumped in when they were low and jumped out when they were high. Here is the broker’s statement.” He drew it from his pocket and passed it across the table to her.

“I feel,” he said, with a pretence of humour, “that you should know I do not secure my entire income from my nefarious profession.”

She made no response to this. She knew who the fourth man had been. Why had he gone there? What had been his object?

===Chinese===
她定定地看着他。

“你说的是真的？”她问。

“不然还能是什么意思？”他反问，“你是知道的，美国铁路股近来上蹿下跳，我也一样。”他又笑了，“低位时跳进去，高位时跳出来。这是经纪行的对账单。”他从口袋里掏出来，隔着桌子递给她。

“我觉得，”他故作诙谐地说，“你应该知道，我并不是全靠那门不光彩的行当挣饭吃的。”

她对此没有作答。她已经知道那第四个人是谁了。他去那里做什么？目的何在？

===Original===
If he had been a detective, or if he had been in the employ of the Government, he would have confessed it. Her heart had sunk when she had read the interesting theory which had been put forward by the journal.

He was the second burglar.

She thought all this with the paper he had passed to her on the table before her.

The broker’s statement was clear enough. Here were the amounts, all columns ruled and carried forward.

“You will observe that I have not put it all to your credit,” he bantered, “some of it has gone to mine.”

===Chinese===
倘若他是个侦探，或是受雇于政府的人，他早就坦白了。可她读到报上抛出的那个有趣的理论时，心却沉了下去。

他就是第二名窃贼。

她面前摊着他递给她的对账单，脑子里翻来覆去想着这一切。

对账单清清楚楚。金额俱在，各栏划线齐整，结转分明。

“你会注意到，我并没有把它全存到你名下，”他打趣道，“有一部分进了我的户头。”

===Original===
“Gilbert,” she asked, “why do you keep things from me?”

“What do I keep from you?” he asked.

“Why do you keep from me the fact that you were in the bank the night before last when this horrible tragedy occurred?”

He did not answer immediately.

“I have not kept it from you,” he said. “I have practically admitted it—in an unguarded moment, I confess, but I did admit it.”

“What were you doing there?” she demanded.

“Making my fortune,” he said solemnly.

===Chinese===
“吉尔伯特，”她问，“你为什么什么事都瞒着我？”

“我瞒你什么了？”他问。

“前天夜里那场可怕的惨剧发生时，你就在银行里——这个事实你为什么瞒着我？”

他没有立即作答。

“我没有瞒你，”他说，“实际上我等于已经承认了——我承认，那是一时失言，但我确实承认了。”

“你去那里做什么？”她追问。

“发家致富啊。”他一本正经地说。

===Original===
But she was not to be put off by his flippancy.

“What were you doing there?” she asked again.

“I was watching three interesting burglars at work,” he said, “as I have watched them not once but many times. You see, I am specially gifted in one respect. Nature intended me to be a burglar, but education and breed and a certain lawfulness of character prohibited that course. I am a dilettante: I do not commit crime, but I am monstrously interested in it. I seek,” he said slowly, “to discover what fascination crime has over the normal mind; also I have an especial reason for checking the amount these men collect.”

===Chinese===
可她不会被他的轻浮敷衍过去。

“你到底去那里做什么？”她又问了一遍。

“我在观赏三个有意思的窃贼干活，”他说，“这样的场面我看过不止一次，而是许多次。你是知道的，我在某方面天赋异禀。造化本意要我做窃贼，可教养、出身，加上骨子里某种守法的天性，禁止我走这条路。我是个业余玩家：我不犯罪，却对犯罪有着天大的兴趣。”他缓缓说道，“我想探明犯罪对正常人的心智究竟有何魔力；另外，我还有一个特别的理由，要查清这些人进账的数目。”

===Original===
Her puzzled frown hurt him; he did not want to bother her, but she knew so much now that he must tell her more.

He had thought it would have been possible to have hidden everything from her, but people cannot live together in the same house and be interested in one another’s comings and goings without some of their cherished secrets being revealed.

“What I cannot understand—” she said slowly and was at a loss for an introduction to this delicate subject.

“What cannot you understand?” he asked.

“I cannot understand why you suddenly dropped all your normal pleasures, why you left the Foreign Office, why you gave up music, and why, above all things, that this change in your life should have come about immediately after the playing of the ‘Melody in F.’”

===Chinese===
她困惑的蹙眉刺痛了他；他本不想烦扰她，可她如今知道得太多，他不得不告诉她更多。

他原以为可以把一切都瞒住她，但同住一个屋檐下、又彼此留意对方的来去行踪，人总会有些珍视的秘密败露出来。

“我没法明白的是——”她缓缓开口，却不知如何引入这个微妙的话题。

“你不明白什么？”他问。

“我不明白你为什么突然抛下了所有寻常的消遣，为什么离开外交部（Foreign Office），为什么舍弃了音乐，尤其是，为什么你人生中的这场巨变，偏偏发生在那曲《F大调旋律》（‘Melody in F’）奏响之后。”

===Original===
He was silent for a moment, and when he spoke his voice was low and troubled.

“You are not exactly right,” he said. “I had begun my observations into the ways of the criminal before that tune was played.” He paused. “I admit that I had some fear in my mind that sooner or later the ‘Melody in F’ would be played under my window, and I was making a halfhearted preparation against the evil day. That is all I can tell you,” he said.

“Tell me this,” she asked as he rose, “if I had loved you, and had been all that you desired, would you have adopted this course?”

He thought awhile. “I cannot tell you,” he said at length; “possibly I should, perhaps I should not. Yes,” he said, nodding his head, “I should have done what I am doing now, only it would have been harder to do if you had loved me. As it is—” he shrugged his shoulders.

He went out soon after, and she found the paper he had been reading, and without difficulty discovered the advertisement.

Then he was the Hatton Garden intruder, and what he had said was true. He had observed these people, and they had known they were being observed.

With a whirling brain she sat down to piece together the threads of mystery. She was no nearer a solution when she had finished, from sheer exhaustion, than when she had begun.

===Chinese===
他沉默了片刻，再开口时，声音低沉而烦乱。

“你说得并不全对，”他说，“在那支曲子奏响之前，我就已经开始观察犯罪这门行当的门道了。”他顿了顿，“我承认，我心里确实有些怕，怕《F大调旋律》迟早有一天会在我窗下奏响，于是我不情不愿地做着防备，以待那个日子到来。我能告诉你的就是这些。”他说。

“告诉我一件事，”他起身时她问道，“倘若我爱过你，倘若我符合你的一切期望，你还会走上这条路吗？”

他想了一会儿。“我没法告诉你，”他终于说道，“也许会，也许不会。是啊，”他点点头，“我还是会做我现在做的事，只不过，倘若你爱过我，做起来会更难一些。如今嘛——”他耸了耸肩。

不久他便出了门，她找出他读过的那份报纸，毫不费力地发现了那则广告。

这么说，他就是哈顿花园的那个不速之客，他说的都是真话。他一直在观察那些人，而那些人也知道自己正被人观察。

她头脑昏乱地坐下来，试图把谜团的千头万绪拼拢起来。等到纯粹因为心力交瘁而停下来时，她离谜底仍不比开始时更近一步。

---
===Original===
Gilbert had not intended spending the night away from his house. He realised that his wife would worry, and that she would have a genuine grievance; apart from which he was, in a sense, domesticated, and if the life he was living was an unusual one, it had its charm and its attraction.

The knowledge that he would meet her every morning, speak to her during the day, and that he had in her a growing friend was particularly pleasing to him.

He had gone to a little office that he rented over a shop in Cheapside, an office which his work in the City had made necessary.

He unlocked the door of the tiny room, which was situated on the third floor, and entered, closing the door behind him. There were one or two letters which had come to him in the capacity in which he appeared as the tenant of the office. They were mainly business communications, and required little or no attention.

He sat down at his desk to write a note; he thought he might be late that night, and wanted to explain his absence. His wife occupied a definite place in his life, and though she exercised no rights over his movements, yet could quite reasonably expect to be informed of his immediate plans.

===Chinese===
吉尔伯特原本没打算在外面过夜。他知道妻子会担忧，也知道她满有理由觉得委屈；再说，从某种意义上讲他是个恋家的人，如今过的日子纵然异乎寻常，也自有其魅力与吸引。

每天清晨能与她相见，白天能与她交谈，而且她在他是日益亲近的朋友——这份认知令他格外愉快。

他去了奇普赛德（Cheapside）一间租在店铺楼上的小办公室，那是他在伦敦金融城的事务使之必不可少的所在。

他开了这间位于三楼的小房间的门锁，走进去，随手带上了门。有一两封信是寄给他这间办公室承租人身份的，多半是商务函件，几乎无需费心料理。

他在书桌前坐下，打算写一张便条；他估计那晚可能晚归，想解释一下自己的缺席。妻子在他生命中占有一个确定的位置，虽然她从不行使支配他行动的权利，却完全有理由期望得知他眼下的打算。

===Original===
He had scarcely put pen to paper when a knock came to the door.

“Come in,” said Gilbert in some surprise.

It was not customary for people to call upon him here. He expected to see a wandering canvasser in search of an order, but the man that came in was nothing so commonplace. Gilbert knew him as a Mr. Wallis, an affable and a pleasant man.

“Sit down, will you?” he said, without a muscle of his face wrong.

“I want to see you, Mr. Standerton,” said Wallis, and made no attempt to seat himself. “Would you care to come to my office?”

===Chinese===
他刚一落笔，门上便响起了敲门声。

“请进。”吉尔伯特有些诧异地说。

这里向来无人来访。他以为进来的会是个四处兜揽生意的行商，可进来的人绝非这般俗流。吉尔伯特认得他，是位沃利斯先生（Mr. Wallis），一个和蔼可亲的人物。

“请坐，好吗？”他说，脸上没有一丝异样。

“我想见你，斯坦德顿先生，”沃利斯说着，并无落座的意思，“你愿意到我的办公室来一趟吗？”

===Original===
“I can see you here, I think,” said Gilbert calmly.

“I prefer to see you in my office,” said the man, “we are less liable to interruption. You are not afraid to come, I suppose?” he said with the hint of a smile.

“I am not to be piqued into coming, at any rate,” smiled Gilbert; “but since this is not a very expansive office, nor conducive to expansive thought, I will go with you. I presume you intend taking me into your confidence?”

He looked at the other man strangely and Wallis nodded.

The two men left the office together, and Gilbert wondered exactly what proposition the other would put to him.

===Chinese===
“我想就在这儿见你也未尝不可。”吉尔伯特平静地说。

“我更愿意在我的办公室里见你，”那人说，“那里不容易被人打扰。你总不至于不敢来吧？”他说时带着一丝笑意。

“至少，激将法是激不动我的，”吉尔伯特含笑道，“不过这间办公室确实不够宽敞，也不利于展开宏大的思路，我就跟你走一趟吧。我猜，你是打算向我交个底？”

他神色异样地看着对方，沃利斯点了点头。

两人一同离开办公室，吉尔伯特暗自揣度，对方究竟会向他提出什么样的提议。

===Original===
Ten minutes later they were in the St. Bride Street store, that excellent Safe Agency whose business apparently was increasing by leaps and bounds.

Gilbert Standerton looked round. The manager was there, a model of respectability. He bowed politely to Wallis, and was somewhat surprised to see him perhaps, for the proprietor of the St. Bride’s Safe Agency was a rare visitor.

“My office, I think?” suggested Wallis.

He closed the door behind them.

“Now exactly what do you want?” asked Gilbert.

===Chinese===
十分钟后，两人到了圣布莱德街（St. Bride Street）的那家铺面，正是那间生意显然蒸蒸日上的出色保险柜商行（Safe Agency）。

吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿环顾四周。经理在场，一副体面人家的典范做派。他向沃利斯恭敬地鞠了一躬，见到他或许还颇为诧异，因为圣布莱德保险柜商行的老板可是位稀客。

“到我的办公室谈吧？”沃利斯提议道。

他随手关上了他们身后的门。

“那么，你到底想要什么？”吉尔伯特问。

===Original===
“Will you have a cigar?” Mr. Wallis pushed the box towards him.

Gilbert smiled.

“You need not be scared of them,” said Wallis with a twinkle in his eye. “There is nothing dopey or wrong with these, they are my own special brand.”

“I do not smoke cigars,” said Gilbert.

“Lie number one,” replied Wallis cheerfully. “This is a promising beginning to an exchange of confidences. Now, Mr. Standerton, we are going to be very frank with one another, at least I am going to be very frank with you. I hope you will reciprocate, because I think I deserve something. You know so much about me, and I know so little about you, that it would be fair if we evened matters up.”

===Chinese===
“来支雪茄？”沃利斯先生把烟盒朝他推过去。

吉尔伯特笑了。

“你用不着怕它们，”沃利斯眼中闪着狡黠说道，“这些烟里没什么迷药之类的名堂，是我自己特供的牌子。”

“我不抽雪茄。”吉尔伯特说。

“谎话第一号，”沃利斯愉快地回敬，“这可是一场交心的良好开端。好了，斯坦德顿先生，咱们要彼此坦诚相待，至少我要对你坦诚相待。我希望你也礼尚往来，因为我觉得自己理应得到一点回报。你对我的底细知道得那么多，我对你的情况知道得这么少，把账扯平了才算公平。”

===Original===
“I take you,” said Gilbert, “and if I can see any advantage in doing so you may be sure I shall act on your suggestion.”

“A few months ago,” said Mr. Wallis, puffing slowly at his cigar, and regarding the ceiling with an attentive eye, “I and one of my friends were engaged in a scientific work.”

Gilbert nodded.

“In the midst of that work we were interrupted by a gentleman, who for a reason best known to himself modestly hid his features behind a mask.” He shrugged his shoulders. “I deplore the melodrama, but I applaud the discretion. Since then,” he went on, “the efforts of my friends in their scientific pursuit of wealth have been hampered and hindered by that same gentleman. Sometimes we have seen him, and sometimes we have only discovered his presence after we have retired from the scene of our labour. Now, Mr. Standerton, this young man may have excellent reasons for all he is doing, but he is considerably jeopardising our safety.”

===Chinese===
“我明白你的意思，”吉尔伯特说，“只要我能在其中看到任何好处，你大可放心，我会照你的建议行事。”

“几个月前，”沃利斯先生缓缓喷着雪茄，以专注的目光端详着天花板，“我和我的一位朋友正从事一项科学工作。”

吉尔伯特点了点头。

“工作正进行到一半，我们被一位先生打断了，那位先生出于只有他自己最清楚的理由，谦逊地把面孔藏在了面具后面。”他耸耸肩，“这种闹剧做派我深恶痛绝，这份谨慎我却击节赞赏。自那以后，”他继续道，“我的朋友们在以科学手段追求财富的路上，屡屡被同一位先生阻挠掣肘。有时我们能见到他，有时直到我们从劳动现场撤走之后，才发现他来过的痕迹。好了，斯坦德顿先生，这位年轻人的所作所为也许桩桩都有十足的理由，可他严重地危及了我们的安全。”

===Original===
“Who is the young man?” asked Gilbert Standerton.

“The young man,” said Mr. Wallis, without taking his eyes from the ceiling, “is yourself.”

“How do you know?” asked Gilbert quietly.

“I know,” said the other with a smile, “and there is an end to it. I can prove it curiously enough without having actually spotted your face.” He pulled an inkpad from the end of the desk. “Will you make a little fingermark upon that sheet of paper?” he asked, and offered a sheet of paper.

===Chinese===
“这位年轻人是谁？”吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿问。

“这位年轻人，”沃利斯先生说着，目光始终不离天花板，“就是阁下。”

“你怎么知道？”吉尔伯特平静地问。

“我知道，”对方微笑着说，“此事就此了结。说来奇妙，我不曾亲眼看清你的脸，却也能拿出证据来。”他从书桌一端拉过一个印泥盒。“请在这张纸上按一个小小的指印吧？”他递过一张纸，问道。

===Original===
Gilbert shook his head with a smile.

“I see no reason why I should,” he said coolly.

“Exactly. If you did we should find a very interesting fingermark to compare with it. In the office here,” Mr. Wallis went on, “we have a large safe which has been on our hands for some months.”

Gilbert nodded.

“Owned by a client who has the keys,” he said.

“Exactly,” said Wallis. “You remember my lie about it. There are three sets of keys to that safe and a combination word. I said three”—he corrected himself carefully—“there are really four. By an act of gross carelessness on my part, I left the keys of the safe in my pocket in this very office three weeks ago.

“I must confess,” he said with a smile, “that I did not suspect you of having so complete a knowledge of my doings or of my many secrets. I remembered my folly at eleven o’clock that night, and came back for what I had left behind. I found them exactly where I had left them, but somebody else had found them, too, and that somebody else had taken a wax impression of them. Moreover,” he leant forward towards Gilbert, lowering his voice, “that somebody else has since formed the habit of coming to this place nightly for reasons of his own. Do you know what those reasons are, Mr. Standerton?”

===Chinese===
吉尔伯特含笑摇头。

“我看没有理由这么做。”他冷冷地说。

“正是。你要是按了，我们便能拿到一枚非常有趣的指印来作比对。在这间办公室里，”沃利斯先生继续说，“我们有一只大型保险柜，搁在我们手上已有几个月了。”

吉尔伯特点了点头。

“箱主是一位握着钥匙的客户。”他说。

“正是，”沃利斯说，“你还记得我在这上头撒过的谎。那只保险柜有三套钥匙和一个转字密码。我说三套——”他小心翼翼地更正自己——“其实有四套。三个星期前，由于我一时极度粗心，我把保险柜的钥匙留在了口袋里，就撂在这间办公室里。

“我必须承认，”他微笑着说，“我没有料到，你对我的行踪、对我的诸多秘密竟了如指掌。那天夜里十一点钟，我想起了自己的荒唐，回来取落下的东西。我发现它们原封不动地搁在我离开时的老地方，但另一个人也发现了它们，而且那另一个人用蜡取了模。不但如此，”他朝吉尔伯特俯过身来，压低嗓音，“打那以后，那位先生养成了每夜来此的习惯，自有他的缘故。你可知道那些缘故是什么，斯坦德顿先生？”

===Original===
“To choose a safe?” suggested Gilbert ironically.

“He comes to rob us of the fruits of our labour,” said Wallis.

He smiled as he said the words because he had a sense of humour.

“Some individual who has a conscience or a sense of rectitude which prevents him from becoming an official burglar is engaged in the fascinating pursuit of robbing the robber. In other words, some twenty thousand pounds in solid cash has been taken from my safe.”

“Borrowed, I do not doubt,” said Gilbert Standerton, and leant back in his chair, his hands stuffed into his pockets, and a hard look upon his face.

“What do you mean—borrowed?” asked Wallis in surprise.

“Borrowed by somebody who is desperately in need of money; somebody who understands the Stock Exchange much better than many of the men who make a special study of it; somebody with such knowledge as would enable him to gamble heavily with a minimum chance of loss, and yet, despite this, fearing to injure some unfortunate broker by the accident of failure.”

===Chinese===
“来挑一只保险柜？”吉尔伯特讥讽地猜道。

“他是来劫夺我们劳动果实的。”沃利斯说。

他说这话时面带微笑，因为他颇有幽默感。

“某位怀着良心、或者说怀有正直感、因而不肯正式落草为窃的人物，正从事着一桩迷人的营生——劫窃贼的贼。换句话说，大约两万镑的硬通货现钞，已经从我的保险柜里不翼而飞。”

“是‘借’走的吧，我毫不怀疑。”吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿说着，靠回椅背，双手插进口袋，脸上是一副冷硬的神色。

“你说‘借’是什么意思？”沃利斯愕然问。

“被一个急等钱用的人‘借’走了；一个比许多专门研究证券交易的人更精通此道的人；一个凭这份本事尽可放手豪赌、几乎稳赚不赔，却仍然唯恐万一失手殃及某个倒霉经纪商的人。”

===Original===
He leant towards Wallis, his elbow upon the desk, his face half averted from the other. He had heard the outer door close with a bang, and knew they were alone now, and that Wallis had designed it so.

“I wanted money badly,” he said. “I could have stolen it easily. I intended stealing it. I watched you for a month. I have watched criminals for years. I know as many tricks of the trade as you. Remember that I was in the Foreign Office, in that department which had to do mainly with foreign crooks, and that I was virtually a police officer, though I had none of the authority.”

“I know all about that,” said Wallis.

He was curious, he desired information for his own immediate use, he desired it, too, that his sum of knowledge concerning humanity should be enlarged.

“I am a thief—in effect. The reason does not concern you.”

“Had the ‘Melody in F’ anything to do with it?” asked the other dryly.

===Chinese===
他朝沃利斯俯过身去，手肘支在桌上，脸偏向一边。他听见外间的门砰地关上，知道此刻他们已孑然独处，而这正是沃利斯精心安排的。

“我当时急需钱，”他说，“我本可以轻而易举地偷到手。我也确实存心要偷。我监视了你整整一个月。我监视罪犯已有多年。这一行的门道，我懂得不比你少。别忘了，我曾在外交部供职，就在那个主要对付外国骗子的部门，我实际上就是个警官，只不过没有执法的权限罢了。”

“这些我全知道。”沃利斯说。

他好奇，他想获取情报供自己当下之用，也想借此扩充自己对人类的认知总量。

“实际上，我就是个贼。原因与你无关。”

“这事和《F大调旋律》有关系吗？”对方冷冷地问。

===Original===
Gilbert Standerton sprang to his feet.

“What do you mean?” he asked.

“Just what I say,” said the other, watching him keenly. “I understand that you had an eccentric desire to hear that melody played. Why? I must confess I am curious.”

“Reserve your curiosity for something which concerns you,” said the other roughly. “Where did you learn?” he added the question, and Wallis laughed.

“We have sources of information—” he began magniloquently.

===Chinese===
吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿霍地站起身来。

“你这话什么意思？”他问。

“就是我说的话，”对方目光锐利地注视着他说，“我了解到，你曾有过一个古怪的愿望，想听那支旋律被奏响。为什么？我必须承认，我很好奇。”

“把你的好奇心留给与你有关的事吧。”这一位粗声粗气地说。“你是从哪儿听来的？”他又追了一句，沃利斯笑了。

“我们有消息来源——”他夸夸其谈地开了腔。

===Original===
“Oh, yes,” Gilbert nodded, “of course, your friend Smith lodges with the Wings. I had forgotten that.”

“My friend Smith—you refer to my chauffeur, I suppose?”

“I refer to your confederate, the fourth member of your gang, the man who never appears in any of your exploits, and who in various guises is laying down the foundation for robberies of the future. Oh, I know all about this place,” he said. He waved his hand around the shop. “I know this scheme of a Safe Agency; it is ingenious, but it is not original. I think it was done some years ago in Italy. You tout safes round to country mansions, offer them at ridiculous prices, and the rest is simple. You have the keys, and at any moment you can go into a house into which such a safe has been sold with the certain knowledge that all the valuables and all the portable property will be assembled in the one spot and accessible to you.”

Wallis nodded.

“Quite right, friend,” he said. “I need no information concerning myself. Will you kindly explain exactly what part you are taking? Are you under the impression that you are numbered amongst the honest?”

“I do not,” said the other shortly. “The morality of my actions has nothing whatever to do with the matter. I have no illusion.”

“You are a fortunate man,” said George Wallis approvingly. “But will you please tell me what part you are playing, and how you justify your action in removing from time to time large sums of money from our possession to some secret depository of your own?”

===Chinese===
“哦，是啊，”吉尔伯特点头，“当然，你的朋友史密斯（Smith）寄宿在温家嘛。我都忘了这一茬了。”

“我的朋友史密斯——你是指我的司机吧？”

“我是指你的同伙，你们那个团伙的第四名成员，那个从不在你们任何一桩买卖里露面、却以各种伪装为将来的劫案打地基的家伙。哦，这地方的情形我一清二楚，”他说着，朝店堂四面挥了挥手。“保险柜商行这套把戏我明白；构思巧妙，却非首创。我记得几年前意大利就有人这么干过。你们把保险柜四处推销到乡间宅邸，以低得可笑的价钱出手，余下的事就简单了。钥匙在你们手里，任何时候你们都可以登门造访任何一户买下这种保险柜的人家，而且笃定地知道，全部值钱物件、全部便于搬动的财物都汇集在一处，任凭取用。”

沃利斯点了点头。

“完全正确，朋友，”他说，“我自己的事无须你来介绍。可否请你确切说明，你扮演的是什么角色？你莫非还自以为名列清白人之中？”

“我不这么想。”这一位简短地答道。“我这些行为的道德问题，与眼下这事毫无干系。我不抱幻想。”

“你是个有福之人，”乔治·沃利斯（George Wallis）赞许地说，“不过可否请你告诉我，你扮演的究竟是什么角色？你时不时地从我们手里把大笔大笔的钱挪到你自己某个秘密的藏所去，这行为你又如何自圆其说？”

===Original===
“I do not justify it,” said Gilbert.

He got up and paced the little office, the other watching him narrowly.

“I tell you I know that I am in intent a thief, but I am working to a plan.”

He turned to the other.

“Do you know that there is not a robbery you have committed of which I do not know the absolute effect? There is not a piece of jewellery you have taken of which I do not know the owner and the exact value? Yes,” he nodded, “I am aware that you have not ‘fenced’—that is the term, isn’t it?—a single article, and that in your safe place you have them all stored. I hope by good fortune not only to compensate you for what I have taken from you, but to return every penny that you have stolen.”

===Chinese===
“我不为自己辩护。”吉尔伯特说。

他站起身，在这间小办公室里踱来踱去，对方目光灼灼地盯着他。

“我告诉你，我知道自己就意图而言是个贼，但我是在按一个计划行事。”

他转向对方。

“你可知道，你们干下的每一桩劫案，其确切的后果我没有一样不知道？你们拿走的每一件首饰，其物主与确切价值，我没有一样不清楚？是的，”他点头，“我知道你们连一件货都还没‘销’过——那一行的术语是这么说的吧？——它们全都存放在你那安全的处所里。我希望凭着好运气，不但补偿我从你那里拿走的东西，还要把你们偷来的每一文钱都物归原主。”

===Original===
Wallis started.

“What do you mean?” he asked.

“To its rightful owner,” continued Gilbert calmly. “I have striven to be in a position to say to you: ‘Here is a necklace belonging to Lady Dynshird, it is worth four thousand pounds, I will give you a fair price for it, let us say a thousand—it is rather more than you could sell it for—and we will restore it to its owner.’ I want to say to you: ‘I have taken ten thousand sovereigns in bullion and in French banknotes from your store, here is that amount for yourself, here is a similar amount which is to be restored to the people from whom it was taken.’ I have kept a careful count of every penny you have taken since I joined your gang as an unofficial member.”

===Chinese===
沃利斯一怔。

“你这是什么意思？”他问。

“归还给合法的物主。”吉尔伯特平静地继续道，“我一直努力使自己有资格对你说：‘这里有一条丁舍德夫人（Lady Dynshird）的项链，值四千镑，我出个公道价买下，就算一千镑吧——比你销赃能得的还略多些——然后咱们把它归还原主。’我想对你说：‘我从你的库房里拿走了折合一万金镑的金条和法国钞票，这里是给你本人的那一份，这里是同等数目的另一份，要归还给被劫的人们。’自打我以编外成员的身份加入你们团伙以来，你们拿走的每一文钱，我都一笔一笔仔细记着账。”

===Original===
He smiled grimly.

“My dear Quixote,” drawled George Wallis protestingly, “you are setting yourself an impossible task.”

Gilbert Standerton shook his head.

“Indeed I am not,” he said. “I have made much more money on the Stock Exchange than ever I thought I should possess in my life.”

“Will you tell me this?” asked the other. “What is the explanation of this sudden desire of yours for wealth—for sudden desire I gather it was?”

“That I cannot explain,” said Gilbert, and his tone was uncompromising.

===Chinese===
他冷峻地笑了笑。

“我亲爱的堂吉诃德，”乔治·沃利斯拖长了声音抗议道，“你这是给自己派了一桩不可能完成的任务。”

吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿摇了摇头。

“绝非如此，”他说，“我在证券交易所赚到的钱，远超我此生自以为能拥有的数目。”

“能告诉我一件事吗？”对方问，“你这突如其来的发财欲望，究竟作何解释——据我猜，它是突如其来的吧？”

“这个我无法解释。”吉尔伯特说，语气毫不妥协。

===Original===
There was a little pause, then George Wallis rose.

“I think we had better understand one another now,” he said. “You have taken from us nearly twenty thousand pounds—twenty thousand pounds of our money swept out of existence.”

Gilbert shook his head.

“No, there is not a penny of it gone. I tell you I used it as a reserve in case I should want it. As a matter of fact, I shall not want it now,” he smiled, “I could restore it to you tonight.”

“You will greatly oblige me if you do,” said the other.

Gilbert looked at him.

“I rather like you, Wallis,” he said, “there is something admirable about you, rascal that you are.”

“Rascals as we are,” corrected Wallis. “You who have no illusions do not create one now.”

“I suppose that is so,” said the other moodily.

===Chinese===
略停了片刻，乔治·沃利斯站起身来。

“我想，咱们最好现在就把话说明白，”他说，“你从我们这里拿走了将近两万镑——两万镑我们的钱，就这么从人世间一扫而空。”

吉尔伯特摇头。

“不，一文都没有花掉。告诉你，我是把它当作备用金，以备不时之需。事实上，我现在已经用不着它了，”他微笑道，“我今晚就能还给你。”

“你要是这么办，就帮了我大忙了。”对方说。

吉尔伯特看着他。

“我挺喜欢你的，沃利斯，”他说，“你身上有种可敬的东西，虽说你是个恶棍。”

“是‘咱们都是恶棍’。”沃利斯更正道，“你这个不抱幻想的人，眼下也别制造出一个来。”

“大概是这样。”这一位郁郁地说。

===Original===
“How is this going to end?” asked Wallis. “Where do we share out, and are you prepared to carry on this high-soul arrangement as long as my firm is in existence?”

Standerton shook his head.

“No,” he said, “your business ends tonight.”

“My business?” asked the startled Wallis.

“Your business,” said the other. “You have made enough money to retire on. Get out. I have made sufficient money to take over all your stock at valuation”—he smiled again—“and to restore every penny that has been stolen by you. I was coming to you in a few days with that proposition.”

“And so we end tonight, do we?” mused Wallis. “My dear good man,” he said cheerfully, “tonight—why I am going out after the most wonderful coup of all! You would laugh if you knew who was my intended victim.”

“I am not easily amused in these days,” said Gilbert. “Who is it?”

“I will tell you another time,” said Wallis.

===Chinese===
“这事将如何收场？”沃利斯问，“咱们在哪儿分账？莫非只要我的商号还开着，你打算把这套高尚的安排永远进行下去？”

斯坦德顿摇头。

“不，”他说，“你的买卖今晚就收摊。”

“我的买卖？”沃利斯愕然问。

“你的买卖，”这一位说，“你赚的钱已足够退休。收山吧。我挣的钱也足够按估价盘下你的全部存货——他又笑了——并且把你偷来的每一文钱都归还回去。过几天我正要带着这项提议来见你。”

“这么说，咱们今晚就散伙，是吗？”沃利斯若有所思，“我亲爱的好人，”他兴致勃勃地说，“今晚——嘿，我还要出去干一票全部买卖里最妙的大活呢！你要是知道我看中的目标是谁，准会笑出来。”

“我近来可不容易发笑了，”吉尔伯特说，“是谁？”

“改天再告诉你。”沃利斯说。

===Original===
He walked to the office door, his hands in his pockets. He stood for a moment admiring a huge safe and whistling a little tune.

“Don’t you think it an excellent idea of mine,” he asked with the casual air of the suburban householder showing off a new cucumber frame, “this safe?”

“I think it is most excellent.”

“Business is good,” said Wallis regretfully. “It is a pity to give it up after we have taken so much trouble. You see, we may not sell half a dozen safes a year to the right kind of people, but if we only sell one—why we pay expenses! It is so simple,” he said.

“By the way, have you missed a necklace of sorts which has been restored to the police? Do not apologise!”

He raised his hand.

===Chinese===
他双手插兜，走向办公室的门。他伫立片刻，欣赏着一只巨大的保险柜，嘴里吹着一支小曲。

“你不觉得这是我的一个绝妙主意吗，”他以郊区住户炫耀新黄瓜棚架的那种随意口吻问道，“这保险柜？”

“我觉得妙极了。”

“生意不错，”沃利斯惋惜地说，“费了这么大劲再撒手，实在可惜。你是知道的，一年里咱们未必能卖给对路的人家半打保险柜，可只要卖出一台——嗐，开销就全有了着落！就这么简单。”他说。

“对了，你可曾发觉少了某条项链，就是那条已经交还警方的物件？不必道歉！”

他抬起了手。

===Original===
“I understand this is a family matter. I am sorry to have caused you any inconvenience.”

His ironical politeness amused the other.

“It was not a question of family,” he said. “I had no idea as to its ownership, only some person had been very careless—I found the necklace outside the safe. Some property had evidently been hidden in a hurry, and had fallen down.”

“I am greatly obliged to you,” said Wallis. “You removed what might possibly have been a great temptation for the honest Mr. Timmings.”

He took a key from his pocket, switched round the combination lock, and opened the safe. There was nothing in the first view to suggest that it was the storehouse of the most notorious thief in London. Every article therein had been most carefully wrapped and packed. He closed the door again.

“That is only half the treasure,” he said.

“Only half—what do you mean?”

===Chinese===
“我明白，这是桩家事。很抱歉给你添了不便。”

他那含讥带讽的客套倒把对方逗乐了。

“这不是什么家事，”他说，“我根本不知道那项链是谁的，只是有人太粗心了——我在保险柜外面发现了它。显见得是有人匆忙藏匿财物时，它跌落了下来。”

“真是多谢你了，”沃利斯说，“你替老实的蒂明斯先生（Mr. Timmings）除去了一个没准是莫大的诱惑。”

他从口袋里掏出钥匙，拨转密码锁，打开了保险柜。乍看之下，绝想不到这里是伦敦最赫赫有名的窃贼的藏宝库。里面的每一件物品都经过最细心的包裹与码放。他又把箱门关上了。

“这只是宝藏的一半。”他说。

“只是一半——什么意思？”

===Original===
Gilbert was genuinely surprised, and a little mocking smile played about the mouth of the other.

“I thought that would upset you,” he said. “That is only half. I will show you something. Since you know so much, why shouldn’t you know all?”

He walked back into the office. A door led into another room. He unlocked this, and opening it passed through, Gilbert following. Inside was a small room lit by a skylight. The centre of the room was occupied by what appeared to be a large cage. It was in reality a steel grill, which is sometimes sold by French firms to surround a safe.

“A pretty cage,” said Mr. Wallis admiringly.

He unlocked the tiny steel gate and stepped through, and Gilbert stepped after him.

“How did you get it in?” asked Gilbert curiously.

===Chinese===
吉尔伯特当真吃了一惊，对方嘴角则浮起一丝嘲弄的笑意。

“我料到这会让你心绪不宁，”他说，“那只是半份。我带你看样东西。既然你知道得这么多，何不让你知道个全貌？”

他走回办公室。一道门通向另一个房间。他开了锁，推门而入，吉尔伯特跟在后面。里面是一间靠天窗采光的小屋。屋子正中盘踞着一个状似巨笼的东西。那其实是一具钢栅笼，法国商行有时出售这种围护保险柜的设施。

“好一只漂亮的笼子。”沃利斯先生赞赏地说。

他打开那扇小小的钢门走了进去，吉尔伯特也跟了进去。

“你们是怎么把它弄进来的？”吉尔伯特好奇地问。

===Original===
“It was brought in in pieces, and has just been set up in order to show a customer. It is very easily taken apart, and two or three mechanics can clear it away in a day.”

“Is this your other department?” asked Gilbert dryly.

“In a sense it is,” said Wallis, “and I will show you why. If you go to the corner and pull down the first bar you will see something which perhaps you have never seen before.”

Gilbert was halfway to the corner, when the transparency of the trick struck him. He turned quickly, but a revolver was pointed straight at his heart.

“Put up your hands, Mr. Gilbert Standerton,” said George. “You may be perfectly bona fide in your intentions to share out, but I was thinking that I would rather finish tonight’s job before I relinquish business. You see, it will be poetic justice. Your uncle—”

“My uncle!” said Gilbert.

“Your uncle,” bowed the other, “an admirable but testy old gentleman, who in one of our best safes has deposited nearly a quarter of a million pounds’ worth of jewellery, the famous Standerton diamonds, which I suppose you will one day inherit.”

“Is it not poetic justice,” he asked as he backed his way out, still covering his prisoner with his revolver, “to rob *you* just a little? Possibly,” he went on, with grim humour, “I also may have a conscience, and may attempt to restore to you the property which tonight I shall steal.”

===Chinese===
“它是拆散了运进来的，刚装好，准备给一位顾客开开眼。拆起来也容易，两三个技工一天工夫就能搬走。”

“这就是你们的另一个部门？”吉尔伯特干巴巴地问。

“某种意义上是，”沃利斯说，“我这就让你看看为什么。你走到角落，把第一根栏杆拉下来，就会见到一样你也许从未见过的东西。”

吉尔伯特刚走到半路，猛然识破了这个把戏的浅露。他迅速转身，可一支左轮手枪已正对着他的心口。

“举起手来，吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿先生，”乔治说，“你打算分账散伙，也许完全出于真心，可我在想，还是先把今晚这票活儿干完，再歇业不迟。你瞧，这称得上诗意的正义。你叔父——”

“我叔父！”吉尔伯特说。

“正是你叔父，”对方欠了欠身，“一位可敬却脾气火爆的老先生，他把价值近二十五万镑的首饰存进了我们最好的一只保险柜，就是那批著名的斯坦德顿钻石，我想有朝一日会由你来继承。”

“抢一抢‘你’，只抢那么一小笔，难道不正是诗意的正义吗？”他一边倒退着出去，一边仍用手枪指着他这位俘虏，问道。“况且，”他带着冷酷的幽默继续说，“我说不定也有一副良心，说不定也会设法把今晚我要偷的财物归还给你呢。”

===Original===
He clanged the gate to, doubly locked it, and walked to the door which led to the office.

“You will stay here for forty-eight hours,” he said, “at the end of which time you will be released—on my word. It may be inconvenient for you, but there are many inconvenient happenings in this life which we must endure. I commend you to Providence.”

He went out, and was gone for a quarter of an hour.

Gilbert thought he had left, but he returned carrying a large jug of coffee, two brand new quart vacuum flasks, and two packages of what proved to be sandwiches.

“I cannot starve you,” he said. “You had better keep your coffee hot. You will have a long wait, and as you may be cold I have brought this.”

He went back to the office and carried out two heavy overcoats and thrust them through the bars.

“That is very decent of you,” said Gilbert.

“Not at all,” said the polite Mr. Wallis.

===Chinese===
他哐当一声拉上栅门，上了双重锁，走向通往办公室的那道门。

“你要在这里待上四十八小时，”他说，“时满之后自会放你——我说话算话。这或许让你不便，但人这一生必须忍受的不便之事多着呢。我把你托付给上天照拂了。”

他出去了，一去就是一刻钟。

吉尔伯特以为他已经走了，谁知他捧着一大壶咖啡、两只崭新的一夸脱保温瓶和两包后来证明是三明治的东西回来了。

“我不能饿着你，”他说，“咖啡你最好装进瓶里保温。你要等上很久，怕你受冷，我把它带来了。”

他回办公室取了两件厚大衣，从栅栏间塞了进去。

“你真是厚道。”吉尔伯特说。

“哪里的话。”彬彬有礼的沃利斯先生说。

===Original===
Gilbert was unarmed, and had he possessed a weapon it would have been of no service to him.

The pistol had not left Wallis’s hand, and even as he handed the food through the grill the butt of the automatic Colt was still gripped in his palm.

“I wish you a very good evening. If you would like to send a perfectly noncommittal note to your wife, saying that you were too busy to come back, I should be delighted to see it delivered.”

He passed through the bars a sheet of paper and a stylograph pen. It was a thoughtful thing to do, and Gilbert appreciated it.

This man, scoundrel as he was, had nicer instincts than many who had never brought themselves within the pale of the law.

He scribbled a note excusing himself, folded up the sheet and placed it in the envelope, sealing it down before he realised that his captor would want to read it.

“I am very sorry,” he said, “but you can open it, the gum is still wet.”

Wallis shook his head.

“If you will tell me that there is nothing more than I asked you to write, or than I expected you to write, that is sufficient,” he said.

So he left Gilbert alone and with much to think about.

===Chinese===
吉尔伯特身上没有武器，纵然有，对他也毫无用处。

手枪始终没有离开沃利斯的手，就连隔着栅栏递食物的时候，那支柯尔特自动手枪的枪柄依然攥在他的掌心。

“祝您晚安。你若想给尊夫人捎一张绝不走漏半点口风的便条，就说你公务太忙赶不回来，我很乐意叫人送去。”

他从栅栏间递进一张纸和一支针管笔。这想得很是周到，吉尔伯特领这份情。

这个人虽是恶棍，其天性之细腻，却胜过许多从未落入法网的人。

他草草写了张致歉的便条，把信纸折好装进信封，封上了口，这才意识到擒获他的人是要过目的。

“真抱歉，”他说，“你可以拆开，胶水还没干。”

沃利斯摇头。

“只要你告诉我，里面除了我请你写的、或我料到你会写的之外，再无别的内容，那就足够了。”他说。

于是他撇下吉尔伯特独自一人，留下他满怀心事。

## VI The Safe Agency / VI 保险柜商行

===Original===
The City of London is filled, as all the world knows, with flourishing and well-established businesses.

It abounds in concerns which proclaim, either with dignity or flamboyantly, the fact that this shop stood where it did a hundred years ago, and is still being carried on by the legitimate descendants of its founders.

There are companies and syndicates and trading associations, housed in ornate and elaborate buildings, suites of offices, which come into existence in the spring and fade away to nothingness in the winter, leaving a residue of unpaid petty accounts, and a landlord who has only this satisfaction—that he was paid his rent in advance.

The tragedies of the City of London lay in a large sense round the ugly and unpretentious buildings of the Stock Exchange, and may be found in the seedy sprinkling of people who perambulate the streets round and round that grimy building like so many disembodied spirits.

But the tragic gambler is not peculiar to the metropolis, and the fortunes made and lost in a day or in an hour has its counterpart in every city in the world where stock transactions are conducted.

===Chinese===
众所周知，伦敦金融城（the City）里满是兴隆发达、根基稳固的商行。

这里不乏这样的字号：或庄重或张扬地宣称，本店百年之前便立足于斯，至今仍由创始人的嫡系子孙经营不辍。

也有各色公司、辛迪加和贸易联合会，栖身于华丽考究的大楼与成套的写字楼里，春天开张，冬天便烟消云散，只留下一堆无人偿付的琐碎账目，还有一位仅有一丝安慰的房东——他的租金毕竟是预付的。

从广义上说，伦敦金融城的悲剧大多盘桓在证券交易所那座丑陋朴素的建筑四周，藏身于那一小撮寒酸的人群里——他们像无数离魂的幽灵，绕着那座灰扑扑的大楼一圈又一圈地踱步。

不过，悲剧性的赌徒并非大都会独有的现象；一日之间乃至一小时之间暴起暴落的财富，在世界上每一座进行股票交易的城市里都有它的对应物。

===Original===
The picturesque sorrows of the city are represented in the popular mind with the human wreckage which strews the Embankment after dark, or goes shuffling along the edges of the pavement with downcast eyes seeking for discarded cigar ends. That is sorrowful enough, though the unhappy objects of our pity are considerably more satisfied with their lot than most people would imagine.

The real tragedy and sorrow is to be found in the hundred and one little businesses which come into existence joyfully, and swallow up the savings of years of some two or three optimistic individuals. The flourishing note heads which are issued from brand new offices redolent of paint and fresh varnish, the virgin books imposingly displayed upon new shelves, the mass of correspondence which goes daily forth, the booklets and the leaflets, the explanatory tables and all the paraphernalia of the inexperienced advertiser, and the trickle of replies which come back—they are all part of the sad game.

Some firms endeavour to establish themselves with violence, with a flourish of their largest trumpets. Some drift into business noiselessly, and in some mysterious way make good. Generally, one may suppose, they came with the invaluable asset of a “connection,” shifting up from the suburbs to a more impressive address.

===Chinese===
在世人心目中，这座城市如画的愁苦，体现在天黑后横陈于河滨大道（the Embankment）的落魄残躯，或垂着眼睛、拖着脚步蹭过人行道边缘、捡寻弃置雪茄烟头的那些人身上。这已经够悲惨了，尽管我们怜悯的那些不幸者，对自己的命运其实比大多数人想象的要满足得多。

真正的悲剧与哀愁，在于那形形色色欢天喜地开张的小生意，一口吞掉两三个乐观者多年的积蓄。从油漆清漆气味刺鼻的崭新办公室里发出的精美笺头，威风凛凛陈列在新书架上的空白账簿，每日大批寄出的函件，小册子和宣传单，说明表格，以及没有经验的广告主的全套行头，还有涓滴般流回来的回音——它们都是这场悲伤游戏的组成部分。

有的商号力求以雷霆万钧之势立足，把最大的号角吹得震天响。有的悄无声息地漂进商界，又以某种神秘的方式站稳了脚跟。一般可以推测，后者带来了一份无价的资产——“人脉”，从郊区搬到了更显赫的地址。

===Original===
One of the businesses which came into existence in London in the year 1924 was a firm which was defined in the telephone book and in the directory as “The St. Bride’s Safe Company.” It dealt in new and secondhand safes, strong rooms and all the cunning machinery of protection.

In its one showroom were displayed safes of every make, new and old, gratings, burglar alarms, cash boxes, big and small, and the examples of all that iron and steel could do to resist the attention of the professional burglar.

The principal of the business was apparently a Midland gentleman, who engaged a staff, including a manager and a salesman, by advertisement, interviewed the newly-engaged employees in the Midlands, and placed at the disposal of the manager, who came armed with unimpeachable testimonials, a sum of money sufficient to stock the store and carry on the business.

He found more supplies from time to time in addition to the floating stock-in-trade, and though orders came very infrequently, the proprietor of the concern cheerfully continued to pay the large rent and the fairly generous salaries of the staff.

===Chinese===
1924年在伦敦开张的商号中，有一家在电话簿和工商名录里登记为“圣布莱德保险柜公司”（The St. Bride’s Safe Company）。它经营新旧保险柜、保险库房，以及一切精巧的防护器械。

它仅有的一间陈列室里摆满了各牌各代的保险柜，新旧俱全，还有铁栅、防盗警铃、大大小小的现金柜——铁与钢所能做到的一切抵抗职业窃贼光顾的例证，这里应有尽有。

这门生意的主事人显然是位中部地区（Midlands）的先生，他登报聘了一批员工，包括一名经理和一名推销员，在中部地区面试了新雇的职员，并把一笔足以备货开业、维持营运的钱，交到那位携着无可指摘的推荐信前来赴任的经理手里。

除了流转的存货，他还时不时地补充货源；尽管订单来得极为稀少，这位东家仍旧高高兴兴地照付高昂的租金和相当优厚的员工薪水。

===Original===
The proprietor would occasionally visit the store, generally late at night, because, as he explained, his business in Birmingham required his constant attention.

The new stock would be inspected; there would be a stocktaking of keys—these were usually kept in the private safe of the firm—and the proprietor would invariably express his satisfaction with the progress of the business.

The manager himself never quite understood how his chief could make this office pay, but he evidently did a big trade in the provinces, because he was able to keep a large motor lorry and a driver, who from time to time appeared at the Bride Street store, brought a safe which would be unloaded, or carried away some purchased article to its new owners.

The manager, a Mr. Timmings, and a respectable member of Balham society, could only imagine that the provincial branch of the business was fairly extensive. Sometimes the motor lorry would come with every evidence of having travelled for many miles, and it would seem that the business flourished, at any rate, at the Birmingham end.

===Chinese===
东家偶尔会来铺子里视察，一般都在深夜，因为照他自己的解释，他在伯明翰（Birmingham）的生意需要他时时照看。

新到的货要验看一遍；钥匙要清点一次——它们通常存放在商行的私人保险柜里——而东家总会对生意的进展表示满意。

经理本人始终不太明白，他的东家靠这家铺子如何生利，但他在外省显然做着大买卖，因为他养得起一辆大型货车和一个司机，那司机时不时出现在布莱德街的铺子，卸下一只保险柜，或是把什么卖定的物件运送给新主顾。

经理蒂明斯先生（Mr. Timmings）是巴勒姆（Balham）地方上一位体面的居民，只能想象这门生意的外省分号颇具规模。有时那辆货车开来，满身风尘，一望便知跑了许多英里，于是这生意——至少在伯明翰那一头——似乎颇为兴旺。

===Original===
It was the day following the remarkable occurrence which is chronicled in the previous chapter that Gilbert Standerton decided amongst other things to purchase a safe.

He needed one for his home, and there were reasons which need not be particularised why such an article of furniture was necessary. He had never felt the need of a safe before. When he did, he wanted to get one right away. It was unfortunate, or fortunate as the case may be, that this resolve did not come to him until an hour when most dealers in these unusual commodities were closed. It was after six when he arrived in the City.

Mr. Timmings had gone away early that night, but he had left a most excellent deputy.

The proprietor had come to London a little earlier that evening, and through the glass street doors Gilbert saw him and stared.

The door was locked when he tried it, and with a cheery smile the new proprietor came forward himself and unbolted it.

===Chinese===
正是在前一章所载那桩惊人事变的次日，吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿（Gilbert Standerton）决定办几件事，其中一件便是买一只保险柜。

他家里需要一只，至于为何非有这件家具不可，个中缘由不必细表。他从前从不觉得需要保险柜。一旦觉得需要，就想立刻弄到手。可惜——或者说幸好，视情形而定——他下这个决心的时候太晚，经营这种稀罕商品的店家大多已经打烊。他赶到金融城时已过六点。

那晚蒂明斯先生早早走了，但他留下了一位再称职不过的代理。

东家那天傍晚比他早些来到伦敦，隔着临街的玻璃门，吉尔伯特看见了他，不由得怔住了。

他推了推门，门锁着；满面春风的新东家亲自上前拔了门闩。

===Original===
“We are closed,” he said, “and I am afraid my manager has gone home. Can I do anything for you?”

Gilbert looked at him.

“Yes,” he said slowly, “I want to buy a safe.”

“Then possibly I can help you,” said the gentleman good-naturedly. “Won’t you come in?”

Gilbert entered, and the door was bolted behind him.

“What kind of safe do you want?” asked the man.

“I want a small one,” said the other. “I would like a secondhand Chubb if you have one.”

===Chinese===
“我们打烊了，”他说，“恐怕我的经理已经回家了。我能为您效劳吗？”

吉尔伯特看着他。

“是的，”他缓缓地说，“我想买一只保险柜。”

“那我也许能帮上忙，”这位先生和颜悦色地说，“请进来好吗？”

吉尔伯特走进去，门在他身后重新上了闩。

“您要什么样的保险柜？”那人问。

“我要一只小的，”这一位说，“要是有的话，我想要一只二手的查布（Chubb）。”

===Original===
“I think I have got the very thing. I suppose you want it for your office?”

Gilbert shook his head.

“No, I want it for my house,” he said shortly, “and I would like it delivered almost at once.”

He made an inspection of the various receptacles for valuables, and finally made a choice.

He was on his way out, when he saw the great safe which stood at the end of the store.

It was rather out of the ordinary, being about eight feet in height and about that width. It looked for all the world like a great steel wardrobe. Three sets of locks guarded the interior, and there was in addition a small combination lock.

===Chinese===
“我想我这里正好有一只。您是要放在办公室用吧？”

吉尔伯特摇头。

“不，是家里用，”他简短地说，“而且我希望能马上送到。”

他把各式藏放贵重物品的容器检视了一遍，终于选定了一只。

他正要出去，忽然看见铺子尽头立着的那只巨型保险柜。

它颇不寻常，约莫八英尺高、八英尺宽，看上去活脱脱像一口巨大的钢制衣柜。三套锁具环卫着内部，另外还有一个小小的转字密码锁。

===Original===
“That is a very handsome safe,” said Gilbert.

“Isn’t it?” said the other carelessly.

“What is the value of that?”

“It is sold,” said the proprietor a little brusquely.

“Sold? I should like to see the interior,” said Gilbert.

The man smiled at him and stroked his upturned moustache thoughtfully.

“I am sorry I can’t oblige you,” he said. “The fact is, the new proprietor took the keys when he completed the purchase.”

“That is very unfortunate,” said Gilbert, “for this is one of the most interesting safes I have ever seen.”

“It is quite usual,” said the other briefly. He tapped the sides with his knuckles in a reflective mood. “It is rather an expensive piece of property.”

===Chinese===
“那是一只非常气派的保险柜。”吉尔伯特说。

“可不是吗？”对方漫不经心地说。

“那一只什么价？”

“已经卖掉了。”东家有点粗声粗气地说。

“卖掉了？我想看看内部。”吉尔伯特说。

那人朝他笑了笑，若有所思地捻着上翘的胡髭。

“很抱歉不能从命，”他说，“实情是，新买主付清货款时把钥匙一并带走了。”

“那太遗憾了，”吉尔伯特说，“因为这是我所见过的最有趣的保险柜之一。”

“这很常见。”对方简短地说。他用指节若有所思地叩着箱壁。“这件家什可相当值钱。”

===Original===
“It looks as if you had it here permanently.”

“It does, doesn’t it?” said the other absently. “I had to make it comfortable.”

He smiled, then he led the way to another part of the store.

Gilbert would have paid by cheque, but something prevented him. He searched his pockets, and found the fifteen pounds which had been asked for the safe.

With a pleasant good night he was ushered out of the shop, and the door was closed behind him.

“Where have I seen your face before?” said the proprietor to himself.

Though he was a very clever man in more ways than one, it is a curious fact that he never placed his customer until many months afterwards.

===Chinese===
“瞧这架势，倒像是要在这里安家落户了。”

“可不是吗？”对方心不在焉地说，“我总得让它住得舒舒服服的。”

他一笑，随即领路走向铺子的另一头。

吉尔伯特本想用支票付款，可有什么东西让他止住了。他摸遍口袋，凑齐了那只保险柜索价的十五镑。

在愉快的道别声中，他被送出店门，门在他身后关上了。

“我从前在哪儿见过你这张脸来着？”东家自言自语道。

此人虽然在方方面面都精明过人，但说来奇怪，直到好几个月之后，他才认出这位顾客是谁。

## XIV The Standerton Diamonds / XIV 斯坦德顿钻石

===Original===
Edith Standerton made a quick preparation for her journey. She would take her maid into Huntingdon, and go without Gilbert. It was embarrassing that she must go alone, but she had set herself a task, and if she could help her husband by appearing at the dinner of his irritable relative she would do so.

She had her evening things packed, and caught the four o’clock train for the town of Tinley.

The old man did her the exceptional honour of meeting her at the station.

“Where is Gilbert?” he asked when they had mutually introduced themselves.

“He has been called out of town unexpectedly,” she said. “He will be awfully upset when he knows.”

===Chinese===
伊迪丝·斯坦德顿（Edith Standerton）为出行匆匆做了准备。她打算带上女仆去亨廷登（Huntingdon），不带吉尔伯特。必须独自前往，这未免尴尬，可她已给自己定下了任务，倘若出席他那位坏脾气亲戚的晚宴能帮上丈夫的忙，她便愿意去。

她收拾好晚装行李，赶上了开往廷利镇（Tinley）的四点钟火车。

老人破例亲临车站迎接她，给了她莫大的礼遇。

“吉尔伯特呢？”两人相互见礼之后，他问道。

“他临时有事被叫出城了，”她说，“他知道了准会懊恼极了。”

===Original===
“I think not,” said the old General grimly. “It takes a great deal to upset Gilbert—certainly more than an opportunity of being reconciled to a grouchy old man. As a matter of fact,” he went on, “there is no reconciliation necessary; but I always look upon anybody whom I have to cut out of my will as one who regards me as a mortal enemy.”

“Please never put me in your will.”

She smiled.

“I’m not so sure about that,” said he, and added gallantly, “though I think Nature has sufficiently endowed you to enable you to dispense with such mundane gifts as money!”

She made a little face at that.

He was delighted with her, and found her a charming companion. Edith Standerton exerted herself to please him. She had a style of treating people older than herself in such a way as to suggest that she was as young as they. I do not know any other phrase which would more exactly convey my meaning than that. She had a charm which appealed to this wayward old man.

===Chinese===
“我看未必，”老将军冷冷地说，“要搅得吉尔伯特心神不安可不容易——总得比一个同乖戾老头和解的机会更了不得的事才行。其实呢，”他接着说，“根本用不着和解；不过，凡是不得不从我遗嘱里除名的人，我一律当作把我视作死敌的家伙。”

“请您千万别把我写进遗嘱。”

她微笑道。

“这我可不答应，”他说，又献殷勤般补了一句，“不过我想，造化赋予你的禀赋已经足够，大可不必再要金钱这种俗物！”

她闻言做了个小鬼脸。

他对她喜欢得不得了，觉得她是个迷人的伴儿。伊迪丝·斯坦德顿使出浑身解数讨他欢心。她有一套对待长辈的法子，能让人觉得她与人家一样年轻。我想不出还有哪句话能比这句更确切地表达我的意思。她有一种魅力，正投这位任性老头的脾气。

===Original===
Edith did not know the cause of the change in her husband’s fortunes. She knew very little, indeed, of his affairs; enough she knew that for some reason or other he had been disinherited through no fault of his own. She did not even know that it was the result of a caprice of this old man.

“You must come again and bring Gilbert,” said the General, before they dispersed to dress for dinner. “I shall be delighted to put you both up.”

Fortunately she was saved the embarrassment of an answer, for the General jumped up suddenly.

“I know what you’d like to see,” he said, “you’d like to see the Standerton diamonds, and so you shall!”

She had no desire to see the Standerton diamonds, had, indeed, no knowledge that such an heirloom existed; but he was delighted at the prospect of showing her, and she, being a woman, was not averse to a view of these precious jewels, even though she were not destined to wear them.

===Chinese===
伊迪丝并不知道丈夫境遇变迁的缘由。说实在的，她对丈夫的事务知之甚少；只知道他不知为了什么缘故被剥夺了继承权，而这并非他自身的过错。她甚至不知道，这正是这位老人一时心血来潮的结果。

“你们务必再来，把吉尔伯特也带来，”将军在大家散开去换晚装前说道，“我很乐意留你们两位小住。”

幸好她得以免去作答的尴尬，因为将军猛地跳了起来。

“我知道你想看什么，”他说，“你想看看斯坦德顿钻石，这就让你看个够！”

她本无意看什么斯坦德顿钻石，实际上压根不知道还有这样一件传家宝；可他一想到能让她开开眼便喜不自胜，而她身为女子，也不排斥一睹这些珍宝的风采，纵然命里注定轮不到她来佩戴。

===Original===
He led the way up to the library, and Jack Frankfort followed.

“There they are,” said the old man proudly, and pointed to a big safe in the corner, a large and ornate safe.

“That is something new,” he said proudly. “I bought it from a man who wanted sixty guineas for it—an infernal, swindling, travelling rascal! I got it for thirty. What do you think of that for a safe?”

“I think it’s very pretty,” said Jack. He could think of nothing more fitting.

The old man glared at him.

“Pretty!” he growled. “What do you think I want with ‘pretty’ things in my library?”

He took a bunch of keys from his pocket and opened the door of the safe, pulled open a drawer, and took out a large morocco case.

“There they are!” he said with pride, and indeed he might well be proud of such a beautiful collection.

===Chinese===
他在前头引路上书房去，杰克·弗兰克福特（Jack Frankfort）跟在后头。

“喏，就在那儿，”老人自豪地说，手指着墙角那只大保险柜——一只气派华美的大保险柜。

“这可是件新玩意儿，”他得意地说，“我是从一个家伙手里买的，他开口要六十几尼——一个伤天害理、招摇撞骗的跑江湖骗子！我三十几尼就拿下了。你说这保险柜买得怎么样？”

“我看很漂亮。”杰克说。他想不出更得体的话了。

老人瞪了他一眼。

“漂亮！”他咆哮道，“你以为我要‘漂亮’玩意儿放书房里做什么？”

他从口袋里掏出一串钥匙，打开箱门，拉开一只抽屉，取出一个摩洛哥山羊皮大匣子。

“都在这儿呢！”他骄傲地说，这样一套美妙的收藏，确实足以令他自豪。

===Original===
With all a girl’s love for pretty things Edith handled the gorgeous jewels eagerly. The setting was old-fashioned, but it was the old fashion which was at that moment being copied. The stones sparkled and glittered as though every facet carried a tiny electric lamp to send forth the green, blue and roseate gleam of its fire.

Even Jack Frankfort, no great lover of jewellery, was fascinated by the sight.

“Why, sir,” he said, “there are nearly a hundred thousand pounds’ worth of gems there.”

“More,” said the old man. “I’ve a pearl necklace here,” and he pulled out another drawer, “look at it. There is nearly two hundred thousand pounds’ worth of jewellery in that safe.”

“In a thirty-guinea safe,” said Jack unwisely.

The old man turned on him.

“In a sixty-guinea safe,” he corrected violently. “Didn’t I tell you I beat the devil down? I beg your pardon, my dear.” He chuckled at the thought, replaced the jewels, and locked the safe again. “Sixty guineas he wanted. Came here with all his fine City of London manner, frock-coat, top-hat, and patent boots, my dear. The way these people get up is scandalous. He might have been a gentleman by the airs he gave himself.”

===Chinese===
伊迪丝怀着姑娘家对漂亮东西的天然喜爱，急切地摩挲着这些华光璀璨的珠宝。镶嵌的式样虽是老派，可眼下时髦人物摹仿的恰恰正是这种老派。宝石熠熠生辉，仿佛每一个切面上都装着一盏小小的电灯，吐放出绿、蓝与玫红的光焰。

就连杰克·弗兰克福特这个对首饰谈不上痴迷的人，也被眼前的景象迷住了。

“哎呀，爵士，”他说，“那里头差不多有十万镑的宝石呢。”

“不止，”老人说，“我这儿还有一串珍珠项链，”他拉开另一只抽屉，“你瞧。那只保险柜里装着将近二十万镑的首饰。”

“装在一只三十几尼的保险柜里。”杰克多嘴道。

老人霍地转向他。

“是装在一只六十几尼的保险柜里，”他声色俱厉地更正，“我不是告诉过你，我把那魔鬼的价杀下来了？请原谅，亲爱的。”他想到这里不由轻笑，把珠宝放回原处，重新锁上保险柜。“他要六十几尼。亲爱的，他到这儿来的时候，摆足了伦敦金融城那套漂亮做派，长礼服、大礼帽、漆皮靴。这路人马的行头简直不成体统。瞧他那副派头，倒真像位上等人。”

===Original===
Jack looked at the safe. He had some ideas of commercial values.

“I can’t understand how he sold it,” he said. “This safe is worth two hundred pounds.”

“What?”

The old General turned on his lawyer in astonishment.

Jack nodded.

“I have one at my office, now that I come to think of it,” he said. “It cost two hundred and twenty pounds, and it is the same make.”

===Chinese===
杰克端详着那只保险柜。他对商品行情还有些概念。

“我不明白他怎么会卖掉它，”他说，“这保险柜值二百镑。”

“什么？”

老将军愕然转向他的律师。

杰克点头。

“说来也巧，我办公室里就有一只，”他说，“花了我二百二十镑，还是同一个牌子。”

===Original===
“He only asked me sixty guineas.”

“That’s strange. Do you mind opening it again? I’d like to see the bolts.”

The General, nothing loath, turned the key and pulled open the huge door. Jack looked at the square, steel bolts—they were absolutely new.

“I can’t understand how he offered it for sixty. You certainly had a bargain for thirty, sir,” he said.

“I think I have,” said the General complacently. “By the way, I am expecting a man to dinner tonight,” he went on, as he led the way back to the drawing-room, “a doctor man from Yorkshire—Barclay-Seymour. Do you know him?”

Jack did not know him, but the girl broke in—

“Oh, yes, he is quite an old friend of mine.”

===Chinese===
“他只要了我六十几尼。”

“那就怪了。能再开一次吗？我想看看门闩。”

将军正乐得再炫耀一回，转动了钥匙，拉开那扇厚重的柜门。杰克察看着方形钢闩——它们崭新锃亮。

“我不明白他怎么会只开六十。您三十几尼买下，实在捡了大便宜，爵士。”他说。

“我也这么想。”将军怡然自得地说。“对了，今晚我有位客人来吃饭，”他一面引路回客厅，一面继续道，“约克郡（Yorkshire）来的一位医生——巴克利-西摩（Barclay-Seymour）。你认识吗？”

杰克不认识，那姑娘却插嘴道——

“哦，认识，他是我的老朋友了。”

===Original===
“He’s rather a fool,” said the General, adopting his simple method of classification.

Edith smiled.

“You told me yesterday that there were only two classes of people, General—rogues and fools. I am wondering,” she said demurely, “in which class you place me.”

The old man wrinkled his brows. He looked at the beautiful young face in his high good humour.

“I must make a new class for you,” he said. “No, you shall be in a class by yourself. But since most women are fools—”

“Oh, come!” she protested, laughingly.

“They are,” he averred. “Look at me. If women weren’t fools shouldn’t I have had a wife? If any brilliant, ingenious lady, possessed of the necessary determination had pursued me and had cultivated me, I should not be a bachelor, leaving my money to people who don’t care two—pins,” he hastily substituted a milder phrase for the one he had intended, “whether I’m alive or dead. Does your husband know the Doctor, by the way?”

===Chinese===
“他是个不折不扣的傻子，”将军说，祭出了他那套简单的分类法。

伊迪丝微微一笑。

“您昨天告诉我，世上的人只分两类，将军——恶棍和傻子。我正琢磨着，”她端庄地说，“您把我归进哪一类。”

老人皱起眉头。他兴致高昂地端详着那张年轻美丽的脸。

“我得为你单设一类，”他说，“不，你自成一类。不过，既然大多数女人都是傻子——”

“哎，瞧您说的！”她笑着抗议。

“本来就是，”他一口咬定，“拿我自己来说。女人若不是傻子，我岂会没有太太？倘若哪位聪明伶俐、又具备必要决断的女士当年追过我、栽培过我，我就不至于打光棍，把钱财留给那些对我的死活连两个——铜板都不肯费心的家伙，”他慌忙用温和些的说法替换了原本想用的字眼，“对了，你丈夫认识那位博士吗？”

===Original===
The girl shook her head.

“I don’t think so,” she said. “They nearly met one night at dinner, but Gilbert had an engagement.”

“But Gilbert knows him,” insisted the old man. “I’ve often talked to him about Barclay-Seymour, who, by the way, is perhaps not such a fool as most doctors. I used to be rather more enthusiastic about him than I have been lately,” he admitted, “and I’m afraid I used to ram old Barclay-Seymour down poor Gilbert’s throat more than his ability or genius justified me doing. Has he never spoken about him?”

The girl shook her head.

“Ungrateful devil!” growled the old General inconsequently.

===Chinese===
姑娘摇头。

“我想不认识，”她说，“有天晚上吃晚饭差点遇上，可吉尔伯特另有约在先。”

“可吉尔伯特认得他，”老人固执地说，“我常跟他念叨巴克利-西摩，说起来，这人也许不像大多数医生那样傻。我从前对他比近来热心得多，”他承认道，“恐怕我把老巴克利-西摩往可怜的吉尔伯特喉咙里灌得太狠了，超出了他的才干天资所能挣来的分量。他从来没提起过这人？”

姑娘摇头。

“忘恩负义的东西！”老将军没头没脑地咆哮道。

===Original===
One of his many footmen came into the drawing-room at that moment with a telegram on a salver.

“Hey hey?” demanded Sir John, fixing his glasses on the tip of his nose and scowling up at his servant. “What’s this?”

“A telegram, Sir John,” replied the footman.

“I can see it’s a telegram, you ass! When did it come?”

“A few minutes ago, sir.”

“Who brought it?”

“A telegraph boy, Sir John,” said the imperturbable servitor.

“Why didn’t you say so at first?” snapped Sir John Standerton in a tone of relief. And Edith had all she could do to prevent herself from bursting into a fit of laughter at the little scene.

===Chinese===
正说着，他那许多男仆中的一个托着银盘走进客厅，盘上放着一封电报。

“咦，咦？”约翰·斯坦德顿爵士（Sir John Standerton）喝问道，把眼镜架到鼻尖上，怒冲冲地仰视着仆人，“这是什么东西？”

“一封电报，约翰爵士。”男仆答道。

“我看得出是电报，你这蠢驴！什么时候到的？”

“几分钟以前，老爷。”

“谁送来的？”

“一位电报投递员，约翰爵士。”那位不动声色的仆人答道。

“那你起先怎么不说？”约翰·斯坦德顿爵士如释重负地厉声说道。这一幕小小的场面，害得伊迪丝费了好大的劲才没让自己笑出声来。

===Original===
The old man opened the telegram, spread it out, read it slowly and frowned. He read it again.

“Now, what on earth does that mean?” he asked, and handed the telegram to the girl.

She read—

> “Take the Standerton jewels out of your safe and deposit them without fail in your bank tonight. If it is too late to send them to your bank place them under an armed guard.”

It was signed “Gilbert Standerton.”

===Chinese===
老人拆开电报，展平了，缓缓读过，皱起眉头。他又读了一遍。

“这到底葫芦里卖的什么药？”他问着，把电报递给姑娘。

她读道——

> “将斯坦德顿珠宝从您的保险柜中取出，务必于今夜存入您的银行。如来不及送往银行，请置于武装卫兵看守之下。”

落款是“吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿”。

## XV The Tale the Doctor Told / XV 医生讲的故事

===Original===
The General read the telegram again. He was, despite his erratic temperament, a shrewd and intelligent man.

“What does that mean?” he asked quietly for him. “Where is Gilbert? And where does he wire from?”

He picked up the telegram and inspected it. It was handed in at the General Post Office at London at 6:35 p.m.

The General’s hour for dining was consonant with his breakfast hour, and it was a quarter after nine when the dinner gong brought Edith Standerton down from her room.

She was worried; she could not understand the reference to the jewels. What had made Gilbert send this message? Had she known more of the circumstances of what had happened on the previous afternoon she would have wondered rather how he was able to send the message.

===Chinese===
将军又把电报读了一遍。他尽管性情古怪，却是个精明睿智的人。

“这是什么意思？”他以他而言算得上平静地问道，“吉尔伯特在哪儿？电报又是从哪儿发的？”

他拿起电报仔细察看。电报是下午6点35分在伦敦邮政总局（General Post Office）交发的。

将军用晚餐的时辰与他用早餐的时辰一脉相承，晚餐锣响、把伊迪丝·斯坦德顿从房里唤下来时，已是九点一刻。

她忧心忡忡；她不明白为什么要提珠宝。吉尔伯特为什么发这封电报？倘若她对前一天下午发生的事知道得再多一些，她倒要纳闷他是怎么把电报发出来的。

===Original===
The General took the warning seriously, but not so seriously that he was prepared to remove his jewellery to any other receptacle. Indeed, the purchase of the safe had been made necessary by the fact that beyond the butler’s strong room, which was strong only in an etymological sense, there was no security for property of any value.

He had made an inspection of the jewels in the safe and had relocked the door, leaving a servant in the library, with strict instructions not to come out until he was instructed to leave by his master.

Edith came down to find that another guest had arrived, a guest who greeted her with a cheery and familiar smile.

“How do you do, Doctor?” she said. “It is not so long since I met you at mother’s. You remember me?”

“I remember you perfectly,” said Dr. Barclay-Seymour.

===Chinese===
将军把这道警告当了真，却还没有当真到准备把珠宝挪进别的容器里去的地步。说实在的，当初之所以非买那只保险柜不可，是因为除了管家那间保险库——它只有词源学意义上算得上“保险”——任何值钱财物都无处安放。

他把保险柜里的珠宝检视了一遍，重新锁好箱门，又留了一个仆人在书房里，严令他不奉主人之命不得离开。

伊迪丝下楼时发现又到了一位客人，那客人满面熟稔亲切的笑意向她问好。

“您好，医生。”她说，“我在母亲家见过您，还没过去多久呢。您记得我吗？”

“记得清清楚楚。”巴克利-西摩医生（Dr. Barclay-Seymour）说。

===Original===
He was a tall, thin man with a straggling iron-grey beard and a high forehead.

A little absent in his manner, he conveyed the impression, never a very flattering one, that he had matters more weighty to think about than the conversation which was being addressed to him. He was, perhaps, the most noteworthy of the provincial doctors. He came out of his shell sufficiently to recognise her and to remember her mother. Mrs. Cathcart had been a great friend of Barclay’s. They had grown up together.

“Your mother is a very wonderful woman,” said Dr. Barclay-Seymour as he took the girl in to dinner, “a remarkable woman.”

Edith was seized with an almost overwhelming temptation to ask why. It would have been unpardonable of her had she done so, but never did a word so tremble upon a human being’s lips as that upon hers.

===Chinese===
他是个瘦高个儿，蓄着一部稀疏的铁灰色胡须，天庭饱满。

他举止有几分心不在焉，给人一种——从来算不得恭维——手里的事比眼下的谈话更值得思量的印象。他或许是外省医生中最负盛名的一位。他从自己的壳里探出头来的程度，恰好够认出她、记起她的母亲。卡思卡特夫人（Mrs. Cathcart）是巴克利的老朋友。两人是从小一起长大的。

“你母亲是位非常了不起的女士，”巴克利-西摩医生领着姑娘入席时说，“一位不同寻常的女士。”

伊迪丝心头涌起一股几乎按捺不住的冲动，想问一句为什么。她倘若真问出口便不可原谅了，但从来没有一个字像那个字一样，在凡人唇边颤动得如此厉害。

===Original===
They ate through dinner, which was made a little uncomfortable by the fact that General Sir John Standerton was unquestionably nervous. Twice during the course of the meal he sent out one of the three footmen who waited at table to visit what he termed the outpost. Nothing untoward had happened on either occasion.

“I do not know what to do about this jewellery. I hope that Gilbert is not playing the fool,” he said.

He turned to Edith with a genial scowl.

“Has he developed any kittenish ways of late?”

===Chinese===
一顿晚饭吃得有些不自在，因为约翰·斯坦德顿爵士将军分明心神不定。席间他两次派出三名侍餐男仆中的一个，去查看他所谓的“前哨”。两次都没有异常。

“这批珠宝怎么办，我没了主意。但愿吉尔伯特不是在胡闹。”他说。

他朝伊迪丝转过一张和颜悦色的怒脸。

“他近来有没有沾染什么猫儿似的娇态？”

===Original===
She smiled.

“There is no word which less describes Gilbert than kittenish,” she said.

“Is it not remarkable that he sent that message?” the General went on testily. “I hardly know what to do. I could get a constable up, but the police here are the most awful and appalling idiots. I have a great mind to have my bed put in the library and sleep there myself.”

He brightened up at the thought.

He had reached the stage in life when sleeping in any other room than that to which he was accustomed represented a form of heroism. After the dinner was through they made their way to the drawing-room.

===Chinese===
她微微一笑。

“要说有哪个词最不像吉尔伯特，那就是‘娇气’了。”她说。

“他发来这么一封电报，岂不蹊跷？”将军烦躁地继续道，“我简直不知如何是好。我可以叫个警察来守着，可这里的警察是天底下最吓人的傻瓜。我很想把我的床搬进书房，亲自睡在那儿。”

他想到这里，精神为之一振。

他这辈子已经到了这样的阶段：只要不在自己惯睡的房间里睡觉，便算得一种壮举。晚饭过后，众人移步客厅。

===Original===
The General was fidgety, and though Edith played and sang a little French love song with no evidence of agitation, she was as nervous as the General.

“I tell you what we will do,” said Sir John suddenly, “we will all adjourn to the library. It is a jolly nice room if you do not mind our smoking.”

It was an excellent suggestion, and one that she accepted with pleasure. She was the only lady of the party, and remarked on the fact as she went upstairs with Sir John.

He glanced hurriedly round.

“I always regard a doctor as a fit chaperone for any lady,” he said with a chuckle—it amused him.

Later he found the complement of the joke, and discoursed loudly upon old women of all professions, a discourse which was arrested by the arrival of the Doctor and Jack Frankfort.

===Chinese===
将军坐立不安；伊迪丝虽不动声色地弹唱了一支法国小情歌，心里却和将军一样紧张。

“我说，咱们这么办，”约翰爵士忽然开口，“干脆都挪到书房去。只要你们不嫌我们抽烟，那屋子妙得很。”

这提议再好不过，她欣然接受。她是席间唯一的女士，上楼时便同约翰爵士提了一句。

他慌忙四下里扫了一眼。

“我一向认为，医生是配得上任何女士的合格女伴。”他呵呵一笑——这话把他自己逗乐了。

随后他又想出了这话的下半截笑料，便高声大发议论，数说起各行各业的老太婆来；这场宏论因医生和杰克·弗兰克福特的到来而中断。

===Original===
The library was a big room, and it was chiefly remarkable for the fact that it contained no more evidence of Sir John’s literary taste than a number of volumes of the *Encyclopaedia Britannica* and a shelf full of *Ruff’s Guide to the Turf*. It was, however, a delightful room, panelled in old oak with mullioned windows standing in deep recesses. These, explained Sir John, opened out on to a terrace—an excellent reason for his apprehension.

“Pull the curtain, William,” said Sir John to the waiting footman, “and then you can clear out. Have the coffee brought in here.”

The man pulled the heavy velvet curtains across the big recesses, placed a chair for the girl, and retired.

“Excuse me,” said Sir John.

He went across to the safe and opened it again. He inspected the case. Nothing had been disturbed.

“Ah,” he breathed—It was a sigh of infinite relief.

===Chinese===
书房是间大屋子，最堪玩味的一点是，屋里能证明约翰爵士文学趣味的，不过几卷《不列颠百科全书》（*Encyclopaedia Britannica*）和一书架的《拉夫赛马指南》（*Ruff’s Guide to the Turf*）。不过这屋子着实宜人，镶着老橡木壁板，直棂窗嵌在深深的窗龛里。约翰爵士解释说，这些窗开向一座露台——这足以为他的忧惧提供绝好的理由。

“拉上帘子，威廉，”约翰爵士对侍立一旁的男仆说，“然后你就可以走了。叫人把咖啡送到这儿来。”

那人把厚重的丝绒帷幔拉过巨大的窗龛，为姑娘安好一张椅子，退了出去。

“失陪一下。”约翰爵士说。

他走到保险柜前，又把它打开。他察看那只皮匣。什么都没有动过。

“啊，”他长出一口气——那是一声如释重负的叹息。

===Original===
“This wire of Gilbert’s is getting on my nerves,” he excused himself irritably. “What the devil did he wire for? Is he the sort of man that sends telegrams to save himself the bother of licking down an envelope?”

Edith shook her head.

“I am as much in the dark as you,” she said, “but I assure you that Gilbert is not an alarmist.”

“How do you get on with him?” he asked her.

The girl flushed a little.

“I get on very well,” she said, and strove to turn the conversation. But it was a known fact that no human soul had ever turned Sir John from his set inquisational course.

===Chinese===
“吉尔伯特这封电报搅得我心神不宁，”他烦躁地自我辩解道，“他到底为什么发电报？他是那种为了省舔信封的麻烦就打电报的人吗？”

伊迪丝摇头。

“我和你一样蒙在鼓里，”她说，“可我向你保证，吉尔伯特不是个危言耸听的人。”

“你和他处得怎么样？”他问她。

姑娘的脸微微一红。

“处得很好。”她说，并努力岔开话题。但众所周知，天下还没有人能把约翰爵士从他那套审讯既定的路数上扳开过。

===Original===
“Happy, and that sort of thing?” he asked.

Edith nodded, keeping her eyes on the wall behind the General’s head.

“I suppose you love him—hey?”

Edith was embarrassed, and no less so were the two men; but Sir John was not alone in imagining that doctors have little sense of decency and lawyers no idea of propriety. They were saved further discussion by the arrival of the coffee, and the girl was thankful.

“I am going to keep you here until Gilbert comes up for you,” said the old man suddenly. “I suppose you know, but probably you do not, that you are the first of your sex that I have ever tolerated in my house.”

She laughed.

“It is a fact,” he said seriously. “You know I do not get on with women. They do not realise that though I am an irritable old chap there is really no harm in me, and I *am* an irritable old chap,” he confessed. “It is not that they are impertinent or rude, but it is their long-suffering meekness that I cannot stand. If a lady tells me to go to the devil I know where I am. I want the plain, blunt truth without gaff. I prefer my medicine without sugar.”

===Chinese===
“幸福吗，之类之类的？”他问。

伊迪丝点点头，眼睛盯着将军脑后的墙面。

“我想你爱他——嗯？”

伊迪丝很是窘迫，两个男人也不轻松；不过，以为医生不懂体面、律师不知分寸的，天下也不止约翰爵士一人。咖啡的送到使他们免去了进一步的盘问，姑娘暗自感激。

“我要把你留在这儿，直到吉尔伯特上来接你，”老人忽然说，“我想你是知道的，多半也不知道：你是头一个被我容留在这座房子里的女性。”

她笑出声来。

“这是实话，”他郑重地说，“你知道我跟女人处不来。她们不明白，我这老头子脾气虽坏，骨子里实在没有半点害处，我这脾气*就是*坏，”他招认道，“倒不是她们无礼或粗鲁，我受不了的是她们那副逆来顺受的温驯。哪位女士要是叫我见鬼去，我心里反倒有底。我要的是不加掩饰的直话直说。我吃药不要搁糖。”

===Original===
The Doctor laughed.

“You are different from most people, Sir John. I know men who are rather sensitive about the brutal truth.”

“More fools they,” said Sir John.

“I do not know,” said the Doctor reflectively. “I sympathise with a man who does not want the whole bitterness of fact hurled at his head in the shape of an honest half a brick, although there is an advantage in knowing the truth sometimes, it saves a lot of needless unhappiness,” he added a little sadly. He seemed to have aroused some unpleasant train of thought. “I will give you an extraordinary instance,” he went on in his usual deliberate manner.

“What’s that?” asked the General suddenly.

“I think it was a noise in the hall,” said Edith.

“I thought it was a window,” growled the General, rather ashamed that he should have been detected in his jump.

===Chinese===
医生笑了。

“您与大多数人不同，约翰爵士。我认识一些人，对赤裸裸的真相当真敏感。”

“那更是傻瓜。”约翰爵士说。

“那倒未必，”医生若有所思地说，“谁不愿意让事实的全部苦味化作半块诚实的砖头砸到自己脑门上，我倒体谅这样的人。虽说有时知道真相也有好处，能省去许多无谓的不快，”他略带伤感地补充道。他似乎勾起了一段不愉快的思绪。“我给你们讲一桩非同寻常的实例吧。”他一如往常，不慌不忙地继续道。

“什么实例？”将军猛然问。

“我想刚才门厅里有响动。”伊迪丝说。

“我还当是窗户。”将军咆哮道，因自己那一惊被人察觉而颇有几分赧然。

===Original===
“Go on with your story, Doctor.”

“A few months ago,” Dr. Seymour recalled, “a young man came to me. He was a gentleman, and evidently not a townsman of Leeds, at any rate I did not know him. I found afterwards that he had come from London to consult me. He had some little tooth trouble, a jagged molar, a very commonplace thing, and he had made a slight incision in the inside of his mouth. Apparently it worried him, the more so when he discovered that the tiny scratch would not heal. Like most of us, he had a terrible dread of cancer.” He lowered his voice as a doctor often will when he speaks of this most dreadful malady. “He did not want to go to his own doctor; as a matter of fact, I do not think he had one. He came to me, and I examined him. I had my doubt as to there being anything wrong with him, but I cut a minute section of the membrane for microscopic examination.”

===Chinese===
“接着讲您的故事吧，医生。”

“几个月前，”西摩医生回忆道，“有个年轻人来找我。他是位上等人，显然不是利兹（Leeds）本地人，至少我不认识他。我后来才知道，他是专程从伦敦来求诊的。他牙齿有点小毛病，一颗磨牙缺了个豁口，再平常不过的小事，却在他口腔内侧划了一道细细的口子。显然这让他忧心，尤其当他发现那处小小的划伤迟迟不愈时。和我们大多数人一样，他对癌症怕得要命。”说到这种最可怕的恶疾，医生压低了嗓音，医者谈及此病时往往如此。“他不愿去找自己的医生；说实在的，我看他压根没有固定的医生。他来找我，我给他作了检查。我怀疑他根本没病，但还是切下一小片黏膜，送去作显微镜检查。”

===Original===
The girl shivered.

“I am sorry,” said the Doctor hastily, “that is all there is in the story which is gruesome unless you think—However,” he went on, “I promised to send him the result of my examination, and I wanted his address to send it. This, however, he refused. He was very, very nervous. ‘I know I am a moral coward,’ he said, ‘but somehow I do not want to know just the bare truth in bald language; but if it is as I fear, I would like the news broken to me in the manner which is the least jarring to me.’ ”

“And what was that?” asked Sir John, interested in spite of himself.

The Doctor drew a long breath.

“It seems,” he said, “that he was something of a musician”—Edith sat upright, clasping her hands, her face set, her eyes fixed upon the Doctor—“he was something of a musician, that is to say, he was very keen on music, and the method he had of breaking the news to himself was unique, I have never heard anything quite like it before in my life. He gave me two cards and an addressed envelope, addressed to an old musician in London whom he patronised.”

===Chinese===
姑娘打了个寒噤。

“抱歉，”医生赶忙说，“故事里可怕的部分到此为止了，除非你们想到——不过，”他继续道，“我答应把检查结果寄给他，便向他要地址。这一点他却拒绝了。他非常非常紧张。‘我知道我是个精神上的懦夫，’他说，‘可不知怎么，我不愿听人用干巴巴的话把赤裸裸的真相告诉我；倘若事情真如我所怕的那样，我希望这消息以对我刺激最小的方式传达给我。’”

“那是哪种方式？”约翰爵士问，不由自主地来了兴致。

医生深深吸了一口气。

“看来，”他说，“他算得上半个音乐家”——伊迪丝坐直了身子，双手紧握，脸色僵住，眼睛直盯着医生——“他算得上半个音乐家，也就是说，他酷爱音乐，而他把消息传达给自己的法子独一无二，我这辈子从未听说过那样的做法。他交给我两张卡片和一个写好地址的信封，收信人是伦敦一位他素有资助的老乐师。”

===Original===
Edith saw the room go swaying round and round, but held herself in with an effort. Her face was white, her hands that held the chair were clenched so tightly that the bones shone white through them.

“They were addressed to an old friend of his, as I say, and they were identically worded with this exception. One of them said in effect you will go to such and such a place and you will play the ‘Melody in F,’ and the other gave the same instructions but varied to this extent, that he was to play the ‘Spring Song.’ Now here comes the tragedy.” He raised his finger. “He gave me the ‘Melody in F’ to signal to him the fact that he had cancer.”

There was a long silence, which only the quick breathing of the girl broke.

“And, and—?” whispered Edith.

“And”—the Doctor looked at her with his faraway eyes—“I sent the wrong card,” he said. “I sent it and destroyed the other before I remembered my error.”

===Chinese===
伊迪丝只觉得房间团团打转，但还是强撑住了。她脸色煞白，攥着椅子的双手紧得指节泛白。

“如我所说，卡片是寄给他一位老朋友的，两张措辞一模一样，只有一处不同。其中一张大意是说：你到某某地方去，演奏《F大调旋律》；另一张也是同样的指示，只是改了一处：改奏《春之歌》（‘Spring Song’）。而悲剧就在这里。”他竖起一根手指。“他指定用《F大调旋律》向他示意：他得了癌症。”

接下来是长久的沉默，只有姑娘急促的呼吸声将它划破。

“那么，那么——？”伊迪丝低声问。

“那么——”医生用他那双恍惚悠远的眼睛望着她——“我寄错了卡片，”他说，“等我记起自己的差错时，卡片已经寄出，另一张也销毁了。”

===Original===
“Then he has not cancer?” whispered the girl.

“No, and I do not know his address, and I cannot get at him,” said Barclay-Seymour. “It was tragic in many ways. I think he was just going to marry, for he said this much to me: ‘If this is true, and I am married, I will leave my wife a pauper,’ and he asked me a curious question,” added the Doctor. “He said, ‘Don’t you think that a man condemned to die is justified in taking any action, committing any crime, for the protection of the loved ones he leaves behind?’ ”

“I see,” said Edith.

Her voice was hollow and sounded remote to her.

“What is that?” said the General, and jumped up.

This time there was no doubt. Jack Frankfort sprang to the curtain that covered the recess and pulled it aside. There stood Gilbert Standerton, white as a ghost, his eyes staring into vacancy, the hand at his mouth shaking.

“The wrong card!” he said. “My God!”

===Chinese===
“那么他没有得癌症？”姑娘喃喃地问。

“没有，可我不知道他的地址，也没法找到他。”巴克利-西摩说，“这事在许多方面都是悲剧。我想他当时正要结婚，因为他对我说过这么一句话：‘倘若这是真的，而我结了婚，我会把妻子丢成一贫如洗。’他还问了我一个古怪的问题，”医生补充道，“他说：‘一个被判处死刑的人，为了保全他撇在身后的亲人，无论采取什么行动、犯下什么罪行，都是正当的——难道您不这样认为吗？’”

“我明白了。”伊迪丝说。

她的嗓音空洞，听在自己耳中遥远得很。

“那是什么声音？”将军说着，霍地跳了起来。

这一次错不了了。杰克·弗兰克福特扑向罩着窗龛的帷幔，一把拉开。吉尔伯特·斯坦德顿站在那里，面白如鬼，双眼直勾勾望着虚空，抵在嘴边的手抖个不停。

“寄错的卡片！”他说，“我的天哪！”

## VIII The Wife Who Did Not Love / VIII 不爱丈夫的妻子

===Original===
Mr. Warrell, of the firm of Warrell & Bird, prided himself upon being a man of the world, and was wont to admit, in a mild spirit of boastfulness, in which even middle-aged and respectable gentlemen occasionally indulge, that he had been in some very awkward situations. He had inferred that he had escaped from those situations with some credit to himself.

Every stockbroker doing a popular and extensive business is confronted sooner or later with the delicate task of explaining to a rash and hazardous speculator exactly how rashly and at what hazard he has invested his money.

Mr. Warrell had had occasion before to break, as gently as it was possible to break, unpleasant news of Mrs. Cathcart’s unsuccess. But never before had he been face to face with at situation so full of possibilities for disagreeable consequences as this which now awaited him.

The impassive Cole admitted him, and the face of Cole fell, for he knew the significance of these visits, having learnt in that mysterious way which servants have of discovering the inward secrets of their masters’ and mistresses’ bosoms, that the arrival of Mr. Warrell was usually followed by a period of retrenchment economy and reform.

“Madam will see you at once,” was the message he returned with.

A few minutes later Mrs. Cathcart sailed into the drawing-room, a little harder of face than usual, thought Mr. Warrell, and wondered why.

“Well, Warrell,” she said briskly, “what machination of the devil has brought you here? Sit down, won’t you?”

===Chinese===
沃雷尔-伯德律师事务所（Warrell & Bird）的沃雷尔先生（Mr. Warrell）自诩老于世故，惯于带着一种温和的自夸口气——连体面的中年绅士偶尔也难免沾染——承认自己经历过一些非常棘手的场面。他的言外之意是，他从那些场面里脱身时，还颇给自己挣了几分光彩。

凡是生意兴隆、门庭广开的股票经纪人，迟早都要面对一桩微妙的差事：向某个鲁莽轻率的投机客讲明白，他的钱投得何等鲁莽、冒了多大的风险。

沃雷尔先生以前就有过机会，尽可能委婉地向卡思卡特夫人（Mrs. Cathcart）转达她投资失利的噩耗。但像眼下这桩悬在他面前的、恶果难料的局面，他还从未亲身领教过。

不动声色的科尔（Cole）放他进门，科尔的脸色随即沉了下来，因为他知道这类来访的分量——仆人们自有那种神秘的法子，能探知主人太太们内心的秘密——沃雷尔先生的到来，通常紧跟着一段紧缩开支、厉行节俭的时期。

“夫人马上见您。”他回来传话道。

几分钟后，卡思卡特夫人仪态万方地走进客厅，脸色比平日更硬了几分，沃雷尔先生暗想，并纳闷缘故。

“哎呀，沃雷尔，”她干干脆脆地说，“是哪路鬼机灵把你引来的？坐吧？”

===Original===
He seated himself deliberately. He placed his hat upon the floor, and peeling his gloves, deposited them with unnecessary care in the satin-lined interior.

“What is it?” asked Mrs. Cathcart impatiently. “Are those Canadian Pacifics down again?”

“They are slightly up,” said Mr. Warrell, with a smile which was intended both to conciliate and to flatter. “I think your view on Canadian Pacifics is a very sound one.”

He knew that Mrs. Cathcart would ordinarily desire nothing better than a tribute to her judgment, but now she dismissed the compliment, realising that he had not come all the way from Throgmorton Street to say kindly things about her perspicacity.

“I will say all that is in my mind,” Mr. Warrell went on, choosing his words and endeavouring by the adoption of a pained smile to express in some tangible form his frankness. “You owe us some seven hundred pounds, Mrs. Cathcart.”

She nodded.

===Chinese===
他不慌不忙地落了座。他把帽子搁在地板上，褪下手套，又以那种多余的细致把它们收进帽子里衬着缎子的内膛。

“什么事？”卡思卡特夫人不耐烦地问，“加拿大太平洋铁路股（Canadian Pacifics）又跌了？”

“还微涨了一点，”沃雷尔先生说，脸上挂着既想安抚又想恭维的笑，“我认为您对加拿大太平洋铁路股的看法十分在理。”

他知道，换作平常，卡思卡特夫人最乐意不过听人称许她的判断力，可这回她把恭维撇在了一边，因为她明白，他绝非大老远从斯罗格莫顿街（Throgmorton Street）赶来，专为说几句夸她眼力的好话。

“我把心里的话全说出来吧，”沃雷尔先生继续道，字斟句酌，并力图摆出一副苦相，好把他的坦白变成看得见摸得着的东西，“您欠我们大约七百镑，卡思卡特夫人。”

她点点头。

===Original===
“You have ample security,” she said.

“That I realise,” he agreed, addressing the ceiling, “but the question is whether you are prepared to make good in actual cash the differences which are due to us.”

“There is no question at all about it,” she said brusquely, “so far as I am concerned, I cannot raise seven hundred shillings.”

“Suppose,” suggested Mr. Warrell, with his eyes still upraised, “suppose I could find somebody who would be willing to buy your necklace—I think that was the article you deposited with us—for a thousand pounds?”

“It is worth considerably more than that,” said Mrs. Cathcart sharply.

“Possibly,” said the other, “but I am anxious to keep things out of the paper.”

He had launched his bombshell.

===Chinese===
“你们手里有充足的抵押品嘛。”她说。

“这我明白，”他应道，眼睛望着天花板，“问题在于，您是否准备用实实在在的现金，把我们应收的差额补足。”

“这根本不成问题，”她粗声粗气地说，“就我这头而言，我连七百先令也拿不出来。”

“假如，”沃雷尔先生提议道，目光仍旧上望，“假如我能找到一位买主，愿意出一千镑买下您那条项链——我记得那是您押在我们这里的物件——您意下如何？”

“它的价值远不止这个数。”卡思卡特夫人尖刻地说。

“或许，”对方说，“可我急着别让这事见报。”

他把炸弹投下去了。

===Original===
“Exactly what do you mean?” she demanded, rising to her feet. She stood glowering down at him.

“Do not misunderstand me,” he said hastily. “I will explain in a sentence. Your diamond necklace has been stolen from my safe.”

“Stolen!”

She went white.

“Stolen,” said Mr. Warrell, “by a gang of burglars which has been engaged in its operations for the past twelve months in the City of London. You see, my dear Mrs. Cathcart,” he went on, “that it is a very embarrassing situation for both of us. I do not want my clients to know that I accept jewels from ladies as collateral security against differences, and you,” he was so rude as to point to emphasise his words, “do not, I imagine, desire your friends to know that it was necessary for you to deposit those jewels.” He shrugged his shoulders. “Of course, I could have reported the matter to the police, sent out a description of the necklace, and possibly recovered the loss from an insurance company, but that I do not wish to do.”
